# STATUS

Tags: IMPLEMENTED (verified) · IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED · PLANNED · EXPERIMENTAL · LATER

## Environment (recorded 2026-11-04, supplied by Sanjai)
- Mac: MacBook Air, model id `Mac16,12`, Apple M4, 16 GB RAM, **fanless**.
- macOS 27.2 (build 26B123). Xcode 27.2 (27B52). Swift 6.4.2 (swiftlang-6.4.2.1.9).
- Built-in camera as reported by AVFoundation: "MacBook Air Camera"; 640x480, 1280x720, 1920x1080 and others at 15–30 fps; `position` reports unspecified.
- `swift build` and plain `swift test` both work (verified). Tests use Swift Testing (`import Testing`).
- No code-signing identities installed (`security find-identity` → 0 valid), so dev bundles are ad-hoc signed.
- **This Mac sleeps aggressively** when idle on battery and on every lid close (observed even with `caffeinate -i` held, and with the lid closed at 3% battery during one long run). Long or timing-sensitive jobs (mutation checks, soaks, simulated end-to-end runs) can be frozen mid-run. Before trusting a slow or odd result check `pmset -g log`; see the stress-test finding below.
- Fallback if the active developer dir is ever the Command Line Tools: plain `swift test` fails to find `Testing`. Workaround (verified once on CLT): `F=/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks; swift test -Xswiftc -F -Xswiftc $F -Xlinker -F -Xlinker $F -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker $F`.

## Step 5 plan additions
- 30–60 minute **thermal soak** test (fanless MacBook Air): run tracking + rendering continuously and record CPU, frame rate, tracking latency and audio dropouts over time, plus whether the system throttles. (Only the short Step 2 session below has been measured so far.)
- **Investigate the camera-to-pose latency** (see Measured values): delivery read about 81 ms in the older Step 2 session but about 63 ms (61-63) in the three self-test runs (07:45, 08:12, 14:58), with Vision 18 ms and a total of 81 ms (79-81); the two sets differ for an unknown reason. Things to TEST, not promises or known causes: how much is exposure/ISP time before the sample timestamp is stamped vs. buffering after it; whether `alwaysDiscardsLateVideoFrames` and queue depth matter; camera format / frame duration (`activeFormat`, `activeVideoMinFrameDuration`) vs. the 640x480 session preset; whether macOS camera effects (Center Stage, Portrait, Studio Light, etc.) add delay; lower frame-rate/exposure behavior in dim light; and what the sample timestamp actually marks. Also decide whether landmark stability (second Vision request) is worth its cost.

- **Step 3a items to measure** (none measured yet): pose-to-ear latency of the pump design (publish → audible change); pump tick jitter under CPU load, heat and with the window hidden; CPU cost of HRTFHQ on the fanless Air; whether `AVAudioEnvironmentNode` ramps orientation changes internally (step response); whether per-sample/per-block interpolation inside a callback (Step 8) is audibly better than the pump.

- **PLANNED (added 2026-11-10): a read-only HUD line with the camera's reported frame-rate range and its active frame duration**, so a changed frame rate can be told apart from the camera being slowed on purpose. Names checked in the macOS 27.2 SDK header `AVCaptureDevice.h` on 2026-11-10 (nothing implemented): `AVCaptureDeviceFormat.videoSupportedFrameRateRanges` (an array of `AVFrameRateRange`, each with `minFrameRate`, `maxFrameRate`, `minFrameDuration`, `maxFrameDuration`) of the device's `activeFormat`, and `AVCaptureDevice.activeVideoMinFrameDuration` / `activeVideoMaxFrameDuration` (the header: the reciprocals of the active maximum and minimum frame rate; "clients can observe automatic changes to the receiver's activeVideoMinFrameDuration by key value observing"). The line must only READ them: the header says that setting them needs `lockForConfiguration:` and throws for unsupported values. **Not yet checked: the availability annotations of these properties on macOS (to be read when it is implemented).**

## Measured values (Step 2 manual session, user-reported from HUD screenshots)
Single session, normal light, MacBook Air M4 (Mac16,12), built-in camera, 640x480 preset, debug build, Vision revision 3. Not a benchmark: one sitting, a handful of readings, dim room not tested.
- Frame rate: **30.0 fps**. **[Update 2026-11-10: the rate has since been 16.6 and 25 fps in other sessions, cause unknown; see "Frame-rate finding (2026-11-10)".]**
- Capture→pose latency in THIS (older) Step 2 session: **about 94–97 ms total = delivery ~81–82 ms + Vision ~13–15 ms** (our filter/state machine cost is inside the total and not separated). **The ~81 ms (in this session) is delivery time** (from the frame's sample timestamp to our capture callback starting), not Vision. This is the dominant term; its cause is unknown (investigation is in the Step 5 plan). Note this excludes any delay before the sample timestamp, so true photon-to-pose latency is at least this much. **In the three later self-test runs (07:45, 08:12, 14:58) it read about 63 ms delivery (61-63) + 18 ms Vision = 81 ms total (79-81)** (see "Step 2d findings"); the two sets differ and the reason is unknown. Neither figure includes the One-Euro filter's own lag (see "Step 2g findings").
- `AVCaptureConnection`: `isVideoMirrored = false`, `videoRotationAngle = 0°` (so the mirroring compensation was not exercised on this Mac).
- Vision confidence: 0.87–0.89 with one face (so it is not a constant 1.0 here; still a weak signal).
- With all invert boxes unticked: raw yaw +25.3° → mapped +25.3°; raw pitch −11.7° → mapped +11.7°; raw roll −3.5° → mapped −3.5°. This only shows the mapping arithmetic (pitch negated); it does not say which physical direction the head was turned.

## Features
"IMPLEMENTED (verified)" means: built and covered by passing unit tests (`swift test`: 639 tests, 502 Core + 40 Tracking + 97 Engine, 2026-11-08 (the Step 6b-2 final run; it was 593 = 470 + 40 + 83 after Step 6b-1; **640 = 502 + 40 + 98 after the R26 follow-up of 2026-11-08**, one Engine test added; **653 = 511 + 40 + 102 after Step 6b-2b**, 9 Core and 4 Engine tests added; **692 = 544 + 40 + 108 after Step 3a-2**, 33 Core and 6 Engine tests added; **719 = 557 + 44 + 118 after Step 3b**, 13 Core, 4 Tracking and 10 Engine tests added); two tests are opt-in and skipped by default (the silent live-hardware smoke test and the Step 2g table printer); also clean under `swift test --sanitize=thread`; the counts are from the Step 6b-2 final run recorded below). "IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED" means it builds but has not been exercised on real hardware. **What real hardware has been used for (counts checked on 2026-11-08 against the report files; none of these runs is simulated):** **3 real camera self-test runs** (2026-11-05, committed reports of 07:45, 08:12 and 14:58, all `inputSource` camera): axes determined 0, 1 and 2 of 3, loss test fail, fail and pass (the jump check was not meaningful in the pass), green-light question never answered, so **none is a clean run**; **17 real tap-probe runs** (2026-11-07, Step 6a: 2 committed reports and 15 untracked ones, all `source` coreAudioTap); **6 real ring-probe runs** (2026-11-08, Step 6b-2, all committed, output gain 0). **[Update 2026-11-10: those counts are as of 2026-11-08. Since then 4 more real camera self-tests (2026-11-08 22:36 and 22:59, 2026-11-09 21:51 and 21:54) and 1 more real ring-probe run (2026-11-09 20:41, 60 s) were committed in `53d61a8`: 7 and 7 in all. In none of the seven self-tests was roll determined, so still none is a clean run; the 17 tap-probe runs are unchanged.]** **[Update 2026-11-10, later: 4 more real camera self-tests (09:25, 09:27, 10:06, 15:34), committed in `1215d83`: 11 real self-test runs in all, none clean, roll determined in none; see "Four more real camera self-test reports (2026-11-10)".]** **What has not happened:** nobody has listened through headphones under a test (the Step 3a listening test, docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP3A.md, has not been run; the Step 3a renderer and the ring-probe engine have been started on the live output device only silently, at volume / gain 0), and **the tracker is not connected to the renderer** (gate, updated 2026-11-09: Sanjai has confirmed yaw and pitch from the real run 215400, roll is NOT confirmed, and no run is clean; the only non-test code that feeds the pose mailbox is the scripted listening-test publisher). **[Update 2026-11-15: the "nobody has listened" statement above is out of date. Sanjai ran the Step 3a-2 blind listening test twice on 2026-11-10 (reports 160214 and 161833, committed in `c9fd7dc`): 7 and 6 of 20 trials got a definite answer, 12 and 14 were not answered, cause UNKNOWN; no conclusion about a left/right swap; see "Two real blind listening reports (2026-11-10)". The tracker is still not connected to the renderer.]** **[Update 2026-11-15 (Step 3b): the camera tracker IS now connected to the renderer in code, behind its own card (yaw and pitch only, roll forced to 0), IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED; it has not been run on the real camera through that card and nobody has heard it; see "Step 3b findings and verification record".]** **[Update 2026-11-15, 22:47 to 23:01 IST: Sanjai reported the two left/right checks (no swap for his one pair of headphones) and a first head-tracked listening (yaw direction correct by ear in one session; no jumps, jitter or clicks; lag "a little", not measured); see "First head-tracked listening". Tags not raised.]** All tuning defaults (One-Euro cutoffs/beta, hold 0.5 s, decay 1.5 s, blend 0.25 s, min confidence 0.5) are UNTUNED placeholders.

## Known limitations of Step 2 (stated, not measured)
- `VNObservation.confidence` is a weak signal (Apple says it can be a constant 1.0; measured 0.87–0.89 in one session). Our own quality signal now gates tracking, but its thresholds are untuned guesses.
- The watchdog handles a stalled or silent camera. It does not handle sleep/wake or device unplug explicitly (Step 6 covers sleep/wake for audio); a tick after wake may see a large frame age and simply treat it as loss.
- Sleep/wake and camera-unplug handling are not implemented.
- Only the largest face is used. No camera selection UI.
- The new Swift-native Vision API (`DetectFaceRectanglesRequest`) is macOS 15+, so the legacy `VN*` classes are used to keep the 14.0 minimum.
- The self-test cannot know which way you actually moved; a tilt of the wrong ear silently inverts the roll result.
- All self-test thresholds (movement, noise, steadiness, leakage, jump, stall) are untuned proposals; real data may show them to be too strict or too loose. INCONCLUSIVE is the designed outcome for weak data.
- The self-test stops the camera when it finishes (to verify the release), so verifying applied signs needs another Start camera.
- Reports from a throttled or overloaded run are flagged by a stall note, but nothing prevents you from running while the Mac is busy.

## ThreadSanitizer finding (2026-11-04, worth remembering)
`swift test --sanitize=thread` initially stayed silent on a deliberately injected race in the C target: a plain struct assignment compiles to `___tsan_memcpy`, which this TSan runtime did not race-check, even though the code was instrumented. Fixed by copying the six fields individually in `CPoseMailbox.c`. After that, both `scripts/tsan-mailbox.sh` and `swift test --sanitize=thread` flag a relaxed-ordering mutation (which produced zero torn reads, i.e. only TSan catches it) and a no-atomics mutation, and stay clean on the real code. Lesson: a green TSan run only counts if TSan has been shown to fail on a broken variant.
**Correction 2026-11-08:** this finding says the C target WAS instrumented. Statements elsewhere that `swift test --sanitize=thread` "does not instrument C targets" (ARCHITECTURE.md, the comments of `scripts/tsan-tapprobe.sh` and `scripts/tsan-systemaudioring.sh`, the Step 6b-1 record) misread it; see "Step 6b-2 real runs", Open items.

## PoseMailbox stress-test finding (2026-11-04): a clock-dependent TEST, not a mailbox bug
**Symptom.** During the first 18-mutation run, `concurrentStressHasNoTornReadsAndMonotonicSequences` failed together with a loss-analyzer test, although the injected bug (M13) touched unrelated code.

**Classification: timing-dependent test (a wall-clock hang guard). Not a mailbox bug, and not ordinary load flakiness.**

Evidence:
1. **Timeline** (`pmset -g log` plus source-file mtimes). The run started 17:50:36 with the lid closed on battery at 3%. The Mac slept at 17:50:57 and 18:01:15, so the script advanced only during brief maintenance wakes (36 minutes in total; the same run takes about 70 s on a settled machine). M13's run ended at 18:25:58, the same second as the wake that ended a 97 s sleep (18:24:21 → 18:25:58): its test process was frozen mid-run.
2. **Mechanism in the code.** The reader ended on `Date() < deadline`, a 60 s *wall-clock* deadline, and wall-clock time keeps running during sleep. The other assertions (`torn`, `backwards`, `newReads`) cannot depend on time, and the algorithm cannot produce torn or backwards reads (the reader only swaps in a slot published after its current one; the `acq_rel` exchanges give the happens-before).
3. **Reproduction.** Freezing the test process (SIGSTOP) for 65 s during a 100M-iteration run made exactly one assertion fail, `r.lastSeq == total` (the reader had seen 22.4M of 100M values), while torn/backwards stayed green: the signature of the original failure.
4. **No failures without a freeze.** 170 valid runs on the unmodified code, 0 failures: stress test alone ×60, whole suite ×30, under ThreadSanitizer ×40 (30 stress + 10 whole suite), with 20 busy processes on 10 cores ×40 (30 stress + 10 whole suite). (An earlier loaded set was INVALID and is not counted: the bundles were still TSan-instrumented from the previous phase and aborted with "Interceptors are not working". Redone correctly.)
5. **Re-run.** The same 18 mutations on a settled machine caught M13 only with its own test (no stress-test failure).

**Fix (test only).** The reader now stops when the writer has finished and it has done one more read. That read starts after the final publish, so a latest-value mailbox must return the final sequence: the verdict uses no clock. Same 65 s freeze and 100M iterations: old design **fails**, new design **passes** (79.5 s, 196M reads). The new test still catches real mailbox bugs: a no-atomics single slot fails `torn == 0`; a reader that never swaps in new data fails `lastSeq == total` in 28 ms (the old design would have spun toward its deadline). Repeats of the new test: 40 plain, 30 under load, 30 under TSan, 0 failures; a 10M-iteration TSan soak is clean.

**The same class of hazard, found by auditing the rest of the suite and fixed:**
- `SimulatedHeadPoseProvider.stop()` was a **real race in production code** (added in Step 2c): it cancelled the timer without waiting for a tick already running, so one more detection could arrive after `stop()` returned, contradicting the protocol's "blocks until released". It now drains the in-flight tick. Regression test `noDetectionIsDeliveredAfterStopReturns`: **6/6 runs fail without the drain, 0/6 with it**.
- `HeadTracker.stopWatchdog()` had the same hole for watchdog snapshots; fixed the same way. Its first regression test was too weak (0/6 failures without the fix, so it proved nothing); it now uses a deliberately slow snapshot handler to keep a tick in flight: **6/6 fail without the drain, 0/6 with it**.
- Fixed sleeps and exact count windows replaced by `waitUntil` (deadline on `SuspendingClock`, which does not advance during sleep) and by an upper bound derived from the measured elapsed awake time; a test that started a real watchdog and asserted an exact snapshot count now disables the watchdog.
Rule recorded in CLAUDE.md: tests never derive a verdict from a wall-clock deadline or fixed sleep.

## App Nap finding (2026-11-04): the app must hold an activity assertion while tracking
While checking the default report path with a simulated run, the loss test began to fail (INCOMPLETE or FAIL, state flapping). The report's own statistics showed why: every run delivered 26.4 fps on average with 125–131 ms frame gaps (≈790 of 900 frames) instead of 30.0 fps and ≈35 ms. Elimination: a bare 30 Hz `DispatchSourceTimer` in a command-line process in the same minute was perfectly regular (33.3 ms, max < 39 ms, at several QoS and leeway settings); the app used only 0–7% of one core and its thread stacks were parked, so nothing was busy or blocked; launching through LaunchServices did not help (foreground launch: 26.4 fps too). Confirmed with the documented `NSAppSleepDisabled=YES` switch on the dev bundle (since removed): **900 frames, 30.0 fps, 33/34/34 ms, PASS**, versus the same minute without it: **789 frames, 26.3 fps, 38/100/130 ms, INCOMPLETE**. So macOS was App-Napping the app because its window was hidden behind others, coalescing its timers.

**Fix.** `HeadTrackingModel` holds `ProcessInfo.beginActivity([.userInitiatedAllowingIdleSystemSleep, .latencyCritical])` from a successful start until stop (API checked in NSProcessInfo.h and by compiling; the "allowing idle system sleep" variant does not keep the Mac awake). With it, simulated runs under the same conditions: 3/3 PASS, 900 / 897 / 900 frames, 30.0 fps. The report now says so when frames stall (statistics note for gaps over 100 ms, loss-test note for gaps over 150 ms); run 2 of those 3 showed a single 134 ms stall and the note fired while the verdict stayed PASS. **Not verified:** whether real camera frame delivery (an AVFoundation callback, not a timer) is affected by App Nap; the assertion is held there too.

## Step 2c verification record (2026-11-04)
**Run, and passed**
- `swift build`; `swift test` 227 tests (187 Core + 40 Tracking); the same under `swift test --sanitize=thread` with no race reports; `scripts/tsan-mailbox.sh` (2M samples: torn=0, backwards=0, exit 0).
- Mutation check: 18 deliberately injected bugs in the new sign / report / loss / session / statistics / tracker logic, one at a time, each file restored and verified afterwards. The first run caught 18/18 but is not trustworthy (the Mac was asleep for most of its 36 minutes). Re-run on a settled machine and again on the final code: **18/18 caught** (71 s each time).
- Stop-contract regression tests (above): 6/6 vs 0/6.
- Simulated end-to-end runs of the real app (real timers, file writing, release step, quitting): documented conventions; `--sim-flip=yaw,roll` (detected: invert yaw and roll only); `--sim-mirrored --lighting=dim` (frames mirrored 100%, same invert answer as unmirrored); the default `docs/test-results/` path from the dev bundle (files written, parsed, numbers-only check passed, then deleted); 3/3 PASS at 30 fps after the App Nap fix.
- HUD layout reviewed by rendering it in-process during a simulated run (idle, mid-hold, cover, results).
- SDK checks (macOS 27.2): `NSSound` named sounds (loaded), `NSPasteboard` (round trip on a private pasteboard; the real clipboard was not touched), `NSWorkspace` file reveal, `AVCaptureSession` `isRunning` / `inputs` / `outputs` / `stopRunning` and its stop notification, `NSProcessInfo` activity options. `AVCaptureSession.isInterrupted` is unavailable on macOS (unused).

**Not run (needs Sanjai and the real camera)**
The self-test on a real camera (so no real sign result exists), the permission prompt inside the self-test flow, the audible cues, the Copy report / Apply / green-light buttons (never clicked), window-close and Cmd+Q camera release, and the behaviour of real frame delivery under App Nap.

## Step 3a findings (2026-11-05): audio-only renderer with a scripted pose, no camera
Scope: Sanjai relaxed the gate for AUDIO-ONLY work. The renderer is driven by a scripted synthetic head through the `PoseMailbox`; the camera link stays closed until the signs are confirmed from a real self-test report. Decision record and trade-offs: docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, "Real-time rule". Listening procedure: docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP3A.md.

**SDK facts (macOS 27.2 SDK headers, read on 2026-11-05)**
- `listenerVectorOrientation` default is forward (0,0,-1), up (0,1,0). The header never says which side +X is, so that was MEASURED (below).
- `listenerAngularOrientation.yaw` is documented clockwise-positive; confirmed by reading it back from the node after setting a vector orientation: our yaw +30 (turn LEFT) reads **-30**, our pitch +25 (up) reads +25, our roll +20 (right ear down) reads +20. Hence the conversion uses vectors. (At exactly +-90 degrees yaw the readback degenerates to `(0, 90, 90)`; at 89 degrees it reads -89. Irrelevant for vectors.)
- Getting or setting node properties from a non-realtime thread can require synchronization with the realtime thread, for "some of these properties" that the header does not name (`AVAudioEngine.h`); so the listener setters are treated as control-thread calls. The realtime-safe source-node API is macOS 27+ and `__SWIFT_UNAVAILABLE_MSG("Swift is not supported for use with audio realtime threads")`.
- `connect(_:to:format:)` and `AVAudioPlayerNode.play()` are deprecated from macOS 27 (replacements `connectNode(_:to:format:)` and `playAudio()` throw instead of raising Objective-C exceptions); used behind `#available(macOS 27.0, *)`.
- `HRTFHQ` is in `applicableRenderingAlgorithms` on this Mac (raw values `[0, 1, 2, 6, 3, 7]`). `listenerHeadTrackingEnabled` exists from macOS 15 and is set to false explicitly. Manual rendering must be enabled before touching the mixer/output nodes; the input node is never touched.

**Measurements (offline rendering on this Mac, pink-noise-burst test tone; they describe the engine's output channels, not your headphones)**
- Source at +X / -X / straight ahead: right-minus-left level **+7.7 / -7.7 / -0.3 dB**. (A white-noise probe gave about +-16 dB: the size depends on the signal; the sign is the finding.)
- Head turned (front source): yaw +30 / +60 / +90 degrees (left) → +5.9 / +7.5 / +7.7 dB; yaw -30 / -60 → -6.4 / -7.8 dB. A head turned 90 degrees left renders exactly like a source at +X with the head straight (+7.7 dB both).
- **Rotation invariance.** Listener rotated by R vs. source moved by R^-1, 8 poses (yaw, pitch, roll, combinations): relative RMS difference **0.000000** for all. Rotation applied the wrong way round: 0.48-1.40. Pose ignored: 0.30-1.39. The audio-side flip switches (yaw / pitch / roll flipped) differ from the truth by 1.18 / 0.49 / 0.48, so the same check would catch a sign error.
- Scripted yaw sweep, whole chain offline (script → mailbox → pump → conversion → engine): correlation between each 0.5 s window's expected azimuth and its measured level difference **0.9855**; levels saturate at about +-7.5 dB beyond roughly 50 degrees (monotonic, not linear). With the audio-side yaw flip on, the correlation is below -0.9.
- Live pump timing, one silent run on an idle Mac (battery power, 48 kHz output): 148 ticks, mean period 10.67 ms (nominal 10.67 ms), longest 10.71 ms. The same numbers from the in-app silent self-check: about 200 ticks, mean 10.67 ms, longest 10.7 ms. NOT measured: under CPU load, with heat, with the window hidden for long, over a long run.
- ARC probe (`swiftc -O -wmo`): no `swift_retain` / `swift_release` / `swift_beginAccess` / `swift_allocObject` in the hot path of class access to the mailbox in the three shapes tried; they appear at setup only. Not a guarantee (see the decision record).

**Notes on what the tests do and do not prove**
- Sign thresholds in the engine tests were first set from a white-noise probe (6 dB), then re-set to 4 dB after measuring the real tone (7.7 dB), about half the measurement. The sign is what the tests assert; the strict check is the invariance test.
- No test and no measurement says how the renderer SOUNDS (externalization, front/back, comfort, whether the left channel is your left ear). Only listening does.
- Two of my own tests had wrong expectations at first (a speaker-geometry sign I reversed, and an exact-fraction check that ignored `Quaternion.slerp`'s normalized-lerp fallback below about 3.6 degrees); both were investigated and the tests corrected, not the code.
- A first version of the stop-drain test deadlocked when the code under test was broken (a timed wait consumed the signal the next unbounded wait needed): found because the mutation harness stalled for ten minutes. All waits in tests are now bounded, and the harness has a per-run timeout.
- Two real self-test report files (not simulated: no `-SIMULATED` suffix) dated 2026-11-05 07:45 and 08:12 appeared in `docs/test-results/` while this step was being built. They were **not read, analysed or committed** here; the camera signs remain UNVERIFIED until Sanjai confirms them. Per the git workflow in CLAUDE.md, real reports are committed together with the step they belong to (these belong to Step 2c, whose commit is already made), so they are still untracked and waiting for Sanjai's instruction.

## Known limitations of Step 3a (stated, not measured)
- **Not sample-accurate.** The listener is updated once per nominal block by a timer on a non-real-time thread; true per-block interpolation inside a render callback needs a renderer we own (Step 8). Whether `AVAudioEnvironmentNode` smooths orientation changes internally is unknown. τ = 20 ms (the interpolation time constant), the tone level (-26 dBFS RMS in the bursts) and the 30 Hz scripted publish rate are UNTUNED placeholders.
- **Verified against the engine, not against ears.** The conversion is checked against what the node renders (channel levels, exact rotation invariance). Channel 0 = left on the physical headphones, the generic HRTF's quality for any person, externalization and front/back confusion are untested. No personalization.
- **Code paths for macOS 14-26 were compiled but never run.** `connect` / `play` are used in their old forms below macOS 27 (behind `#available`), and this Mac runs 27.2, so only the new forms ran. The package minimum is 14.0.
- **Device change** (headphones plugged in or out while the test runs) only produces a notice and an idle state; not exercised on a real device change. Bluetooth headphones add their own latency, unmeasured here.
- **AirPods-style system head tracking** can not be switched off by this app; the manual test asks the user to turn it off. The app itself sets `isListenerHeadTrackingEnabled = false` (macOS 15+); that assertion is not mutation-checked (the property is already false by default here).
- **ThreadSanitizer cannot see a missing lock around the `Applied` struct copy in the pump** (bulk-memcpy blind spot, same as the mailbox finding); that lock is checked by review only. The scalar timing counters' lock IS covered: with that lock removed and the race window widened by a 1 ms sleep, TSan reports `data race ... ListenerPosePump.timing.getter` (7 reports); on the real code it is clean.
- Volume safety relies on the user's system volume plus the card's slider (default 30%, applied before the engine starts). There is no limiter and no loudness measurement.
- One virtual source only; no distance/room modelling; reverb off; the source is 2 m away with the engine's default inverse-distance attenuation.

## Step 3a verification record (2026-11-05)
**Run, and passed**
- `swift build` (debug and release, no warnings); `swift test` **303 tests** (230 Core + 40 Tracking + 33 Engine, one opt-in); the same under `swift test --sanitize=thread` with no race reports; `scripts/tsan-mailbox.sh` (2M samples: torn=0, backwards=0, exit 0).
- **Mutation check of the new logic: 26 distinct injected bugs** (conversion direction, forward/up swap, each flip switch, geometry sign and inverse, interpolator alpha / bad targets / slerp order, script signs, direction helper, tone ramps and level, renderer source mirror / listener vectors / output type / HRTFHQ / volume, pump flips / unchanged-send / time / retargeting, timer drain, publisher source update), one at a time, every file restored and byte-compared afterwards. First pass **24/26**: one mutant did not compile (a Swift self-assignment error; re-run in a compilable form: caught) and the stop-drain race was caught in only 2 of 3 runs, so a deterministic gated test was written (a sink that parks a tick inside itself) and the drain mutant is now caught **5/5**. That test first deadlocked on the broken code (see findings) and was fixed.
- The strong offline checks (source side, rotation invariance, whole-chain sweep, flip mirrors the sweep, zero volume = exact silence) are in the Engine tests; the measured numbers are in "Step 3a findings".
- **Live, silent:** `SA_LIVE_AUDIO=1 swift test --filter LiveEngineSmokeTests` (engine started on the real output device at 48 kHz, volume 0 asserted before start, 148 pump ticks, clean stop) and the app's own `--audio-test-selfcheck` (the card's model code start / readout / yaw flip / stop, volume 0). No camera and no microphone were opened by either.
- Release-object scan for Swift runtime calls in the render-thread-capable Core code: none found in 124 functions (one-time observation, not a gate).

**Not run (needs Sanjai)**
Any listening: tests A-E through headphones, which channel reaches which ear, how the HRTF sounds, loudness, clicks at the loop point or when the tone jumps, dropouts; whether flips are ever needed. The HUD card's menu, slider, checkboxes and buttons (never clicked; layout never seen). Behaviour under load, heat, Bluetooth, device changes and for long runs. The macOS 14-26 branches. Everything camera-related is as before: the real self-test report has not been confirmed.

## Step 2d findings (2026-11-05), from the two real self-test reports
Sources: run **A** = `docs/test-results/selftest-20261105-074551-normal-light.{json,md}` (07:45) and run **B** = `...-081218-normal-light.{json,md}` (08:12). Both are real camera runs ("MacBook Air Camera", 640x480, Vision revision 3), normal light, all invert flags off, not simulated, no sleep during either (pmset log), 900 frames at 30.0 fps with a 33 ms maximum frame interval and no stall note, camera release CLEAN, green light "not asked". Nothing from them was applied, **no sign default was changed**, and the tracker is still not connected to the renderer. Everything below describes what the reports and the code show; everything introduced in Step 2d stays **UNVERIFIED on hardware until a clean real run**.

**Measured in those two sittings (not benchmarks: one sitting each, normal light, debug build).** Delivery (sample timestamp to callback) mean 61 / 63 ms (p95 65 / 65, max 67 / 68); Vision mean 18 / 18 ms (p95 22 / 21, max 32 / 31); total sample-to-pose mean 79 / 81 ms (p95 84 / 84, max 93 / 92); mean quality 0.80 / 0.76 over the hold + rest window. The earlier Step 2 session read 94-97 ms total (delivery 81-82 ms). The two sets differ and the reason is unknown; both belong to the Step 5 latency investigation.

**What the sign results looked like (printed, not confirmed).** A: yaw, pitch and roll all INCONCLUSIVE. B: yaw DETERMINED (raw sign +, invert off; repetitions +53.4° and +63.0°, leakage 0.49 and 0.40), pitch and roll INCONCLUSIVE. Two patterns are visible in the numbers and are what the coaching targets:
- The moves were big and uncoached: turns of 37-63° of yaw (no target was given), chin-up 7-27°, ear tilts 7-25°.
- The axes were strongly coupled. In 3 of the 4 roll repetitions raw Vision **yaw** changed by -41°, -51° and -57° while roll changed 18°, 25° and 7° (the fourth: yaw +4°), the same sign in both runs. Yaw turns also moved roll and pitch (B: roll -26° and -25°, pitch -15° and -19° for yaw +53° and +63°). Whether that is how Vision's angles couple when a face is turned or rolled, or how the moves were performed, cannot be told from numbers alone; it is the main reason roll was never determined. The live coach now flags another axis moving.

### 1. Loss-test flapping (run B)
**What the report shows.** The analysis window (which starts 1 s before the cover cue) began in `decaying`, then `locked, holding, locked, holding, locked, holding, decaying, locked, holding, locked, holding, decaying, static, locked`, with the first hold lasting 0.07 s instead of 0.5 s. So tracking was already lost, and chattering, **before** the cover cue, during the held turn. When the first loss happened the pose was 58.7° away from neutral, while the instruction said "about 25°" (run A: 11.8°). Frames kept flowing normally the whole time (30.0 fps, 33 ms maximum interval, face found in 100% of the statistics-window frames), so this was not a stall or a sleep.

**What the code shows (the mechanism).** The pipeline compared the per-frame quality, the weakest of seven components, with one threshold (0.5). Any score that hovers around 0.5 flips the state on every frame by construction; the synthetic tests now reproduce exactly that chatter with the old single-threshold configuration (`QualityGateConfig.singleThreshold`, 30-60 transitions on hovering quality) and show the new gate does not.

**What is NOT known.** Which component was limiting in run B. The reports did not record it. Likely candidates at a large yaw are face size, Vision confidence and angular speed, but that is a hypothesis; the new "which component limited it" table (per group of phases) will show it in the next real run.

**Change.** `QualityGate`: hysteresis (loss below 0.40, recovery at or above 0.60) and dwell (0.10 s below before loss, 0.20 s above before recovery; a short dip coasts with the pose frozen and nothing fed to the filter; frames that stopped arriving are a loss at once). Tests drive the real pipeline with quality hovering around the threshold (a 3 Hz sine 0.38-0.62, noise inside the band, single-frame and two-frame dips, a lost state that never reaches the recovery threshold) and show 0 chatter, while a real loss still reaches holding / decaying / static on time (hold 0.5 s measured from the declared loss, never a jump) and recovery needs an uninterrupted good stretch. The loss test now also waits for a locked tracker before the cover cue and reports **INVALID** (not FAIL) if tracking was not locked, which is what run B was. The report records the limiting component per phase group. All thresholds are UNTUNED.

### 2. The 16.9° / 18.7° single-frame pose step in LOCKED state
**What the reports show.** A: 16.93°, in `locked`, 2.23 s after the cover cue. The report's "locked before loss" time is 3.23 s counted from the window start (1 s before the cue), so `holding` began about 2.23 s after the cue: the same moment as the step, i.e. the step is on the last locked frame before the loss. B: 18.68°, in `locked`, 6.35 s after the cover cue, which is 0.70 s after the final relock (the relock came 0.65 s after the uncover cue, i.e. at 5.65 s), well past the 0.25 s reacquire blend. Both are far above the 10° threshold and above anything the designed blends produce in these runs (the decay and reacquire blends peak at about 2° per frame or less for the poses involved).

**What is NOT known.** Whether either was a real fast head turn or a Vision outlier. The reports store only the largest step's size and state, not the raw angles around it, so the question cannot be answered from them. One observation, not a conclusion: run A's step sits exactly where tracking was being lost (when the hand covered the lens), the place where a garbage last frame would be expected, but a deliberate quick movement at the cover cue would look the same.

**Change.** The report now stores a numbers-only window (±15 frames) of raw Vision angles, mapped angles, the filtered output pose, state, quality, limiting component and face box around the largest step, plus a shape classification (spike that comes back within 5 frames / movement spread over several frames / abrupt and sustained / face appeared or vanished / output-only, i.e. a blend or filter effect / indeterminate). Thresholds (30% to count as "back", 50% of the 10-frame displacement for "multi-frame", 25% of the output step for "output-only") are untuned and printed. **No outlier rejection or velocity limit was added**, because the data to justify a threshold does not exist and an untested limit risks flattening legitimate fast turns. Decision rule for the next run: a `spike` shape means add a median-of-three or jump-reject stage and justify its threshold from the window; `multiFrameMovement` or `abruptAndSustained` means the step is movement (or a mode switch) and the loss-test threshold, not the tracker, is what needs revisiting; `outputOnly` points at the pipeline. The classifier is tested to never call a 20°-90° turn spread over 2-12 frames a spike.

### 3. Sign-detector noise floor (required movement of 67-70° in run A)
**What the code shows.** The noise figure was **already a robust spread, not a range**: `RobustStats.sigma` = 1.4826 × the median absolute deviation of the instructed axis in the reference window. A value of 14° therefore means that at least half of the 30-45 reference samples differed from the median by roughly 9° or more: the "baseline" window was not still, so the figure measured movement inside the window, not jitter. The values: run A yaw rep 1 14.1° (45 samples of the baseline phase), roll rep 1 13.5°; run B roll rep 1 8.3°, rep 2 4.2°; all others 0.3-3.9°. The raw samples were not stored, so what moved cannot be shown.

**Change (no threshold was loosened).** The requirement is unchanged (max(floor, 5 × robust spread); floors 15° / 10° / 10°; leakage limit 0.6; two agreeing repetitions). The report now prints, for every repetition, baseline statistics of the reference and hold windows per axis (n, median, σ from MAD, range, p10-p90, drift of overlapping sub-windows) so a large figure can be read for what it is, and the reason now says "reference window not steady (spread, drift)" instead of "movement too small" when the reference's own spread sets the requirement. Tests show the figure ignores outliers (4 wild samples out of 40 move σ from about 1° to under 2.5° while the range would have said about 80°) and that an unsteady reference stays unusable. **LIKELY**: a new, clearly separate outcome when at least two repetitions had enough, steady movement and agree on the direction but cross-axis leakage failed. It is shown as "LIKELY (not applied)", never part of `recommendedMapping`, never offered for Apply. On the numbers of these two reports nothing becomes LIKELY (no axis had two repetitions with enough movement and leakage as the only failure), which is the intended conservatism.

### 4. Coaching and a locked start for the loss test
The run panel shows live per-axis bars (deviation from the start pose, target band) with "good / a bit more / too far / other axis moving" during holds and "steady / still moving / not back at the centre" during rests, and asks for about 30° yaw, 20° pitch, 20° roll and 25° for the loss turn, one axis at a time. The cover cue waits until the tracker has been LOCKED for 1.0 s in a row (at most 10 s); otherwise the run ends with the loss test INVALID.

### 5. Why the green-light question was "not asked" in both reports
**What the code shows.** The default value was `not asked` and only a click on one of two buttons replaced it; the buttons sat inside the fifth and last card of the results (below signs, apply, loss and statistics), shown only after the 1.2 s release check. "Copy report" is higher up and copies the report as it is at that moment. Nothing recorded that the question had been displayed, so "not asked" could mean "shown but not answered". It cannot be told from the files whether the question was seen. **Change:** the question is the first thing in the results as soon as they appear (the app has already stopped the camera by then), and it is recorded as **yes / no / not answered**: a real run starts as "not answered", Copy report says so if it is still open, and the files show it. Simulated runs say "not asked (no camera light applies)". Reports written before this change still load (schema 1; the new fields are optional).

### Known limitations of Step 2d (stated, not measured)
- The gate thresholds (0.40 / 0.60 / 0.10 s / 0.20 s), the coach thresholds, the LockGate (1.0 s / 10 s) and the evidence-classifier fractions are untuned placeholders. First lock now takes at least 0.2 s.
- A forward jump of the capture timeline while no face is visible completes the hold / decay at once (the machine measures elapsed time from timestamps). Real capture timestamps are continuous, so this is not expected; the resume test now uses a continuous timeline.
- MAD is blind to a bimodal window whose larger cluster holds more than half of the samples; the drift figure and the live "still moving" check are what catch slow movement.
- Whether the coach's per-axis feedback is usable for a real person (it shows magnitude only, never direction), and whether 30° / 20° / 20° moves are big enough for Vision to give clean signs, is untested.
- The green-light banner could not be rendered for review (only real runs show it); its layout and buttons were never seen.

## Step 2d verification record (2026-11-05)
**Run, and passed**
- `swift build` (debug and release, no warnings); `swift test` **383 tests** (310 Core + 40 Tracking + 33 Engine, one opt-in); the same under `swift test --sanitize=thread` with no race reports; `scripts/tsan-mailbox.sh` (2M samples: torn=0, backwards=0, exit 0). New logic runs on the tracker's lock only; no new threads.
- **Mutation check of the new logic: 38 deliberately injected bugs**, one at a time (gate: hysteresis, both dwells, countdown resets, forceLost, default thresholds; pipeline coast and frames-stopped; state machine coast; session lock gate, locked clock, stale and watchdog frames, give-up, shift; coached target; loss INVALID and evidence centring; evidence shape rules; LIKELY promotion, one-repetition LIKELY, recommended mapping, wording, range-vs-MAD noise, dropped noise term, leakage flag, hold steadiness; coach bands and flags; green-light default and quality fractions). **38/38 caught**, no survivors, no compile failures, no hangs (every run had a 420 s timeout). All nine files were restored and byte-compared afterwards. Each pattern was checked to match exactly once before the run.
- Behaviour tests worth naming: hovering quality gives 30-60 state changes with the old single threshold and 0 with the gate (a 3 Hz sine 0.38-0.62, noise inside the band, one- and two-frame dips, a lost state that never reaches the recovery threshold); a real loss still reaches holding / decaying / static with hold 0.5 s from the declared loss and no jump; recovery needs 0.2 s uninterrupted; the cover cue waits for 1.0 s LOCKED, ignores stale frames and watchdog ticks, shifts later phases by the wait and gives up as INVALID after 10 s; 20°-90° turns over 2-12 frames are never classified as spikes; the noise figure ignores 4 wild samples out of 40 (σ about 1° where the range would have said about 80°); no sign threshold changed; LIKELY never reaches `recommendedMapping`; the two committed real reports (schema 1) still decode.
- Existing tests that encoded the old single-threshold behaviour were changed on purpose, not loosened: seven tracker / pipeline tests (one bad frame is now a coast, loss needs 0.1 s, the first lock needs 0.2 s), one resume test now uses a continuous camera timeline, two guard tests (simulated move sizes now 30° / 20° / 20°; the report-size cap 40 kB to 100 kB for the 46 kB report with the evidence window), and wording tests for the green-light states.
- **Simulated end-to-end run of the real app** (`--simulate --autorun-selftest --render-ui`, results into a scratch folder, nothing committed): the flow passes with the lock gate opening at the nominal time, the coaching bars render ("Good — 30° (target about 30°)"), the report has the quality groups, baseline tables and an evidence window (output-only for the healthy blend), and the whole run takes 50 s. The simulated person is locked and compliant, so it says nothing about real people.

**Not run (needs Sanjai and the real camera)**
A clean real self-test with the Step 2d changes: whether the coaching bars help, whether 30° / 20° / 20° moves give DETERMINED axes, whether the hysteresis thresholds remove the chatter on a real face (and which quality component limits at a large yaw), what the evidence window says about the 17-19° steps, the loss test with a locked start, the green-light banner and buttons (AppKit-backed controls were never clicked or rendered; the banner only appears for real runs), dim room, and the Step 3a listening test. Everything introduced here is **UNVERIFIED on hardware**.

## Step 2e findings (2026-11-05), from the 14:58 real run
Source: `docs/test-results/selftest-20261105-145801-normal-light.{json,md}` (schema 2, the Step 2d build). Real camera run ("MacBook Air Camera", 640x480, Vision revision 3), normal light, all invert flags off, not simulated; 899 frames at 30.0 fps in the statistics window, 33 ms maximum frame interval, face found in 100% of frames, camera release CLEAN, **green light NOT ANSWERED**. The two files are committed on their own as `b8e354d`, exactly as the app wrote them. Nothing was applied, **no sign default was changed**, Sanjai has **not confirmed any sign**, and the tracker is still not connected to the renderer. Everything below is what the report and the code show; everything added in Step 2e stays **UNVERIFIED on hardware**.

**Signs (printed, not confirmed).** yaw DETERMINED, raw sign + (Δ +66.0° and +72.0°, leakage 0.28 and 0.15; the same sign as run B); pitch DETERMINED, raw sign − (Δ -40.9° and -28.5°, leakage 0.04 and 0.46); roll INCONCLUSIVE (Δ -3.7° and -2.7°, required 10°).

**Absolute raw Vision values (median, degrees; the schema-2 report stores them for all three axes).** Roll holds: yaw -60.2 and -59.4, pitch +27.2 and +27.2, roll -1.9 and -0.3. Their references (the rests before them): yaw -2.4 and -4.0, pitch +0.4 and +8.4, roll +1.8 and +2.5, close to the baseline (yaw -2.2, pitch +4.0, roll +1.8). So the large yaw delta in the roll holds comes from the holds, not from a reference that was still turned. The window before the first roll hold followed the look-UP holds, not a yaw hold. Yaw holds +63.8 and +69.5 (targets 30°, so 2.2-2.4×); pitch holds -34.4 and -33.6 (the two Δ differ, -40.9 vs -28.5, only because the references were +6.5 and -5.1; the holds agree within 0.8°). Across three real runs, roll holds have read raw yaw -41° to -58° in 5 of 6 repetitions with raw roll moving 3-25°.

**What the data cannot show:** whether the head was turned about 60° to the side with the chin down, tilted in a way Vision reads as yaw and pitch, or both. One derived hint, not a verdict: the left-turn holds (raw yaw about +64° to +70°) shrank the face box to a minimum of 0.150 and 69% of their frames were under 0.20, while the roll holds (raw yaw -60°) had a derived minimum of 0.190 and 89% of frames at 0.20 or more. (Box height was not stored; it is derived by inverting the size score, 0.10 + 0.10 × quality for size-limited frames, which matched the 14 size-limited frames of the loss-step window that have both numbers to 0.0000, and the 17 unlimited frames there all had 0.201 or more. Group means are lower bounds.)

**Why the coach said nothing (by code reading, with the stored window medians; the coach output was not recorded).** The other-axis warning waited for the instructed axis to reach half its target (10° for roll); roll reached 3.7° and 2.7°, so the message would have been "A bit more: 4° so far, aim for about 20°", with the yaw bar drawn at about 58° of its 60° scale, grey and unflagged. The yaw holds would have read "too far" (66° and 72°), pitch hold 1 "too far" (41°), pitch hold 2 "good" (28°, other axes 13° and 2°).

**Tracking quality.** Outside the cover phase every low-quality frame was limited by "size" (100%). The size score is 0 at a face-box height of 0.10 and 1 at 0.20, so a box of 0.15-0.16, which the evidence window shows at a raw yaw of about 65°, scores about 0.5-0.6, inside the 0.40-0.60 hysteresis band. In the look-UP holds 29% of frames were below 0.40 (derived minimum box 0.129). Hypothesis for the 08:12 chatter, not shown by any data: size hovering in that band at a large yaw. Whether the tracker state left LOCKED during any hold was **not recorded** (only the loss test's states are).

**Loss test.** PASS: locked → holding → decaying → static → locked, holding 0.50 s and decaying 1.50 s as configured, 2.93 s locked before the loss, reacquire 1.18 s after the uncover cue (includes your reaction time). The jump check is WEAK: the held pose was 2.4° from neutral (you turned to about 69° and came back toward the centre around the time you reached for the camera). The largest output step was 8.59° (threshold 10°), in LOCKED state 0.74 s after the cover cue; the evidence window classifies it as `multiFrameMovement` (raw step 16.5° in one frame inside a 41.9° move over 10 frames), a description of the shape consistent with a real fast return, not a verdict on the earlier 17-19° steps (different data). One run, started LOCKED, head near neutral at the loss: it does not show that the 08:12 chatter is fixed.

**What Step 2e changes.** (1) The report records what was missing: whole-phase face-box height, quality and limiting component, and the tracker-state seconds per phase, plus a per-frame series so any window can be recomputed (schema 3). (2) The coach warns about any non-instructed axis over a placeholder limit (15°) at any time, names the wrong motion, and flags the bars. **Not changed:** thresholds, the quality ramp, the hysteresis, the sign defaults, the tracker-to-renderer wiring, and the detector.

### Known limitations of Step 2e (stated, not measured)
- The 15° limit is a placeholder. In the 14:58 yaw holds roll read -16.7° and -7.8° at yaw +63.8° and +69.5°. If that coupling is Vision's rather than the head's, a legitimate turn of that size could trigger the new warning. How often it fires on a compliant move is unknown until a real run with the new coach.
- The message names the wrong motion by which axis moved (magnitude); it cannot know the direction, so "right ear toward your right shoulder" is an instruction, not a judgment.
- Per-phase state seconds assume a frame's state holds until the next frame arrives (frames come about every 33 ms; watchdog ticks count as frames). A stall without ticks would be booked to the last state.
- The per-phase statistics cover the whole phase, including the first second of a hold in which the head is still moving into position; the sign detector uses the last 2 s. The series allows other windows.
- The quality settings in effect (including the size ramp) are not recorded in the report; the text assumes the defaults.
- The JSON grew to about 210 KB. Compact encoding would roughly halve it at the cost of readability; not done.
- The coach view change (every flagged axis, orange label and value) has no unit test; it was rendered from the shipped view code in a scratch package (three scenarios) and never seen in the running app.
- The 14:58 report's green-light answer is NOT ANSWERED; it stays that way in the files.

## Step 2e verification record (2026-11-05)
**Run, and passed**
- `swift build` (debug and release, no warnings); `swift test` **428 tests** (355 Core + 40 Tracking + 33 Engine, 45 of the Core tests are new; one opt-in Engine test skipped by default); the same under `swift test --sanitize=thread` with no race reports. `scripts/tsan-mailbox.sh` was not re-run: the PoseMailbox and the threading were not touched in this step.
- Existing tests changed on purpose, not loosened: the coach test whose "before the move is underway" case now warns (and its message wording); the schema-version test (2 to 3); the report-size caps (100 kB and 150 kB to 400 kB for the 210 kB report); and the numbers-only structure guard, which used to forbid any array over 40 items and now allows exactly one exception, `frameSeries`, whose columns may hold only numbers, booleans or null (and 4 legend strings), at most 5,000 rows each (a normal run has about 1,400; a 640x480 pixel buffer would have 307,200).
- **Mutation check: 49 deliberately injected bugs in four files**, one at a time, each pattern checked to match exactly once. Coach: absolute limit removed, only once the move is underway (the old behaviour), `>=` instead of `>`, hard-coded instead of configured, default 150, ratio rule removed or applied too early, only the worst axis flagged, the least-moving axis named, wrong motion names, too-far outranking the warning, the instructed axis' angle printed instead of the culprit's, the instructed axis counted as an "other" axis, flags not reported. State seconds: a frame's state credited to the previous interval, no clipping at either window end, nothing before the first frame, the last frame owning no time, watchdog ticks dropped, static and holding mis-booked. Statistics: median as mean, min and max swapped, non-finite values kept, width for height, frames-with-box, dominant limiting component and tie order, `<=` for the two quality thresholds, median quality as minimum. Phases and series: nominal instead of shifted start times, phases that never started, time not relative to the run start, yaw and pitch columns swapped, radians instead of degrees, state codes swapped, watchdog ticks dropped, box column, phase column, rounding, non-finite values, row order. Report: schema version, records not written, series not written, per-phase table absent.
  - The first full run caught 46 of 49. Two **survived** (K02 `>=` for the limit: my boundary test was blind because 15° goes through degrees, radians and back as 14.999999999999998; T06 watchdog ticks left out of the per-phase timeline: no test went through `phaseRecords` with ticks) and one (F05) crashed the test process on an out-of-range index, which the harness labelled "did not compile" (it compiles). Fixes: the boundary test now uses the deviation the coach itself measured; a phase-level test with ticks as the only frames; row counts are required before indexing.
  - **Final full run on the final tests: 49/49 caught**, no survivors, no hangs, all four files byte-identical afterwards (SHA-256 compared).
- **Simulated end-to-end run of the real app** (`--simulate --autorun-selftest --exit-after-selftest --results-dir=<scratch>`, nothing committed): the app wrote a schema-3 JSON (about 210 kB) and markdown (about 12 kB): 17 phase records, a series of 1,412 rows in 10 columns of equal length, numbers only, no image-related words in the file; the phase state seconds add up to the run length (47.05 s, with a 0.05 s lock-gate wait that shifted the later phases' start times).
- **Bars rendered** from the shipped `CoachBars` source (extracted by script into a scratch package) for three scenarios: the 14:58 roll-hold numbers (yaw and pitch rows orange, message "You are turning your head (58°) instead of just tilting it. Tilt only: right ear toward your right shoulder, nose still pointing at the screen."), a clean tilt (green "Good — 18°"), and yaw creeping to 17° before the tilt starts (flagged). The view has no unit test and was not seen in the running app.
- The real schema-2 14:58 report decodes with the new reader (that test is skipped silently where the file is absent).

**Not run (needs Sanjai and the real camera)**
A real run with the Step 2e build: whether the 15° warning helps or annoys (in particular whether it fires on legitimate turns because of Vision's own cross-axis coupling), what the per-phase table and the per-frame series say about the head during the roll holds and whether the tracker left LOCKED during any hold, the green-light answer for the 14:58 run, a dim-room run, and the Step 3a listening test. Everything introduced in Step 2e is **UNVERIFIED on hardware**.

## Step 2f findings and verification record (2026-11-05)
**1. Coach: "too far" first.** Cause: in the Step 2e order the wrong-axis check ran before the too-far check. In the 14:58 run the yaw holds were 66° and 72° from the starting pose (target 30°) and moved roll by 18.5° and 10.8° and pitch by 16.4° and 10.8°; with the Step 2e rules hold 1 would have been "You are tilting your head sideways (19°) instead of just turning it" for what was an overshoot (whether that coupling is the head's or Vision's is not known). Now, once the instructed axis is past `tooFarFraction` (1.5) x its target the answer is always "Too far (…°, aim for about …°) — come back a little" and no axis is flagged. Below that the Step 2e rules are unchanged (absolute 15° limit at any time, half-target ratio rule once the move is underway), so a wrong motion in the band, and the roll case of 14:58 (3.7° of tilt with yaw and pitch far away), are still called out. No threshold changed; the later, now unreachable too-far branch was removed. In-band moves that couple into other axes by more than 15° are still flagged; that part of the Step 2e limitation stands.

**2. A head turning at constant speed with short quality dips (synthetic, measured before any assertion was added).** Setup: the real `HeadPosePipeline` with default One-Euro parameters and the default gate, 30 fps, a perfectly steady head and exact orientations, still for 1 s (the gate locks) and then turning about yaw at constant speed; dip frames have quality 0.2 (below the 0.40 loss threshold) but still carry the true orientation. Largest per-frame step of the OUTPUT pose, degrees (ratio to the control in brackets):

| Turn speed (head per frame) | control | 1-frame dip | 2-frame dip | 3-frame dip (probe only) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 45 deg/s (1.50°) | 1.505 | 3.004 (2.00x) | 4.505 (2.99x) | 6.006 (3.99x) |
| **90 deg/s (3.00°)** | 3.041 | **6.009 (1.98x)** | **9.012 (2.96x)** | 12.014 (3.95x) |
| 180 deg/s (6.00°) | 6.205 | 12.013 (1.94x) | 18.018 (2.90x) | 24.021 (3.87x) |

Five isolated dips in one run gave the same maxima (90 deg/s: 6.079 for 1-frame, 9.110 for 2-frame dips), so dips do not accumulate. The state stayed LOCKED in every dip run (no holding, no decaying). The 3-frame column and the 45 / 180 deg/s rows come from a throwaway probe that was not kept; the committed tests cover 1- and 2-frame dips at all three speeds. Per-frame steps around a 2-frame dip at 90 deg/s: `3.01 3.01 3.01 0.00 0.00 9.01 3.00 3.00 3.00 3.00` (control: 3.00 throughout); around a 1-frame dip: `3.01 3.01 3.01 0.00 6.01 3.00 …`. Largest angle between the output and the true head orientation at 90 deg/s: 8.5° (control: the maximum over the run, which includes the start of the turn; the steady-state lag is 8.0°, see "Step 2g findings"), 11.1° (1-frame dip), 14.1° (2-frame dip).

**What that means.** **The dip case is clearly worse than the control**: the coasting pose freezes for the dip and then catches up in ONE step of (dip frames + 1) x the head's per-frame motion, so a listener would hear the scene stall for 33-67 ms and then lurch by 2-3 frames' worth. The catch-up is bounded by how far the head really moved (no overshoot, no snap, the output is back on the control's trajectory one frame later), so I judged it reasonable to pin that and **did not change the gate or the filter**. It is not smooth, though, and the step scales with speed: for comparison, the loss test's pose-jump threshold is 10°; a 2-frame dip at 90 deg/s gives 9.0° (below it), a 1-frame dip at 180 deg/s gives 12.0° and a 3-frame dip at 90 deg/s gives 12.0° (both above it). The gate's loss dwell is 0.10 s, so a dip of up to 3 frames at 30 fps still coasts. Options, none implemented and none decided: (a) slew-limit the catch-up after a coast (spreads the step over a few frames, adds a little lag); (b) extrapolate the pose during the coast with the filter's velocity estimate (no stall, but it overshoots if the head stops); (c) leave it, because the step is bounded by the head's own motion and short dips at normal speeds are small. This is a synthetic result: real dips may also come with bad angles, which this test does not model.

### Known limitations of Step 2f (stated, not measured)
- The dip test uses perfect orientations and quality dips only; real low-quality frames also carry noisy or wrong angles (the reason they are not fed to the filter). It pins no smoothness.
- 30 fps only; the default One-Euro parameters and gate (all untuned placeholders); no sensor noise.
- A real run is needed to see how often dips of 1-3 frames occur and whether anyone can hear the catch-up.

### Step 2f verification record
- `swift build` (debug and release, no warnings); `swift test` **439 tests** (366 Core + 40 Tracking + 33 Engine; one opt-in Engine test skipped by default); the same under `swift test --sanitize=thread` with no race reports. The PoseMailbox and the threading were not touched, so `scripts/tsan-mailbox.sh` was not re-run.
- One existing test changed on purpose: `anotherAxisMovingIsCalledOutOnceTheMainMoveIsUnderway` used to assert that another axis outranks "too far" (60° of a 30° target with pitch 50°); it now asserts "too far". It was the only test that failed after the reorder.
- **Mutation check: 24 injected bugs, 24/24 caught** (the 24 of the Step 2f harness; **K12, the Step 2e coach mutant dropped from it, is a SURVIVOR, so counting it the Step 2f figure is 24 of 25**, see "Step 6a findings"), every pattern matched exactly once, all files byte-identical afterwards. Six new coach mutants (early too-far check disabled; `>=` instead of `>`; threshold 99x; too-far only when no other axis is over the limit; **the old order restored with two patches**; too-far reporting flagged axes), four new pipeline mutants for the dip tests (coast returns the neutral pose; a coasting frame treated as a loss; **the filter reset on every coast**, i.e. the pose snaps back to raw after a dip; the loss dwell ignored), and 14 of the 15 Step 2e coach mutants re-run unchanged (the 15th, K12 "too-far outranks the warning", was dropped here as "equivalent to the real code"; **that label was unproven and is corrected in the Step 6a record below: K12 is a SURVIVOR that differs from the real code only for a NaN deviation**).
- **Not run (needs Sanjai and the real camera):** a real run with the Step 2e / 2f build. Everything here is **UNVERIFIED on hardware**.

## Step 2g findings (2026-11-05): filter characterization, SYNTHETIC
**Every number in this section is SYNTHETIC: the real `HeadPosePipeline` (default One-Euro parameters minCutoff 1.0 Hz, beta 0.5, derivative cutoff 1.0 Hz; default quality gate) driven by exact, noise-free or seeded-noise orientations at a perfectly regular 30 fps. No camera, no hardware claim, no tuning.** Nothing in the pipeline, filter, gate, state machine, coach or wiring was changed. The harness is `Tests/SpatialAudioCoreTests/FilterCharacterizationTests.swift`; `SA_PRINT_FILTER_TABLE=1 swift test --filter FilterCharacterization` prints the tables below.

**Method.** *Constant turn:* the head sits still for 1 s (the gate locks), then turns about yaw at constant speed for 4 s; the lag is the mean over the LAST second of how far the output is behind the input at the same frame (signed, positive = behind); "lag spread" is max minus min over that second (0 = converged); time lag = lag / speed. *Settle:* the head then stops; the time until the output stays within the tolerance of the final orientation for the rest of a 3 s window (resolution one frame, 33 ms). *Jitter:* a still head with independent seeded Gaussian noise (Box-Muller, same seed for every sigma) on yaw, pitch and roll, 3,000 frames, the first 90 discarded; gain = standard deviation of the output's Euler angle / that of the generated input, per axis.

| turn speed (deg/s) | head per frame (deg) | steady-state lag (deg) | time lag (ms) | lag spread, last second (deg) | lag predicted from the cutoff law (deg) | settle to 1° (ms) | settle to 0.1° (ms) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 45 | 1.50 | 5.14 | 114 | 0.0000 | 5.14 | 267 | 667 |
| 90 | 3.00 | 8.02 | 89 | 0.0000 | 8.02 | 300 | 667 |
| 180 | 6.00 | 11.14 | 62 | 0.0000 | 11.14 | 267 | 633 |
| 360 | 12.00 | 13.83 | 38 | 0.0000 | 13.83 | 200 | 500 |

| noise sigma (deg, per axis) | input std yaw / pitch / roll (deg) | output std yaw / pitch / roll (deg) | jitter gain yaw / pitch / roll | mean gain |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.5 | 0.500 / 0.507 / 0.498 | 0.172 / 0.180 / 0.173 | 0.343 / 0.355 / 0.348 | 0.349 |
| 1.0 | 1.001 / 1.014 / 0.996 | 0.375 / 0.393 / 0.378 | 0.375 / 0.388 / 0.380 | 0.381 |
| 2.0 | 2.001 / 2.028 / 1.991 | 0.856 / 0.898 / 0.863 | 0.428 / 0.443 / 0.434 | 0.435 |
| 4.0 | 4.003 / 4.056 / 3.983 | 2.030 / 2.129 / 2.047 | 0.507 / 0.525 / 0.514 | 0.515 |

**What the numbers show (synthetic).**
- The filter's lag is large and speed-dependent in the way its design says: the angular lag grows with speed (5.1° at 45 deg/s to 13.8° at 360 deg/s) while the time lag shrinks (114 ms to 38 ms) because the cutoff rises with speed. The measured lag equals the lag predicted from the cutoff law (a first-order smoother with cutoff = minCutoff + beta x speed has a steady lag of step x (1 - a) / a per frame) to within 0.5% at every speed, so the harness and the pipeline agree with the filter's own formula. For this beta the continuous-time limit of the angular lag at very high speed is 1 / (2 pi beta) = 0.318 rad = 18.2°.
- After a stop the output is within 1° after 200-300 ms and within 0.1° after 500-667 ms (to the 33 ms frame resolution).
- Jitter is cut to about 0.35 (sigma 0.5°) to 0.52 (sigma 4°) of the input spread, rising with the noise because the noise itself raises the speed estimate and so the cutoff. At sigma 4° the output still wanders with a standard deviation of about 2°. So at rest the filter trades roughly 0.35-0.5 of the jitter for the lag above while moving; whether that trade is right is not decided here.
- For scale only (not a sensor-noise measurement, and those windows include real head motion): the robust spreads of the real "stay still" windows in the 14:58 report were 0.3-1.8° (3.65° in the first baseline).
- **The measured 81 ms (sample timestamp to pose ready, three real runs) does not include this algorithmic lag**; in head-motion terms the two add up (this synthetic filter lag is 89 ms at 90 deg/s, 38-114 ms across the speeds), and the audio side (the pose pump's 20 ms smoothing, the audio buffers) adds more that has not been measured. No end-to-end figure is claimed.
- **No test anywhere pins the default One-Euro parameters:** changing the default beta to 0 or minCutoff to 2 Hz breaks no test in the whole Core suite (mutation check below). That is by design for these new tests (they assert properties, not targets), but it means a later tuning change is not guarded by any test, only recorded by this table.

### Known limitations of Step 2g (stated, not measured)
- Synthetic throughout: exact orientations, a regular 30 fps, white Gaussian noise independent per axis. Real Vision noise is not white or Gaussian, real frames jitter in time, and real heads accelerate; none of that is modelled.
- Turns are about yaw only (the quaternion filter treats axes alike, but pitch and roll lag was not measured); constant speed only (no acceleration phase); the lag is read in the steady state, not during the start of a turn (the 90 deg/s run's largest output-to-head angle in the Step 2f probe, 8.5°, includes that start; the steady value is 8.02°).
- Settle times have one-frame resolution and depend on the 1° / 0.1° tolerances chosen here.
- The 360 deg/s case is faster than a face can be tracked; it is there to show the trend, not as a use case.

### Step 2g verification record
- `swift build` (debug and release, no warnings); `swift test` **449 tests** (376 Core + 40 Tracking + 33 Engine; two opt-in tests skipped by default); the same under `swift test --sanitize=thread` with no race reports. The mailbox and the threading were not touched, so `scripts/tsan-mailbox.sh` was not re-run.
- **Mutation check of the new assertions: 15 injected changes, 15/15 as expected.** Caught (13): the filter output equal to the input; the filter overshooting the input; the filter nearly frozen; the cutoff FALLING with speed; the speed term subtracted from the cutoff; the speed term ignored (caught only by the cutoff-law cross-check); the filter bypassed in the pipeline; and six bugs in the harness itself (uniform instead of Gaussian noise, settle measured on the wrong side of the tolerance, time lag in the wrong unit, lag read in the first instead of the last second, the "output" spread measured on the input, the lag with the wrong sign). **Survived, as designed (2): default beta 0.5 to 0.0 and default minCutoff 1.0 to 2.0 Hz**: tuning changes, not property violations, and no test in the Core suite (old or new) fails on them. All files byte-identical afterwards.
- **Not run (needs the real camera):** anything about a real face. Everything here is synthetic.

## Step 6a findings and verification record (2026-11-05 / 06): system-audio capture probe
**Scope.** Measure only: no rendering, no camera, nothing muted, no audio stored. **The probe has never been run against a real tap**: creating one triggers the system-audio permission prompt on Sanjai's screen and the report has to be a real, filled-in one from his run (exact steps: docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP6A.md). Nothing here changes any sign default, and the tracker is still not connected to the renderer. Design, header quotations and open questions are in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md ("System audio capture probe").

**CORRECTION (Step 6a fix commit).** The commit message of `98f22d9` and the first version of this record said a surviving mutant, C12 (the guard `p->bytesPerSample != 0` removed from the frame calculation in the C callback), was an "**equivalent mutant**" because arm64 returns 0 for an integer division by zero. **That claim was wrong.** The code is C: a division by zero there is undefined behaviour, not a defined result that happens to match, and "returns 0 on this chip" is not a reason to call a mutant equivalent. C12 was a SURVIVOR, a test gap. Looking at it also showed that the original guard was **incomplete**: it checked only `bytesPerSample`, but the divisor was the 32-bit product `bytesPerSample * channels`, and the channel count comes from the HAL: `4 * 0x40000000` wraps to 0 modulo 2^32. The new `scripts/ubsan-tapprobe.sh` (clang `-fsanitize=integer-divide-by-zero -fno-sanitize-recover=integer-divide-by-zero`) was run against the unfixed callback BEFORE the fix and FAILED: `CTapProbe.c:66:45: runtime error: division by zero`, program aborted, script exit code 134. The fix (below) makes it pass (exit 0).

**What the SDK headers say (macOS 27.2 SDK, read before any code).**
- `AudioHardwareCreateProcessTap` and `AudioHardwareDestroyProcessTap` are `API_AVAILABLE(macos(14.2))`: **the minimum macOS from the headers is 14.2**. The tap properties, the tap-list property and the aggregate-device tap keys have no availability annotation. **This Mac runs 27.2 and cannot test 14.2 against 14.4.** Apple's sample page says 14.2 in its text and 26.0 in its metadata and is not used for the version.
- `NSAudioCaptureUsageDescription` is NOT in the CoreAudio, Foundation, AVFAudio or AVFoundation headers; its only source is Apple's sample page, which also says the prompt appears "the first time you start recording from an aggregate device that contains a tap". UNVERIFIED until the run.
- `AudioDeviceIOProc` is `CA_REALTIME_API` = `__attribute__((nonblocking))` with this clang; `AudioHardwareDestroyAggregateDevice` is asynchronous ("may take place after the call to this routine has returned"); `kAudioAggregateDeviceTapAutoStartKey` makes `AudioDeviceStart` wait for audio (not used); a private aggregate device is not persistent across launches of its creator.
- Swift spellings were checked with `swiftc -typecheck` only (nothing executed).

**What was observed on this Mac, read-only (no tap, no permission involved).** `--list-audio-devices` (the real Core Audio list reads): device list read status 0, tap list read status 0, 4 devices, 0 aggregate devices, 0 taps. `AudioGetHostClockFrequency()` returned 24,000,000 ticks per second (seen in the header of a SIMULATED report; the call itself is real). One observation each, on macOS 27.2.

**Verification record.**
- `swift build` (debug and release, no warnings); `swift test` **505 tests** (408 Core + 57 Engine + 40 Tracking; two opt-in tests skipped by default); the same under `swift test --sanitize=thread` with no race reports.
- **Core tests** (all with a scriptable fake backend, no Core Audio): status names (only the header's, never invented), FourCC, format descriptions, aggregator arithmetic (exact intervals, per-second buckets, RMS, dBFS, silence versus unknown levels), and the sequencer: success order, **a failure injected at every step** (create tap, read UID, read format, allocate, create aggregate, create IO proc, start) unwinds everything created in reverse order and never starts, a failed destroy does not skip the next one, a destroy status alone makes the teardown not clean, the asynchronous destroy is polled until the objects leave the lists (bounded by a fake clock, so no verdict depends on real time), refusal creates nothing, signal stop, no audio process object, silence and "no callback" are said out loud.
- **Engine tests**: the C callback on synthetic AudioBufferLists (non-interleaved and interleaved frames, NaN / infinity flagged and excluded, NULL buffer, non-float and 64-bit-float formats flagged not guessed, empty lists, overflow counted not overwritten, publish order), the simulated backend through the real sequencer (exact synthetic timeline, sine level, silence, SInt16 and interleaved formats, deterministic), and one threaded test that waits on a completion condition (5 callbacks seen), never on a fixed sleep.
- **Real-time checks, each shown to fail on a broken variant.** (1) Compile time: `-Werror=function-effects` rejects `malloc` and `free` inside the callback (shown, then restored). (2) **Release-disassembly scan**: `scripts/scan-rt-callback.sh` on the shipped release object: `_SATapProbeIOProc`: 82 instructions after the fix (83 before it), 0 calls, 0 branches leaving the function. On deliberately broken variants (compiled with the compile-time check off): `malloc` in the callback FAILS (1 call), a mutex lock/unlock FAILS (2 calls), a 512-byte struct copy that compiles to a hidden `memcpy` call FAILS (1 call); an unmodified control passes. One attempted variant (a 4 KB `memset` of an otherwise unused buffer) PASSED the scan because clang deleted it as dead code: that variant was not actually broken at machine-code level, so it is not counted as evidence. (3) **ThreadSanitizer on the C target** (`scripts/tsan-tapprobe.sh`: writer calling the real callback while a reader reads published records; 200,000 records, 1,000 overflow drops): clean on the real code; **a data race is reported on all three broken variants** (publish before writing; relaxed publish store; relaxed consumer load). The relaxed-publish variant produced no torn record at all (failures = 0) and was still flagged, i.e. TSan sees the missing happens-before that the data checks cannot. All six broken variants (three for the scan, three for TSan) were regenerated from the FIXED source and fail again, and the control passes. (4) **UBSan on the frame arithmetic** (`scripts/ubsan-tapprobe.sh`, hostile channel counts incl. the wrapping one): FAILED on the unfixed callback (exit 134), passes on the fixed one (exit 0); it also FAILS (exit 134) on the variant with the 32-bit product restored and on the variant where creation accepts a zero sample size again.
- **Mutation check (corrected).** History: the first run of 56 mutants killed 53 by `swift test` and three survived: R08 (teardown reported clean whatever the destroy statuses; my fake kept a failed-to-destroy object in the lists, so the lists alone made it "not clean"), R17 (a failed start still counted as started, so stop was called; my test skipped its no-stop assertion for the `start` step) and C12 (the zero-divisor guard removed; mislabelled "equivalent", see the correction above). R08 and R17 were fixed with tests and then killed. **C12 was never killed by any test and was wrongly dropped from the count.** After the fix the harness has **58 mutants**: the 55 earlier ones (with C01 re-targeted to the new 64-bit line) plus three replacements for C12, each with its killer:
  - **C12a** (creation accepts a zero sample size again): killed by the C-level test `aZeroSampleSizeIsRefusedAtCreationSoTheCallbackNeverDividesByZero`; also fails `scripts/ubsan-tapprobe.sh` (exit 134), because the callback no longer guards.
  - **C12b** (the runner no longer refuses a tap format with no whole-byte sample size): killed by the Core test `aTapFormatWithoutAWholeByteSampleSizeIsRefusedWithItsRealCauseBeforeAnythingIsAllocated` (two cases: 0 and 4 bits per channel).
  - **C12c** (the 64-bit divisor changed back to a 32-bit product): **survives `swift test` (no test fails; shown, not assumed) and is killed by `scripts/ubsan-tapprobe.sh` alone (exit 134).** There is deliberately NO Swift test for the wrapped channel count: one was written (`aChannelCountThatWrapsA32BitProductIsHandled`), passed on both versions on arm64 because it could not fail there, and was deleted in a cleanup commit. The UBSan script is the real check (the test counts above are after that deletion: 505, 57 in Engine).
  **Final run on the fixed code: 58 mutants; 57 killed by `swift test`; 1 (C12c) survives `swift test` and is killed by the UBSan script; no survivor without a killer within these 58; nothing in them is labelled equivalent.** (K12 from Step 2e, dropped from the Step 2f harness, is a survivor outside these 58: see below.) All mutated files were byte-identical afterwards. Coverage: aggregator (13), status / format / report (8), sequencer (17), C callback and creation (11 + the three C12 replacements), Engine pieces (6). **K12 re-checked (Step 2f mutant, a later cleanup).** The mutation is `if let culprit = flagged.max(by:` becoming `if d <= c.tooFarFraction * target, let culprit = flagged.max(by:` (SelfTestCoach.swift, after the early return `if d > c.tooFarFraction * target { return .tooFar }`). Re-run against the committed tests: **SURVIVED.** My earlier statements that it is "equivalent" / "holds by construction" were wrong: after the early return `d <= tooFarFraction * target` is true for every `d` except NaN, and `d` CAN be NaN: `median` filters non-finite inputs but the even-count median `(s[n/2-1] + s[n/2]) / 2` can overflow to infinity, and `inf - inf` is NaN. A temporary test (not kept) with yaw angles of 2e306 rad (x57.3 stays finite, the sum of the middle two overflows) and a 100 degree pitch gave `dev = nan 100.0 0.0`: the real code answers `otherAxisMoving`, the mutant `tooSmall`, and the test killed K12. So K12 differs from the real code on exactly one class of input, a NaN deviation, which Vision angles cannot produce; it is a SURVIVOR, not an equivalent. A cleaner fix than pinning that input would be to treat a non-finite deviation as "no face" in the coach; not done (proposed only).
- **Simulated end-to-end run of the real app bundle** (`open -n -W --stdout ... build/SpatialAudio.app --args --tap-probe-simulate 3 --results-dir=<scratch>`): no window, a 3 s run, JSON and markdown written, file names ending in `-SIMULATED` (ignored by git), the report banner says SIMULATED SOURCE, teardown verified through the (fake) lists. This tested the launch path and the report writing only. The simulated files stayed in a scratch folder.
