**Final run: 25 mutants, 25 killed, 0 survived, 0 did not compile (the IDs skip R24: it was never defined, see "Step 6b-2 real runs", Open items). No mutant is labelled equivalent** (no equivalence claim was needed or made; the one survivor of the first run, R10, was shown NOT equivalent).
- **R26a and R26b (run after the final 25-mutant run; added 2026-11-08, written down at 20:20:02 before they ran: the scratch note's file time; **an earlier version of this line, the description string in `scripts/mutate_ringprobe.py` and the message of the pushed commit `b9300c7` say "20:02", which was my misreading of 20:20:02**).** They are the ticker wake-priority mutant that the never-defined R24 was sketched to be (see "Step 6b-2 real runs", Open items a). R26a: an interrupt is returned before a stop request. R26b: a tick is returned before a stop request or an interrupt. **`aStopRequestBeatsAnInterrupt...` kills R26a (and only that test did) and does NOT kill R26b: R26b SURVIVED it, in two runs.** Analysis: not equivalent (with a tick pending the mutant returns `.tick` where the protocol promises `.signal` or `.interrupt`); the test could never have a tick pending next to another event (part 1 starts no timer, part 2 posts an interrupt immediately after starting a 1 ms timer, so whether a tick is pending when `wait()` runs is a race the mutant wins). Fix: `DispatchRingTicker.post(...)` made internal (visibility only) and `everyCombinationOfPendingEventsIsDeliveredInPriorityOrderWithoutATimer`, which posts exact combinations directly. After it: R26a killed (2 tests), R26b killed (1 test), R08 still killed; 640 tests pass, the ticker and ring-probe suites pass under TSan. Commit `b9300c7`. In the run, a stop request behind a pending tick would cost one extra sample before the run stops (the runner also reads the engine's configuration-change count after every wake), so R26b is a protocol violation of small practical effect; it is still a bug the tests must catch.
- **The first-run survivor, R10, in full.** With the pre-start check removed, a tampered volume of 0.5 still ended with the error thrown and `isRunning == false`, because the check AFTER the start threw the same error and stopped the engine again; my test could not tell "never started" from "started and stopped at once". On a real-time engine the second would start the output at a non-zero volume for an instant, which is exactly what the pre-start check exists to prevent. Fix: `startAttempts`, asserted at 0 after a refused start and 1 after a good one. The mutant's match text in `scripts/mutate_ringprobe.py` changed for the new line (its meaning did not).
- **R07 under ASan, quantified.** The ASan build of `CSystemAudioRing` is instrumented (10 ASan symbols in its object). With R07 applied and the real-time simulated test run 10 times under `--sanitize=address`: **10 of 10 runs failed; ASan itself reported a heap-use-after-free in 3, the event-order assertion in the other 7** (when ASan aborts the process the assertion output is lost). ASan alone is therefore a lottery here (the threads wake every ~10.7 ms and the free-to-join window is short); the order assertion is the dependable detector.

### Known limitations of Step 6b-2 (stated, not measured)
- **Everything about the real tap, the real-time engine, Bluetooth, the engine's real pull sizes, the order of the two blocks and whether the sample time stays valid under the output's rate conversion is UNVERIFIED.** Whether a real-time engine pulls source nodes at gain 0 is the first thing the first run answers (`calls` 0 = INVALID). AVAudioEngine starting from an `.accessory` app whose main thread is blocked inside `App.init` (as the 6a probe is) with no run loop spinning has not been run. **[Update 2026-11-08, after the real runs, measured on one Mac: the real tap was created and its format read in all six runs; the engine DID start from the `.accessory` app inside `App.init` in all six (that is how the probe runs; nothing more is known about run-loop effects); the real-time engine pulled both source nodes at gain 0 in all six; the pull size was 512 frames on the 48 kHz output and 558 on the 44.1 kHz output; the sample time was valid on every call, also with the rate conversion; the two blocks were called equally often with no phase mismatch. Still UNVERIFIED: the permission prompt (whether one appeared in any run, and its text, were never recorded), the order of the two blocks within a cycle, Bluetooth beyond one output device, sleep / wake, device changes.]**
- **frameCount per call (min / median / max) is not observable.** The ring keeps `calls` and `framesRequested`; the report gives per-second means and says so. The true per-call distribution needs a min / max counter in `CSystemAudioRing`, which would void the 6b-1 verification. **Decision (Sanjai, 2026-11-08): no counter now; revisit it in the recovery step, which changes the ring anyway.**
- **Count-only ring:** an underrun leaves a reader starved at equal rates; a reader lapped at the start stays lapped. **If the engine takes longer to start than the ring's slack (the serve window 16,384 frames minus the prime depth 2,048 is 14,336 frames, about 299 ms at 48 kHz, arithmetic not a measurement), every block of the run can be an overrun.** `prepare()` before the prime shortens that gap; the report records the measured start-to-first-pull time and the fill at the first pull. T and N were not tuned.
- Whether the host-tick clock stops during sleep is not stated in the headers read; both clocks are recorded and flagged separately; sleep and wake were not exercised. The 3 s gap, the 0.1 % rate-change threshold, the 10 s prime wait and the 5 s first-pull wait are named guesses or hang guards, not measurements.
- The runner has no iteration bound of its own: a runner that never advanced measured time would loop (R08b showed this on a virtual clock; on hardware the timer bounds it).
- `SystemRingBackend` repeats the 6a chain calls instead of sharing them, so it can drift from `SystemTapBackend`. A change of the tap's own format during a run is not detected. The default-output-device change is checked once per sample. A text scan guards the render APIs; it knows only the APIs it lists.
- The gate from 6b-1 stands: no listening build, no output gain above 0, no audible use of the ring until the recovery design is implemented and mutation-checked.

**Not run (at the first commit).** Anything with a real tap, a real-time engine, a device, a permission prompt, sleep / wake or a rate change: the whole of docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP6B2.md. **Since then:** the six real runs below; still not run: sleep / wake, a device or rate change during a run, protected content, the denied permission path, a muted tap, anything audible, and manual Run 2 (Bluetooth, 600 s).

## Step 6b-2 real runs (2026-11-08, one Mac, macOS 27.2)
**Scope and honesty.** Six real (non-SIMULATED) `--ring-probe` reports of one session on one MacBook Air M4 (Mac16,12), macOS 27.2: three on a Bluetooth output (44.1 kHz) and three on the built-in speakers (48 kHz; 120 s, 60 s and 600 s). The first five were committed first (`e9661ee`, `e182181`) and the sixth, `201826` (600 s), later in a commit of its own; all exactly as the app wrote them; **every number below was read from those committed JSON files, not typed from a message.** All six used the same app bundle (`build/SpatialAudio.app`, binary and Info.plist dated 15:55:29, built when HEAD was `beb7598`; not rebuilt in between; the code of `77d4759`, `d992849` and `beb7598` is identical). Tags: **[measured]** = read from a report, **[derived]** = arithmetic on measured numbers, **[hypothesis, UNVERIFIED]** = an explanation nobody has tested. The probe never played anything (output gain 0, tap unmuted) and records counts and timing, not levels. Manual Run 2 (Bluetooth, 600 s) was **not** run; the Bluetooth runs are three of 120 s. Manual Run 1 is `193455`, Run 3 is `195133`; `201826` is an extra 600 s speakers run, not one of the manual's runs.

| Report (created) | Output | Span (s) | Outcome | Engine start to first pull (s) | Fill at first pull (frames) | Mean frames per call | Readers' frames / host s (L = R) | Producer frames / host s | Fill slope, frames/s (ppm of 48 kHz) | Underrun blocks (L = R) | Devices before / during / after | First underrun, derived upper bound (measured 1 Hz) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `160334` (16:01:31) | Bluetooth 44.1 kHz | 120.000 | ok | 0.130 | 7634 | 558.00 | 48062.33 | 47999.93 | -62.276 (-1297.4) | 147 | 6 / 8 / 7 | at or before 118.3 s (115.0 s) |
| `160349` (16:01:49) | Bluetooth 44.1 kHz | 120.001 | ok | 0.052 | 4050 | 558.00 | 48062.18 | 47999.78 | -62.474 (-1301.5) | 5202 | 6 / 8 / 7 | at or before 59.6 s (56.0 s) |
| `160609` (16:04:08) | Bluetooth 44.1 kHz | 120.000 | ok | 0.095 | 5586 | 558.00 | 48062.20 | 48004.07 | -62.612 (-1304.4) | 2537 | 6 / 8 / 7 | at or before 90.6 s (87.0 s) |
| `193455` (19:32:55) | built-in 48 kHz | 120.001 | ok | 0.038 | 3072 | 512.00 | 47999.72 | 47999.72 | 0.000 (0.0) | 0 | 4 / 6 / 5 | none |
| `195133` (19:50:33) | built-in 48 kHz | 60.001 | ok | 0.040 | 3584 | 512.00 | 47999.60 | 47999.60 | +0.406 (+8.5) | 0 | 4 / 6 / 5 | none |
| `201826` (20:08:26) | built-in 48 kHz | 600.001 | ok | 0.020 | 2560 | 512.00 | 47999.94 | 47999.94 | -0.517 (-10.8) | 0 | 4 / 6 / 5 | none |

Last column: the "derived upper bound" is served blocks divided by calls per host second (10,190 / 86.13, 5,135 / 86.13, 7,800 / 86.13 on Bluetooth). **It is an upper bound, not the time of the first underrun**: blocks are still served after the first underrun while the fill hovers near one block (the served count was 9,905 at the first sample with underruns and 10,190 at the end in `160334`; 4,823 and 5,135 in `160349`; 7,492 and 7,800 in `160609`). The figure in parentheses is **measured**: the first 1 Hz sample whose underrun counter is above 0, which came 3 to 4 s after the first sample with a fill below 512 frames (111.0, 53.0 and 84.0 s).

**Same in all six runs [measured]:** outcome ok, stopped by timeout; output volume read back 0.000 before the start, after the start and at the end; no configuration-change notification; no flag; teardown clean (both lists agree: our aggregate device and tap are gone); both readers called on every cycle with identical call counts (10,337 on Bluetooth, 11,251, 5,626 and 56,251 on speakers; largest left-right difference in any snapshot 0); a valid sample time on 100 % of calls (also on the 44.1 kHz output); phase self-changes, phase mismatches and weak-rule violations 0; no overrun, torn, oversize or bad-buffer block; no rejected commit; this process's own audio process object (excluded from the tap) read the same before the tap existed and after the engine started in all six runs (`160334`: 151 and 151, `160349`: 152 and 152, `160609`: 152 and 152, `193455`: 142 and 142, `195133`: 142 and 142, `201826`: 143 and 143).

### Findings
1. **The late-engine-start risk did not occur [measured].** Engine start to both readers called was 0.020 to 0.130 s; the fill at the first pull was 2,560 to 7,634 frames, never near the 16,384-frame serve window (the worst case used about 47 % of it [derived]); no overrun in any run. The slack, (16,384 - 2,048) / 48,000 = about 299 ms [derived], was never close to used. Six runs on one Mac say nothing about a slower start.
2. **Built-in speakers, 48 kHz source to 48 kHz output [measured]:** 512 frames per call (512.00 in every interval), 93.75 calls per second, no underrun and no overrun in 780 s of runs (120 s + 60 s + 600 s). **No drift is resolved.** The producer and the readers agree within the endpoint resolution of the report (+/- 177.8 ppm over 120 s, +/- 355.6 ppm over 60 s). The fill moves only in 512-frame steps (min 3,072, max 3,584 in both runs). **The per-minute slopes (4.4 and 4.4 ppm; 4.4 ppm in the 60 s run) and the whole-run slope of `195133` (+8.5 ppm) are NOT drift measurements:** the fill series repeats with a period of 4 samples (3072, 3072, 3584, 3072 in `193455`; 3584, 3072, 3584, 3584 in `195133`, for as long as I looked, 16 samples) [measured], which is what 93.75 commits and 93.75 calls per second (375 / 4 per second) sampled at 1 Hz give with zero drift [derived]. **Resolution of the fill slope [derived]:** one 512-frame step over 120 s is 512 / (48,000 x 120) = 89 ppm, and a single step in the middle of a window moves the least-squares slope by about 133 ppm; so a drift smaller than tens of ppm can hide inside the quantization. (A brief for this record gave "about +/- 8 ppm over 120 s" and "about 1 ppm for 600 s"; those are the size of the slope the repeating pattern itself produces, not a detection limit, and I did not copy them: a 600 s run gives 512 / (48,000 x 600) = 17.8 ppm per step [derived].)
3. **Bluetooth, 48 kHz source to 44.1 kHz output [measured]:** every call asks for 558 frames (558.00 in every interval, a mean) at 86.13 calls per second; the engine's output node ran at 44,100 Hz (the device's actual rate read 44,100.021 Hz in all three runs). **[derived]** 558 = ceil(512 x 48,000 / 44,100) = ceil(557.28), and 558 / 557.279 - 1 = +1,294 ppm by rounding alone; the three fill slopes (-1,297.4, -1,301.5, -1,304.4 ppm) leave 3.0, 7.1 and 10.0 ppm beyond it, and the readers' 48,062.2 to 48,062.3 frames per host second is 86.133 cycles per second x 558 [derived]. The output device's own rate reading does NOT show an offset of that size. **[Hypothesis, UNVERIFIED]:** whether the mixer really consumes all 558 frames per cycle decides whether the ring truly drains at 62 frames per second or this is only how the requests are counted; to be settled in Step 6b-2b (named in the brief of this record; its scope is not written anywhere yet; **the offline attempt could not settle it, see "Step 6b-2b offline experiment"**). **[measured]** consequence in the count-only ring: each Bluetooth run drained from its start fill and starved once the fill fell below one block (fill at the end 146, -3,438, -1,390; underrun blocks 147, 5,202, 2,537).
4. **Overlap [derived from the created times and file times]:** `160334` (16:01:31, written 16:03:34) and `160349` (16:01:49, written 16:03:49) overlapped for about 105 s (16:01:49 to about 16:03:34): two probes, two taps, two engines at once on one Bluetooth output. `160609` ran alone. **[measured]** The three slopes agree within 7.0 ppm, so the overlap does not show in the rate; the different start fills (7,634, 4,050, 5,586) account for the different underrun counts.
5. **Device list [measured, in-process lists read before, while running and after teardown]:** Step 6a (no engine; the two committed 6a reports) read 4 / 5 / 4 and 6 / 7 / 6 (+1 while running, +0 after); the Step 6b-2 runs read 6 / 8 / 7 (Bluetooth) and 4 / 6 / 5 (speakers): +2 while running and **+1 still listed after the engine was stopped and detached** and the aggregate device and tap were destroyed (ours are reported gone). A fresh `--list-audio-devices` process after `193455` saw 4 devices, 0 aggregate devices, 0 taps, i.e. the original count; it was not repeated after the Bluetooth runs. **Cause unknown. [Hypothesis, UNVERIFIED]:** a per-process device created for the engine.
6. **Operator notes, as stated by Sanjai:** for Run 3 (`195133`) only: "I didn't play any song or audio at the time." For the other five runs (`160334`, `160349`, `160609`, `193455`, `201826`), **not recorded**: what was playing, any glitch of the original audio, the permission prompt text, settings touched, whether the headphone sound changed. Nothing is inferred for them. **No report records whether a permission prompt appeared; the tap was created with status 0 in all six runs, so permission was in place or was granted without it showing in a report, and the prompt text has not been recorded for any run to date (6a or 6b-2).** The reports hold no audio level, so they neither show nor contradict the statement for Run 3. For `193455` (the other speakers run) no operator statement exists in this record, and the report cannot say what was playing.
7. **Not shown by these runs:** sound; latency; CPU; ring content (the ring is count-only and the probe never inspects samples); per-call frame counts (only per-interval means; a counter is deferred to the recovery step by Sanjai's decision of 2026-11-08); drift at ppm level (see findings 2 and 8; an optional 600 s speakers run would resolve about 18 ppm per 512-frame step [derived]); anything about sleep / wake, device or rate changes during a run, protected content, the denied permission path, or the real-time-safe source-node initializer.
8. **600 s on the built-in speakers (`201826`, created 20:08:26) [measured]:** outcome ok, stopped by timeout, measured span 600.001 s; 56,251 calls on each reader (93.75 per second, 512 frames per call in every interval), no underrun, overrun, torn, oversize or bad-buffer block, a valid sample time on every call, lockstep as in the other runs (left and right identical at every one of the 601 samples), output volume 0.000 three times, teardown clean, devices 4 / 6 / 5. **The fill stayed in one 512-frame band:** over the 601 samples it took only two values, 2,560 (362 samples) and 2,048 (239 samples); start 2,560, end 2,560, min 2,048, max 2,560. Producer and readers both ran at 47,999.94 frames per host second (-1.2 ppm against the declared rate; the report's endpoint-ratio resolution is +/- 35.6 ppm, two commits, a different quantity from the next one). **[derived]** The fill is quantized to 512 frames and ended where it began, so the net rate difference between the producer and the readers over the 600 s is smaller than about 18 ppm (one 512-frame step over 600 s = 17.8 ppm); nothing larger is resolved and nothing is shown. **The whole-run slope (-0.517 frames/s, -10.8 ppm) and the ten per-minute slopes (-64.0, 4.4, 4.4, -5.3, -46.8, -25.5, -6.8, 6.8, -66.1, -19.6 ppm; spread 72.9 ppm) are what single 512-frame steps produce inside a window, not a drift measurement.** Engine start to first pull 0.020 s, fill at the first pull 2,560. **Operator notes for `201826`: not recorded** (what was playing, glitches, settings touched; nothing is inferred). **Load: CPU load during this run is unknown.** A brief for this record said the run overlapped my mutation and TSan runs; **the timestamps say it did not:** the run lasted 20:08:26 to about 20:18:26, and the scratch note that started my follow-up (the R26 experiment) was written at 20:20:02, with all of its mutation, full-suite and TSan runs after that (output files 20:20:26 and later). The only command of mine near the window was the push of `0ddbe52` (committed 20:03:20; the push time is not recorded). What else ran on the Mac during the 600 s is not known.

### Open items
- **a. What was mutation R24? ## Step 6b-2b offline experiment (2026-11-08): does the mixer consume what it pulls? OFFLINE, one Mac, UNVERIFIED for the device path
**Scope.** Code `4e013f5` (tests and analyzer), this record and `scripts/mutate_resampling.py` in a separate commit. **An AVAudioEngine in OFFLINE manual-rendering mode only: no device, no real-time engine, no Bluetooth.** Tags: **[measured offline]** = printed by the opt-in table (`SA_RESAMPLING_TABLE=1 swift test --filter ResamplingConsumptionTable`) on this Mac, 2026-11-08, K = 2000 renders per case, 20 warm-up renders excluded; **[derived]** = arithmetic on those; **[hypothesis, UNVERIFIED]**. No sound, latency or CPU claim; no report file in `docs/test-results`. Rules kept: new files only (no change to `CSystemAudioRing`, `SASourceNode.m`, the scans, the allow-list, `RingProbeEngine` or any existing test); no new C function and no new call in the render block; output gain above the mixer's own default is set only by a guard that refuses any engine not in manual-rendering mode (tested); single-threaded (produce, render, snapshot, repeat), no sleeps, timers, threads or wall-clock verdicts; only the case-1 controls are asserted.

**Method.** A producer writes the ramp Float(j) x 2^-20 (j = source frame) through the real `SARingIOProc` into the real ring (N = 32768; 512-frame commits until the left reader's fill is at least 4096); two real source nodes at 48 kHz (node 1 muted) feed the main mixer of an offline engine at the output rate; each render yields the left reader's pulled frames and calls (deltas of its counters), its cursor and the left output samples. The analyzer fits (sample x 2^20) against the output index (source frames per output frame times the gain G) and builds the gap g_k = cursor_k - last output sample x 2^20 / G (source frames pulled but not yet played). **Plan, tolerances and predictions were written to scratch files before any code or run** (file time 21:02:15; the plan quotes them): control tolerance = 4 x the quantization step the analyzer derives from the data (0.25 source frames at G = 1 while the ramp stays below 2^20 frames); "near" bands for labelling only: +/- 50 ppm of a hypothesis' ratio deviation and +/- 0.05 frames per render of its gap slope. The mutant list M01-M12 was written at 21:02:15 too, M13-M15 at 21:08:23, all before any mutant ran (the first mutant run started 21:10:23).

**Two things the first table run showed (reported, not hidden).** The first run used the plan's "G measured in case 1 and reused". (1) **That assumption is false:** cases 2-4 all gave slope x G = 0.769650666 = ideal x 0.7071068; case 5 gave 3.000046892 for an ideal 3. So the printed "ratio deviations" of run 1 for cases 2-4 (-292,883.45 ppm) were a gain error, not resampling behaviour (run 1's output is kept in the scratch directory and reproduced as table [A] below). (2) **Prediction P-pull was violated** (below). **Post-hoc change, written down before run 2 (plan addendum, 21:08:23):** G is measured per case with a constant source (the producer writes 0.25; output mean / 0.25; assumes a unity-DC-gain converter, which the numbers below show is true only to about 14 ppm), and a control was added that the DC-derived and ramp-derived G of the control agree within 1e-6 (tolerance written before run 2; it holds). No tolerance of the original plan was changed. The table's [B] analysis uses G per case.

### The table (K = 2000; every number [measured offline] unless marked)
| Case | Source -> output, render | Pull per render (distinct values) | Pull mean vs ideal (derived) | Calls per render | G per case (constant source) | Slope x G | Measured ratio vs ideal, deviation [B] | Gap slope per render [B] (ppm of pulled) | Gap peak-to-peak [B] | Run 1 deviation with G from case 1 [A] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (control) | 48000 -> 48000, 512 | 512 x 1980 | 512.0000 vs 512 | 1 | 1.000000000 | 1.000000000 | 1.000000000 vs 1.000000000, +0.00 ppm | +0.00000 (+0.0) | 0.000 (tolerance 0.25) | +0.00 ppm |
| 2 | 48000 -> 44100, 512 | **557 x 1428, 558 x 552** | 557.2788 vs 557.2789 | 1 | 0.707116547 | 0.769650666 | 1.088435378 vs 1.088435374, +0.00 ppm | -0.00000 (-0.0) | 17.869 (step 0.088) | -292,883.45 ppm |
| 3 | 48000 -> 44100, 441 | 480 x 1980 | 480.0000 vs 480 | 1 | 0.707116547 | 0.769650666 | 1.088435378 vs 1.088435374, +0.00 ppm | -0.00095 (-2.0) | 1.982 (step 0.088) | -292,883.45 ppm |
| 4 | 48000 -> 44100, 1024 | **1114 x 876, 1115 x 1104** | 1114.5576 vs 1114.5578 | 1 | 0.707116547 | 0.769650666 | 1.088435378 vs 1.088435374, +0.00 ppm | -0.00002 (-0.0) | 37.231 (step 0.177) | -292,883.45 ppm |
| 5 | 48000 -> 16000, 512 | 1536 x 1980 | 1536.0000 vs 1536 | 1 | 1.000015616 | 3.000046892 | 3.000000043 vs 3.000000000, +0.01 ppm | -0.00003 (-0.0) | 0.623 (step 0.250) | +15.63 ppm |

R^2 of the ramp fit is 0.99999999997 or better in every case. No underrun and no overrun in any case; the counters did not move after any `renderOffline` returned. Process threads: 11 before the engine, 12 after its start, 12 after the renders (case 1; 12 / 12 / 12 in the later cases, the extra thread already existing): reported, not asserted.

**Control results (case 1; all asserted, all held).** K = 2000 renders completed; every render pulled exactly 512 frames in exactly one call; the gap series was constant (peak-to-peak 0.000 against a tolerance of 0.25 source frames); no underrun; counters unchanged right after each render; a second identical run was identical sample for sample (pulls, calls, cursors, output); the DC-derived G (1.000000000) equals the ramp-derived G within 1e-6. What the control cannot assert: that the converter behaves the same when the ratio is not 1 (cases 2-5 are printed, not asserted), and which thread calls the render block (below).

### Predictions and which held
- **P-pull** (case 2 pulls exactly 558, case 3 exactly 480, case 4 exactly 1115, case 5 exactly 1536; a deviation would itself be a finding): **held for 3 and 5, VIOLATED for 2 and 4.** The offline engine pulled 557 or 558 (mean 557.2788, the ideal) in case 2 and 1114 or 1115 (mean 1114.5576, the ideal) in case 4. **The offline path differs from the device path**, where every call asked for exactly 558 (558.00 in every interval, Step 6b-2 real runs).
- **H1** (consumes all pulled frames: ratio deviation +1293.9 / +396.7 ppm in cases 2 / 4, gap flat) and **H2** (consumes the ideal ratio and keeps the surplus: deviation 0, gap +0.7211 / +0.4422 frames per render): **neither held in cases 2 and 4** (label H3 in the plan's terms). In cases 3 and 5 the surplus is 0, so H1 and H2 coincide there (no discriminating power; they served as controls of the method: the ratio deviation was +0.00 and +0.01 ppm, the gap flat).
- **The plan's assumption "G measured in case 1 is reused": false** (see above).
- **Post-hoc category, not a prediction that held (call it H4):** the offline mixer pulls the ideal count on average (a mix of floor and ceil), the measured ratio equals the ideal to 0.00 ppm, and the gap has zero slope: **it consumes what it pulls, and what it pulls is the ideal; there is no surplus to keep or consume.**

### Findings
1. **[measured offline] G is not a constant of the mixer.** 1.000000000 at 48 kHz and 1.000015616 at 16 kHz, but **0.707116547 at 44.1 kHz in all of cases 2, 3 and 4**, i.e. 1/sqrt 2 (0.707106781) within +13.8 ppm [derived], a -3 dB scaling. **I do not know why** the 44.1 kHz output is scaled and the 16 kHz one is not [hypothesis: none; not investigated]. The converter's DC gain is not exactly 1 either (+13.8 and +15.6 ppm [derived] relative to 1/sqrt 2 and 1); the analysis divides by the DC gain, and the ramp then gives ratio deviations of 0.00 and 0.01 ppm, so the ramp and the DC see the same gain.
2. **[measured offline] The offline mixer asks for exactly what it needs, on average:** case 2 mean 557.2788 (ideal 557.27891), case 4 mean 1114.5576 (ideal 1114.55782); one call per render. **[derived]** With a mean pull equal to the ideal, the surplus H2 needs (0.7211 / 0.4422 per render) is 0 in this engine, so **this experiment cannot tell whether a mixer consumes all of a constant 558: the offline engine never asks for one.**
3. **[measured offline] The gap has zero slope, but it is not constant:** peak-to-peak 17.9 (case 2) and 37.2 (case 4) source frames, 2.0 (case 3) and 0.6 (case 5), against quantization steps of 0.09 to 0.25. **[derived]** No tolerance was written for cases 2-5 (only the control is asserted); the oscillation is the converter's own buffering and the 557 / 558 alternation [hypothesis, UNVERIFIED: not investigated].
4. **[hypothesis, UNVERIFIED] For the device path:** the Step 6b-2 real runs show a constant 558 per call at 86.13 calls per second and a ring that drained 62 frames per second. This experiment gives NO evidence about whether the device-path mixer consumes all 558 (the drain would then be real) or consumes the ideal ratio and keeps the surplus, because its offline counterpart pulls a different (ideal-average) sequence. Why the two paths pull differently is not known.
5. **Thread, [measured offline] / header:** `AVAudioEngine.h` describes offline mode only as "without any realtime constraints" and does not say which thread calls a source block. Evidence collected, not proof: the counters never changed after `renderOffline` returned (2000 renders x 5 cases), a repeat run was identical, the process gained one thread at the engine start and none during the renders. **Which thread runs the block is UNVERIFIED; nothing contradicted "the calling thread".** If it had been another thread the plan was to stop and report.

### Mutation check (test-the-test; `python3 -I scripts/mutate_resampling.py`, detector `swift test --filter ResamplingConsumption`)
| Mutant | The bug | Killed by (tests that failed) | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| M01 | slope against the wrong axis (sxy / syy) | `anIdealResampler…`, `aMixerThatConsumesExactly…`, `aMixerThatKeepsTheSurplus…` (+1) | KILLED |
| M02 | warm-up not excluded (analyze) | `theWarmUpRendersAreExcluded…`, `pullStatistics…`, `inputThatCannotBeAnalyzed…` | KILLED |
| M03 | ideal ratio inverted | `anIdealResampler…`, `aMixerThatConsumesExactly…`, `aMixerThatKeepsTheSurplus…` (+1) | KILLED |
| M04 | gap against the cursor before the render | `aConstantGapIsReportedWithItsKnownValue…`, `anIdealResampler…`, `theWarmUpRendersAreExcluded…` | KILLED |
| M05 | gap not divided by G | `anIdealResamplerGivesTheIdealRatioAndAFlatGap` (synthetic G = 0.7071) | KILLED |
| M06 | mean pull per call, not per render | `pullStatisticsCountDistinctValuesAndCallsPerRenderNotPerCall` | KILLED |
| M07 | distinct-pull count one too low | `pullStatistics…`, `theWarmUpRendersAreExcluded…` | KILLED |
| M08 | gain guard removed | `theGainGuardRefusesAnyEngineThatIsNotInManualRenderingMode` | KILLED |
| M09 | ramp restarts at 0 on every commit | `theControlCaseKeepsEveryAssumptionOfTheHarness`, `theRampProducerWritesTheExactRamp…` | KILLED |
| M10 | the producer commits nothing before a render | `theControlCaseKeepsEveryAssumptionOfTheHarness` | KILLED |
| **M11** | **node 1 is not muted** | **none** | **SURVIVED** (predicted) |
| M12 | control built at the wrong output rate | `theControlCaseKeepsEveryAssumptionOfTheHarness` | KILLED |
| M13 | constantGain: warm-up not excluded | `theConstantGainIsTheMean…ExcludesTheWarmUp` | KILLED |
| M14 | constantGain: mean x constant, not / constant | `theConstantGainIsTheMean…`, `theControlCase…` | KILLED |
| M15 | the DC producer writes the ramp | `theControlCaseKeepsEveryAssumptionOfTheHarness` | KILLED |
**15 mutants: 14 killed, 1 survived, 0 did not compile; the tree was restored byte for byte after the run (compared against a backup). No mutant is labelled equivalent.** **M11 analysis:** with node 1 un-muted, both readers carry the identical ramp, so the output is exactly twice the ramp: run with M11 applied, the control's G is 2.000000000 (DC and ramp agree), every other quantity is unchanged (pulls 512 x 1980, gap flat, ratio 1.000000000) and the control tests pass. It is not equivalent code (the signal doubles), but it is undetectable by what the harness measures, because G is measured and absorbs it. It could only be caught by asserting G = 1, which the plan forbids (G must not be assumed). Consequence: the mute is an assumption of the design that no test checks here (an earlier, separate test of the ring probe did show that muting one node works). It does not affect any number in the table.

### Verification (this Mac, 2026-11-08)
`swift build` debug and release without diagnostics; `swift test` **653 tests** (511 Core + 102 Engine + 40 Tracking), all pass (640 before); the new tests (13) pass under `swift test --sanitize=thread` with no report. **TSan is weak evidence here:** the harness is single-threaded by design, so a clean run says only that nothing unexpected raced; I did not construct a broken variant that TSan would catch, so it is not counted as a check of anything. `scripts/scan-render-api-coverage.sh`: 120 source files, 0 violations (the new files name no render-block API). `git status` before the commit: no change to `Sources/CSystemAudioRing`, the scan scripts, `rt-block-allowlist.txt`, `RingProbeEngine` (or any Engine source) or any existing test. The opt-in table is skipped without its environment variable (checked).

### What was NOT run
The device path: no real-time engine, no Bluetooth, no tap; any run in which the mixer is asked for a constant 558 per 512 frames; `AVAudioEnvironmentNode` or any mixer other than the main mixer; other quality settings of the converter; K other than 2000; render sizes and rates beyond the five cases; a check of which thread calls the block; a broken variant for TSan; any sound, latency or CPU measurement; any report in `docs/test-results`. No recovery design is proposed (not asked).

## Sign confirmation by Sanjai (2026-11-09), from self-test run 215400
**What this records.** A statement by Sanjai about which way he moved in one real camera self-test, and what it does and does not settle. **No code and no default was changed by it.** Tags: **[measured]** = read from the report (`docs/test-results/selftest-20261109-215400-normal-light.{json,md}`, schema 3, **committed in `53d61a8`**, exactly as the app wrote it, after the privacy grep, on Sanjai's instruction; when this section was first written it was untracked); **[stated by Sanjai]** = his own words; **[derived]**.

**The run [measured].** Created 2026-11-09T21:54:00+05:30; real camera ("MacBook Air Camera", 640x480, Vision revision 3), normal light; frames mirrored 0 %, rotation 0°; **invert flags in effect during the test: yaw off, pitch off, roll off.** Signs: **yaw DETERMINED, raw Vision sign +, invert needed: no** (repetitions +37.6° with leakage 0.27 and +49.0° with 0.52); **pitch DETERMINED, raw Vision sign -, invert needed: no** (repetitions -19.1° with leakage 0.34 and -14.0° with 0.57); **roll INCONCLUSIVE** (only 0 of 2 repetitions usable: +2.3° and +3.5°, both below the required 10.0°). Loss test PASS (observed locked, holding, decaying, static, locked; largest step 5.47° against a 10.0° threshold) **but the jump check is WEAK** (the held pose was 4.3° from neutral, 10° needed). Camera release: programmatic check CLEAN. **Green-light question: NOT ANSWERED** (in the report). It is therefore **not a clean run** (roll inconclusive, green light unanswered, jump check weak).

**What Sanjai stated [stated by Sanjai].** In his message of 2026-11-09 21:59 (about five minutes after the run; I do not have that message itself: the wording below is taken from his instruction of the same day to record it): he answered "yes" to both questions: (1) in the "turn left" holds he turned toward his left-hand side, (2) in the "look up" holds he raised his chin toward the ceiling.

**What that settles [derived].** The report states its own limit: "Signs assume each move was performed in the instructed direction; the test cannot see which way you actually moved." For yaw and pitch that assumption is now met by his statement, so **yaw (raw Vision sign +, no invert) and pitch (raw Vision sign -, no invert) are confirmed by Sanjai from run 215400.** This is one run, one session, one camera, normal light, one person. The same raw signs were printed (not confirmed) in earlier real runs recorded above (yaw in the 08:12 run and the 14:58 run of 2026-11-05, pitch in the 14:58 run). **"No invert" means: the existing default mapping (all invert flags off, including its mirror compensation) is already correct for yaw and pitch, so there is nothing to apply and nothing was applied.**

**What it does NOT settle.**
- **Roll is NOT confirmed.** The report is INCONCLUSIVE for roll (movements of 2.3° and 3.5°), and Sanjai made no statement about the tilt holds. The roll sign default stays UNVERIFIED on hardware.
- It is not a clean run, and the green-light (camera light) answer is still missing.
- Nobody has listened to anything driven by these signs (the Step 3a listening test has not been run); mirroring was observed at 0 % only; no dim-room result is recorded here.
- **The tracker is still NOT connected to the renderer** (as instructed; checked in the code on 2026-11-09: the only non-test code that publishes to the pose mailbox is the scripted listening-test publisher, and the app's head-tracking model does not reference the renderer or the mailbox). The gate in CLAUDE.md ("the tracker is not connected to the renderer until Sanjai confirms the camera signs from a real self-test report") is met for yaw and pitch only; CLAUDE.md was not edited, and connecting is a later, explicit decision.

**Three other real self-test reports, `selftest-20261108-223638-normal-light`, `selftest-20261108-225905-dim-room` and `selftest-20261109-215116-normal-light` (the run before this one, 21:51), are committed together with this one in `53d61a8`** (when this section was first written they were untracked and unread by me; for the commit I read only their headers). They are not interpreted in this section; their green-light answers and camera-release checks are in "Camera-light observations by Sanjai (2026-11-09)".

## Frame-rate finding (2026-11-10): the camera's frame rate differs between sessions, cause UNKNOWN
**What this records.** The measured frame rates so far, and the two new readings by Sanjai. **No code changed.** Tags: **[measured]** = read from a report's JSON or counted in the code; **[stated by Sanjai]** = what he read from the HUD; **[derived]**; **[header]** = text of the macOS 27.2 SDK header `AVCaptureDevice.h`, read 2026-11-10; **[hypothesis, UNVERIFIED]**.

**New readings [stated by Sanjai], 2026-11-10 09:01, read from the HUD "Frame rate" row after "Start camera":** **Test A (as is): 25 / 25 / 25 fps at 5 s / 30 s / 60 s.** **Test B (bright light): 24.9 / 25 / 24.9 fps** at the same times. **Not answered for Test A: whether the charger was connected, whether Low Power Mode was on, and what the room light was** (NOT ANSWERED; for Test B only "bright light" was stated). There is no report file for these readings.

| Session | Source | Frame rate |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-05, Step 2 manual session | HUD screenshots (see "Measured values") | 30.0 fps |
| 2026-11-05 07:45, 08:12 and 14:58 self-tests; 2026-11-08 22:36 (normal light) and 22:59 (dim room) self-tests | [measured] `stats.frameIntervalMs` mean = p95 = max = 33.3 ms, 899 / 899 / 898 / 899 / 899 frames in the 30 s window (the 22:36 and 22:59 reports are committed in `53d61a8` and not otherwise recorded here) | 30 fps [derived: 1000 / 33.3] |
| 2026-11-09 21:51 and 21:54 (run 215400) self-tests | [measured] mean = p95 = max = 60.2 ms, 498 frames in the 30 s window; the report of run 215400 says "Mean frame rate: 16.6 fps" (both reports committed in `53d61a8`) | 16.6 fps [derived] |
| 2026-11-10 09:01, Test A (as is) | [stated by Sanjai] HUD at 5 / 30 / 60 s | 25 / 25 / 25 fps |
| 2026-11-10 09:01, Test B (bright light) | [stated by Sanjai] HUD at 5 / 30 / 60 s | 24.9 / 25 / 24.9 fps |

**Observations.**
1. **[measured / stated] The rate is not constant between sessions:** 30, 16.6 and 25 fps, on the same Mac, the same built-in camera ("MacBook Air Camera") and the same 640x480 preset. Within the sessions with a report the frame interval was constant to the report's precision (mean = p95 = max), so each session ran at a steady rate rather than dropping frames irregularly [derived].
2. **[derived] Bright light did not bring the rate back to 30:** Test B read 24.9 / 25 / 24.9 against 25 / 25 / 25 for Test A. That is one comparison in one sitting with the other conditions unanswered.
3. **[measured] All of these rates lie inside the 15 to 30 fps that AVFoundation reported for the built-in camera's formats when this file first recorded them (line 8, 640x480 included).**
4. **[measured] Our code never asks for a frame rate.** `CameraHeadPoseProvider` sets only `sessionPreset = .vga640x480`; no active format, no `activeVideoMinFrameDuration` / `activeVideoMaxFrameDuration`, no `autoVideoFrameRateEnabled` anywhere in `Sources/` (searched 2026-11-10).
5. **[header] A device property that lowers the frame rate by light level exists but does not match the numbers:** `autoVideoFrameRateEnabled` (macOS 15.0 and later; default false; resets to false when the active format changes) makes "the device automatically adjust the active frame rate, depending on light level", and for formats with a maximum of 30 fps it switches between 30 and 24 (60 / 30 / 24 for a 60 fps maximum). **[derived]** That is neither 25 nor 16.6, and we never enable it, so on its face it explains none of the readings. Whether the camera or the system slows capture by another automatic means is **UNKNOWN** (nothing I read in the header says).
6. **Candidate factors named by Sanjai, none tested: the charger, Low Power Mode and the room light. Cause: UNKNOWN [hypothesis, UNVERIFIED for each].** The 25 fps readings, the 16.6 fps session and the 30 fps sessions were not compared on any of them (not recorded for the earlier sessions either).
7. **[measured] A consequence in run 215400 (16.6 fps):** "frame rate" was the limiting quality component in 95 % of the frames of the statistics window (mean quality 0.71; the per-phase median qualities were 0.72), and in 100 % of the frames of several single phases. Whether quality was lower at 30 fps is not compared here, and the quality components are untuned.

**Not shown.** What changes the rate; whether tracking latency or the sign results depend on it (the sign results of run 215400 are recorded above and were taken at 16.6 fps); any measurement of power state or light level. **PLANNED for Step 5:** the read-only HUD line with the reported frame-rate range and the active frame duration (see "Step 5 plan additions"); the header was checked for the names, not yet for their macOS availability.

5. **After the self-tests of 21:51 and 21:54 he said the light went off** (which of the two he did not say). **The app recorded the green-light question as "not answered" for both** [measured: `selftest-20261109-215116` and `selftest-20261109-215400`], so his words are not in those reports.
6. **Run 225905 (2026-11-08 22:59, dim room) recorded the light as STILL ON** [measured: "Green light (visual, user-reported): NO — the light is STILL ON"], **while its programmatic release check, made 1.2 s after stop, was clean** (isRunning false, 0 inputs, 0 outputs, 0 callbacks after stop) [measured]. **Still UNEXPLAINED.** **[Hypothesis, UNVERIFIED, untested]: the answer was clicked before the light had gone off.**

**What the reports add [measured].** The programmatic release check was clean in all four reports committed in `53d61a8` (223638, 225905, 215116, 215400), made 1.3, 1.2, 1.3 and 1.3 s after stop. The green-light answers recorded by the app: 223638 "the light went OFF", 225905 "STILL ON", 215116 and 215400 not answered.

**Derived.** (a) The delays he reports for the light going off (about 1 s for Stop and window close, 1 s or less for window close alone, about 2 s for Cmd+Q) are of the same order as the 1.2 to 1.3 s at which the programmatic check is made, so an answer given within the first second or two after Stop could read "STILL ON" while the capture session was already stopped; that is what the hypothesis in item 6 says, and nothing here tests it (I found no timestamp of the answer among the report's top-level fields, only the answer itself). (b) The app ends its process when the last window closes (`applicationShouldTerminateAfterLastWindowClosed` returns true in the code), which fits `pgrep` printing nothing 5 s after a window close; the PID of item 4 is not explained by that.

**What this does and does not show.** The privacy rule is that the camera is released on stop and exit. The evidence is the clean programmatic check in four of four reports and his by-eye observations that the light went off within about 1 to 2 s after Stop, window close and Cmd+Q, with one contrary report (225905) that is unexplained. **Not shown:** any other Mac, macOS version or camera; another app holding the camera; a crash or `kill -9`; sleep; anything beyond one person's eyes (the light is a visual signal only). The earlier item in "Still missing" (camera light off after Stop / window close / Cmd+Q) is therefore partly answered, not closed: item 6 and item 4 stay open.

## Step 3a-2 findings and verification record (2026-11-10): the blind listening test replaces the typed Q&A
**Goal and exit.** Replace the typed question-and-answer of docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP3A.md with a guided, BLIND, in-app test that records the answers itself (the system collects the data, no typed forms). Exit: a card in the HUD runs the trials, Sanjai answers only with buttons, the app writes a numbers-only report (json + md) to docs/test-results/ and shows the path. **Status: IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED. Nobody has run it with ears, and the card has never been clicked.** **[Update 2026-11-15: it has since been run twice by Sanjai (2026-11-10, two real reports, mostly unanswered); see "Two real blind listening reports (2026-11-10)". Still IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED for sound.]** No camera, no tracker link, no ring, no microphone; default volume 20 % (UNTUNED) and the slider is fixed during a run.

**What was built (as designed by Sanjai, with the changes below)**
- 20 trials: static LEFT / RIGHT / AHEAD and head 45° LEFT / RIGHT with the tone fixed in the room, **4 of each**, in an order from a **recorded seed** (SplitMix64, written out in the code, so the order is reproducible on any machine; Swift's system RNG is not used), no condition three times in a row. Buttons: Left ear / Right ear / Middle / Can't tell. The expected position is not shown during a trial and is not even reachable: `BlindFrame` (everything the UI and the audio side can read) has no truth field (a `Mirror` test pins its field list), and `BlindRunner.results` is nil until the run is over.
- End-of-test buttons: clicks or crackling (yes / no), inside or outside the head, AirPods Spatial Audio (off / on / not using AirPods). Unanswered is recorded as "not answered", never as a default.
- Collected automatically: output device transport category, nominal rate, buffer size, output channel count (**no name, no UID**: the existing device reader returns a name; only the numeric fields are copied, and a test checks the JSON for names, UIDs and paths), pose-pump tick count / mean / longest period, `ProcessInfo.thermalState` and `isLowPowerModeEnabled` at the start and the end (**SDK headers checked first**: `thermalState` from macOS 10.10.3, `isLowPowerModeEnabled` from macOS 12.0; the package minimum is 14), start and end time, seed, volume, schema version 1.
- Report: per-trial truth and answer and reaction time, counts per condition, a 5 × 5 confusion table, chance stated as **1 in 3** per definite answer under uniform guessing among the three position buttons ("can't tell" is an abstention), an exact binomial tail as a rough guide, the channel-swap flag, and the settings the report was produced with. `--blind-listening-simulate` writes a `-SIMULATED` report from a made-up listener (never committed, decides nothing; `.gitignore` already covers the suffix).

**What I changed from the design, and why**
1. **The tone is faded out before the scene changes and in after it (80 ms), and the source and head move only while it is silent.** Without that, the tone's position would jump while audible; a position jump can click, and a click would contaminate the "clicks or crackling" question. Cost: the fade is a volume ramp on the main mixer from a 100 Hz timer (about 8 steps); whether it zippers audibly is **UNKNOWN**, and the report says that fade noise is part of the clicks answer.
2. **The answer buttons switch on 1.2 s after the onset for every condition** (after the head has stopped turning, and so that the delay leaks nothing about the condition).
3. **A second flag, "head-turn reversed"**, in addition to the channel-swap flag. The channel-swap flag uses only the static LEFT / RIGHT trials, because the head-turn answers also depend on the yaw convention; the second flag is assessed only if the static answers were mostly right (at least 6 definite, 75 % right), so a swap and a yaw error are not confused. If the ears are swapped, the head flag says "not assessable".
4. **Both flags are hints with UNTUNED thresholds** (at least 6 definite answers and at least 75 % in the opposite ear; chosen before any data existed; stated in the report). "Definite" = left ear / right ear / middle; "can't tell" and "not answered" never count.
5. **Chance is 1 in 3 only if the listener guesses uniformly among the three position buttons.** The trial mix is not uniform (middle is one condition in five), so the binomial tail is a rough guide, not a test; the report says so. 20 trials is a small sample.
6. **Truth is derived from geometry, not typed:** the relative azimuth of the tone at the end of the head turn, with a ±15° "middle" band (UNTUNED; the scenes used give the same truth for any band from 1° to 44°). It is also pinned against hand-derived values (head turned LEFT with the tone ahead = tone at the RIGHT).

**Run, and passed (this Mac, 2026-11-10)**
- `swift build` (debug and release) clean; `swift test`: **692 tests** = 544 Core + 40 Tracking + 108 Engine (was 653); 33 Core and 6 Engine tests are new. Known values come from an **independent Python implementation** (SplitMix64 outputs, three shuffled orders including one that needs a redraw because the first shuffle has a run of three, the binomial tails), not from the Swift code. No test uses a sleep or a wall-clock verdict: the runner takes the clock as an argument, the Engine tests pass virtual time to `controlTick(at:)`, and the session's clock is injectable. `scripts/scan-render-api-coverage.sh`: 126 files, 0 violations.
- A property test plays a whole 20-trial run at 1/120 s steps and checks that **the source never moves while the tone is audible** and that the head pose never steps more than 1° per step while audible (smoothstep peak is about 0.56° per step).
- **Mutation check: `scripts/mutate_blindlistening.py`, 22 mutants, 22 killed, 0 survived, 0 compile errors / patch errors**, each killed by named assertion failures (not by a compile error), files restored and byte-compared after each. The list S01-S06 (scoring), F01-F06 (flags), T01-T06 (plan and runner) was written to a timestamped scratch file **before the scoring and flag code existed** (09:35:45); T07 (scene switch before the fade-out ends) and E01, E04, E05 (Engine session) were appended **after the code and tests existed but before any mutant ran** (09:47:30). Two mutants (S02 and S05) had to be written in a different form than the list's one-line wording, because the literal change (making `matches` accept "can't tell") sits on a branch "can't tell" never reaches and would have been an equivalent mutant; they were rewritten before running.
- **Simulated rehearsal in the real app bundle** (`--blind-listening-simulate=swapped` and `=abstain`, `--mute`, results to a scratch directory, no audio, no camera): both exited 0 with empty stderr and wrote `-SIMULATED` reports; the `abstain` report was read (all five conditions 4 × "can't tell", both flags "not assessable", no device facts because nothing is measured in a rehearsal, no names or paths). That run found one wording bug in the report ("Sound location: not answered the head"), now fixed and pinned by a test. Two rehearsals in the same second share a file stem and overwrite each other; real runs are minutes apart.

**Not run, not known (UNVERIFIED)**
- Anything by ear: whether the left channel reaches your left ear, the HRTF, front/back, inside / outside the head, loudness (the 20 % default is a guess), whether the fades click or zipper.
- The live path of this session: the real `EnvironmentRenderer` driven by this session, the 100 Hz control timer's jitter, the pump timing during a run, the engine starting at volume 0 on the real output device (the old card did this once, silently), a device change mid-run (the model reports a stopped engine and discards the run), Bluetooth, sleep during a run (the clock does not count sleep, so a trial's 12 s timeout can span a long sleep in wall time).
- The card: its layout, the buttons, the end-of-test rows (a selected option is shown with a tint and an outline; never seen), **Save report** from a real click, the file landing in docs/test-results/ from a real run. `swift test --sanitize=thread --filter BlindListening` (39 tests) ran afterwards with no ThreadSanitizer report, but per the CLAUDE.md rule **that result does not count**: it was not shown to fail on a deliberately broken variant, and the Engine tests never start the real timers, so no cross-thread access of the session happens in them (the mailbox was not touched; in live use the producer calls come from the main thread before the timers start and then from the timer queue).
- The head-turn conventions at the audio side are those verified offline in Step 3a; the tracker is still **not connected to the renderer** (gate unchanged: yaw and pitch confirmed by Sanjai from run 215400, roll not, no clean run).
- Headphone make and model are not recorded by design (privacy; he can write them next to the file name).

**Still open from before (not part of this step):** 12 untracked tap-probe reports contain a device name and need a scrub before any commit; the 44.1 kHz offline run was never produced; camera-light run 225905 unexplained; the Step 5 HUD frame-rate line is PLANNED (availability not checked); the pre-release path scrub.

## Four more real camera self-test reports (2026-11-10), numbers read from the JSON
**What this records.** Four real self-test runs on the camera (`inputSource` camera, none simulated, no `-SIMULATED` in any name or text), made on 2026-11-10 at 09:25, 09:27, 10:06 and 15:34 (+05:30), committed on their own as `1215d83` exactly as the app wrote them (privacy grep with positive controls first: 0 hits in all 8 files for the host name, the account name, the full name, `/Users`, `ClaudeHome`, `@`, `.local`, AirPods / Beats / Bluetooth / UID; every positive control was found). Same machine for all four: Mac16,12, macOS 27.2 (26B123), built-in camera at 640×480, mapping flags all off, frames mirrored 0 %, normal light. **All figures below were read from the JSON files by me; Sanjai's own account of each run was compared with them (see "Comparison").** Tags: **[measured]** = in the report; **[derived]** = my arithmetic on report values; **[hypothesis, UNVERIFIED]**. Nothing here changes code, a default, or the gate: **no sign was applied, Sanjai has not confirmed anything from these four, and the tracker is still not connected to the renderer.**

**Real camera self-test runs so far: 11, none clean** (7 committed before + these 4; `git ls-files` lists 11 `selftest-*.json`). A run is not clean here because the green-light question was never answered in any of the four, roll was determined in none, and the loss test did not pass with a meaningful jump check in any of them (below). Roll has been determined in none of the 11 (the first seven per the records above; these four from the JSON).

| Run | yaw | pitch | roll | loss test | fps (mean) | frames in the 30 s window | mean quality | green light | release check |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 092516 | DETERMINED, raw + (rep 1 +46.5°, rep 2 +50.2°), invert not needed | INCONCLUSIVE (rep 1: yaw 17.8° vs pitch 15.7° on a pitch move; rep 2: reference window not steady, spread 4.7°) | INCONCLUSIVE (movement 4.4° and 3.5°, need 10°) | **PASS, weak**: observed locked → holding → decaying → static → locked as expected; the held pose was only 8.1° from neutral (need ≥ 10° for a meaningful jump check); max one-frame jump 5.9° | 30.0 | 900 | 0.738 | not answered | clean |
| 092706 | INCONCLUSIVE (movement 0.5° and 1.6°, need 15°) | INCONCLUSIVE (0.7° and 0.1°) | INCONCLUSIVE (0.2° and 0.9°) | **INCOMPLETE**: observed only `locked`; static never reached ("cover too short, or only partly covered?"); held pose 0.0° from neutral; max jump 0.5° | 30.0 | 900 | 1.000 | not answered | clean |
| 100655 | **LIKELY**, raw + (rep 1 +25.4°, rep 2 +31.6°), invert not needed, **never applied**; leakage failed in both reps (roll 43.4° vs yaw 25.4°; roll 29.9° vs yaw 31.6°) | DETERMINED, raw − (rep 1 −44.3°, rep 2 −41.8°), invert not needed | INCONCLUSIVE (movement 4.8° and 7.8°, need 10°) | **INCOMPLETE**: observed `locked, holding, decaying, locked, holding, decaying, static, locked`; notes "decaying lasted 0.08 s, configured 1.50 s" and "state flapped: holding was entered more than once (partial cover?)"; jump check meaningful (held pose 46.9° from neutral), max jump 6.2° (not flagged) | **25.97** | **779** | **0.599** | not answered | clean |
| 153425 | INCONCLUSIVE (rep 1: hold not steady, spread 18.1° vs 36.5°, and roll 28.5° vs yaw 36.5°; rep 2: roll 58.4° vs yaw 42.1°) | DETERMINED, raw − (rep 1 −30.7°, rep 2 −19.0°), invert not needed | INCONCLUSIVE (reference window not steady in both reps: spread 9.4° and 7.0°) | **FAIL**: sequence as expected, but a **16.2° one-frame step in the output pose, 6.2 s after the cover cue, state locked** (threshold 10°, jump check meaningful, held pose 16.8° from neutral) | 30.0 | 900 | 0.905 | not answered | clean |

Release check in all four: running before stop, `isRunning` false after stop, 0 inputs, 0 outputs, 0 callbacks after stop, session object deallocated, one stop notification, taken 1.23, 1.21, 1.25 and 1.21 s after stop [measured]. The green light was not answered in any of them, so the by-eye light is not in these reports (Sanjai's earlier observations are in "Camera-light observations by Sanjai (2026-11-09)").

**The loss-test failure of 153425 in detail [measured].**
- The step is `stepDegrees` 16.2° in the **output pose**, at 6.21 s after the cover cue, with the tracker LOCKED. The cover cue was at 38.04 s and the uncover cue at 43.04 s of the run, so the step is at about 44.25 s: [derived] 1.2 s after the uncover cue and about 0.4 s after the tracker was locked again (it re-acquired 0.78 s after the uncover cue). Time spent in each state was as configured (holding 0.50 s, decaying 1.50 s, static 2.41 s); the failing element is only the jump check.
- The report classifies the **raw** Vision orientation as a **"spike"**: it jumped 25.4° in one frame (758°/s) and was back within 30 % of that 3 frames later, "shaped like a single-frame outlier".
- The raw roll in the 31-frame window around the step: it fell from −56.25° (at −0.098 s) to −45.4°, −28.1° and −2.9° (at 0.000 s), i.e. **−56.2° to −2.9° within 0.098 s** (raw yaw −0.5° to −0.3° and raw pitch −6.9° to −8.6° in the same frames); the window's raw roll ranged from −56.2° to +3.3°. Swings of 15 to 25° between neighbouring frames also occur before and after the step (for example +3.3° → −19.2° → −38.1° in the first three frames of the window, at −0.499 to −0.431 s), so the "spike" is the largest of several large roll movements in a second in which the head was meant to be still [derived from the frames].
- Cause: **UNKNOWN**. [Hypothesis, UNVERIFIED, untested] that the hand leaving the picture or a partly occluded face disturbs Vision's roll estimate; nothing in the report says so.

**Roll evidence [measured].** Roll is unreliable in these runs; it moves with the other two moves and is hardly read at all during the tilt:
- Raw roll during the two **turn-LEFT holds** (should be near the reference): 092516 medians −8.1° and −8.1° (min −29.9°, −33.6°); 100655 **+44.5° and +29.6°** (max +54.8°); 153425 **−26.5° and −67.4°** (frame minimum −92.2° in the second hold). The sign of roll leaking into a left turn is not the same between runs.
- In 153425 the second turn-LEFT hold's **median raw roll is −74.7°** (range 75.5°, σ 13.4°; the reference before it −16.2°) [from `signs.yaw.repetitions[1].holdStats`]. This is the "−75°" Sanjai read.
- During the two **tilt-RIGHT-EAR holds** the raw **yaw** reads far from the reference in 092516, 100655 and 153425 (hold medians −49.8°/−51.4°, −49.0°/−41.0°, −54.9°/−50.9°; frame minimum −64.7°) while the raw roll medians are +7.2°/+4.2°, −0.8°/−3.6° and +22.9°/+16.0°. [Derived] In these three runs the tilt move was read mainly as a yaw change, not as the intended roll. Why (the move as done, Vision's angle coupling, or both) is **UNKNOWN**; in 092706 nothing moved. Whether the 092516 / 100655 / 153425 tilts also involved a head turn by the person is not recorded.
- 153425 roll repetitions: +31.5° and +27.0° with reference windows not steady (spread 9.4° and 7.0°), so 46.9° and 35.2° would have been required (5× the spread); not usable. In no run was a roll sign determined, and none is hinted at (no LIKELY).

**Sign consistency with the earlier confirmation [derived, not a new confirmation].** Where an axis was determined, the raw sign agrees with what Sanjai confirmed from run 215400 on 2026-11-09 (yaw raw +, no invert; pitch raw −, no invert): yaw raw + in 092516 (DETERMINED) and 100655 (LIKELY), pitch raw − in 100655 and 153425 (DETERMINED). Roll: nothing. **No sign default was changed and none is applied.**

**Frame rate [measured].** 30.0 fps in three runs (frame interval mean 33.34 ms, max 33.3 ms), **25.97 fps in 100655** (mean interval 38.5 ms, max 47.0 ms; 779 frames in the window instead of 900). In 100655 the limiting quality component was "size" in 100 % of the frames and 27 % of the frames were below the loss threshold; mean quality 0.599, against 0.738 (size limited 56 %), 1.000 and 0.905 in the others. Vision time per frame was higher in 100655 (mean 24.7 ms, p95 29.4 ms) than in 092516 (18.5 / 21.6), 092706 (18.7 / 21.2) and 153425 (14.0 / 16.2). **Cause of the 25.97 fps: UNKNOWN** (the earlier "Frame-rate finding" lists the unchecked candidates; the power state and light level were not recorded for this run). The loss test of 100655 (state flapped, decaying 0.08 s) happened in the run with the lowest quality and frame rate; **a connection is not shown** [hypothesis, UNVERIFIED].

**Comparison with Sanjai's account.** Every item he listed matches the JSON: 092516 yaw DETERMINED, pitch and roll INCONCLUSIVE, loss PASS (weak), 30.0 fps; 092706 all INCONCLUSIVE with no head movement, loss INCOMPLETE (never covered: only `locked` observed); 100655 pitch DETERMINED, yaw LIKELY, roll INCONCLUSIVE, loss INCOMPLETE, 26.0 fps (25.97); 153425 pitch DETERMINED, yaw and roll INCONCLUSIVE, loss FAIL, a 16.2° step 6.2 s after the cover cue, shape "spike", raw roll −56° to −3° within 0.1 s (−56.25° to −2.90° in 0.098 s), 30.0 fps; green light not answered ×4; release check clean ×4; "−75° in the second left-turn hold" = hold median −74.7°. One detail he did not list that the data add: in 100655 the "partial cover" is how the report words the flapping (`holding` entered twice, decaying 0.08 s); what actually happened with the hand is not in the report. 092516's pitch rep 1 failed on the wrong-axis rule (yaw 17.8° against pitch 15.7°), which he did not mention.

**What these four do not show.** A clean run (the Step 2d / 2e criteria): all three axes DETERMINED, a loss test that passes with a meaningful jump check, and an answered green light. Any sign for roll. Why the jump happened at 153425 and why 100655 ran at 26 fps. Anything about dim light (all four normal light). Still missing: the items in "Manual results still missing". Procedure: docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP2.md.

All figures below were read from the JSON. **The reports say nothing by themselves about why trials went unanswered; Sanjai has not said. That cause is UNKNOWN and is not filled in here.** No code, default or gate changed.

**The answers in played order [measured]** (SL / SR / SA = static left / right / ahead; HL / HR = head 45° left / right; answers L / R / M = left ear / right ear / middle, ? = can't tell, – = not answered):
- 160214: order `HL SR HL SR SL SL SA HR SA HL HR SA HR SR SL HL HR SR SA SL`; answers `– – L R – – – – – – – – – ? M M M R M –`. The 8 answered trials: #3 HL (expected right ear) → left ear; #4 SR → right ear; #14 SR → can't tell; #15 SL (expected left ear) → middle; #16 HL → middle; #17 HR (expected left ear) → middle; #18 SR → right ear; #19 SA → middle. Reaction times of the answered trials 2.17 to 4.73 s (median 2.97 s).
- 161833: order `SL SR HR SA HL HR SL SA SA HL HR SR SA SR HL SL SR SL HL HR`; answers `L – – R L L R M – – – – – – – – – – – –`. The 6 answered trials: #1 SL → left ear (matches); #4 SA (expected middle) → right ear; #5 HL (expected right ear) → left ear; #6 HR (expected left ear) → left ear (matches); #7 SL (expected left ear) → right ear; #8 SA → middle (matches). Reaction times 1.95 to 11.73 s (median 5.72 s; the timeout is 12 s).
- **From these numbers [derived]:** in both runs the answered trials come in blocks, and both runs end with an unanswered stretch (trials 20 and 9 to 20). 12 and 14 trials ran into the 12 s timeout, which accounts for about 144 s and 168 s of the 200 s and 234 s. Presses were recorded in both runs, so the buttons did enable and register at least in those trials. **Why the other trials got no press is not in the reports and not known.**

**What this does and does not show.**
- **No conclusion about a left/right swap.** Both flags are "not assessable" (too few definite answers: at least 6 are needed). The answers that exist point both ways (run 161833: #1 and #6 as expected, #7 the opposite ear), with 6 to 8 answers per run; nothing can be read from them. The two pending 1-minute checks (below) **stay pending**. **[Update 2026-11-15: both checks were done; see "Result: the two 1-minute left/right checks".]**
- **The "AirPods on" answer of run 160214 conflicts with Sanjai's earlier statement that he was not using AirPods.** It is recorded as the report says, **not corrected**. The reports carry only the device's transport category (Bluetooth), never a name, so they cannot settle which is right. If AirPods Spatial Audio really was on, the system's own head tracking would stack on top of the scripted head (docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP3A.md, "Before you start"); that is [hypothesis, UNVERIFIED] and nothing in the report shows it either way.
- **"Clicks or crackling: yes" and "inside the head" (run 160214, one report)** are recorded as answers. They are not interpreted. The report text itself notes that the fade steps are part of the clicks answer; whether the fade or something else caused any click is UNKNOWN.
- Nothing about HRTF quality, front/back or loudness can be read from 14 answers. The card, the buttons, the fade, the timers and the report writing from the real card have now been used twice on a real Mac (so those are exercised, not verified for sound); Bluetooth at 44.1 kHz (the engine converts from 48 kHz) was the output both times, and the buffer size differed (320, 512) between the runs for a reason that is not known.

**Pending (not done), unchanged:** the two 1-minute left/right checks before the first head-tracked listening (section above), and Sanjai's account of the unanswered trials.

## Decision by Sanjai (stated 2026-11-10, afternoon IST; recorded in this file on 2026-11-15)
**Date, per Sanjai:** he stated this decision on **2026-11-10, in the afternoon (IST)**. His attempt that day to send it to me was **not recorded**, so it entered this file only on 2026-11-15.

**Roll stays at 0 in the first tracked build; roll stays PLANNED until a pure tilt is measured. Yaw and pitch are confirmed (no invert).** The evidence for why roll is not used is in "Four more real camera self-test reports (2026-11-10)" (roll never determined in 11 real runs; raw roll leaks into turns with a sign that changes between runs; the tilt move was read mainly as yaw). This is a decision, not a measurement; no code was changed to implement it in this commit, and the tracker is still not connected to the renderer.

## Step 3b findings and verification record (2026-11-15): the camera tracker is connected to the renderer (head-tracked listening)
**Goal and exit.** The first head-tracked sound: camera → Vision → One-Euro → calibration → state machine → `PoseMailbox` → `ListenerPosePump` → `AVAudioEnvironmentNode`, behind its own HUD card with one Start and one Stop, roll forced to 0, no jump on face loss, live readouts, a low capped volume, and a manual test. **Status: IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED. It has never run on the real camera through this card, and nobody has heard it.** **[Update 2026-11-15, about 22:53 to 23:01 IST: Sanjai ran it once on the real camera and listened; see "First head-tracked listening". The tag is unchanged.]** Procedure: docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP3B.md. No CLAUDE.md edit (Sanjai decides the gate wording).

**Decisions by Sanjai (stated in his message of 2026-11-15; the roll decision itself was stated on 2026-11-10, afternoon IST, see "Decision by Sanjai" above; the dates of the others are not recorded)**
- **The gate is open for yaw and pitch.** Their signs are confirmed (default mapping, no invert; run 215400). Roll stays forced to 0 and stays PLANNED until a pure tilt is measured (see "Decision by Sanjai" above).
- The two blind listening runs (160214, 161833) were inconclusive (most trials not answered, no conclusion about a left/right swap); **a further blind run is parked.**
- **The two 1-minute left/right checks stay PENDING and are done before the first head-tracked listening** (stereo voice test outside our app; Test A's first sound). The manual puts them first. This build has not been listened to. **[Update 2026-11-15: both checks were done at about 22:47 IST and reported no swap for his one pair of headphones, then the first head-tracked listening followed; see "Result: the two 1-minute left/right checks".]**
- No listening report was added in this step (the two existing ones were committed earlier in `c9fd7dc`).

**What was built**
- **A separate card**, "Head-tracked listening — the CAMERA moves the sound", instead of a pose-source picker inside the scripted card: the scripted session is a different class, and the exclusion is the same either way. `ListeningModeExclusion` (Core, tested) lets only one of scripted / blind / camera run, and the camera card also refuses while the HUD's camera or the guided self-test is in use. One **Start** (audio engine created first so a missing output fails before the camera opens, then the camera, then the sound) and one **Stop** (pose feed cut, sound off, camera stopped, the existing programmatic release check runs; Cmd+Q does the same synchronously). Recenter is the existing one.
- **Pose path, nothing new on the audio thread.** The tracker's snapshot handler (camera queue and watchdog queue) calls `TrackedListeningSession.publish` before the hop to the main actor. The two queues would be two producers for a single-producer mailbox, so `publish` serializes callers with a lock (control threads only). The pump (a control-thread timer, 10.67 ms) interpolates, converts with `ListenerFrame` and calls the renderer's listener setter. The renderer's source, listener and volume setters are control-thread calls, as in Step 3a. No render callback of ours; no `PoseMailbox` change (so no TSan run).
- **Mapping:** while the card runs, the tracker uses the default mapping (no invert); a saved or manual mapping is set aside and restored on stop.
- **Roll is 0, removed in three places** by tested conversions (not the Invert checkbox): (1) `RollRemoval` before the One-Euro filter (an opt-in `HeadTracker.setRollForcedToZero`; the real runs showed raw roll swinging ±50° between frames, which would otherwise raise the filter's cutoff for yaw and pitch; the raw angles and `mappedRawEuler` stay for display); (2) `RollRemoval` in `publish`, before the mailbox; (3) `ListenerMapping.zeroRoll` at the pump's output. (3) was added after the first silent self-check showed a peak **sent** roll of 0.003°: interpolating between two roll-free poses leaves a tiny roll. With it the sent roll is 0 to about 1e-15° and the listener's right vector is horizontal on every block (tested over combined yaw and pitch moves).
- **Source sound: continuous pink-ish noise (`SteadyNoise`), −30 dBFS RMS (UNTUNED), a seamless 4 s loop, played by Apple's player node.** Why: the old 0.3 s burst loop disappears while you turn, so there is nothing to follow; a sine carries almost no spectral cue for an HRTF; a steady broadband noise is audible all the time and carries the cues. Drawbacks stated: it can sound like a hiss, and without onsets front/back is harder (a moving head helps). Placed 2 m straight ahead, never moves. `EnvironmentRenderer.init` got an optional `loop:` parameter (default unchanged: the Step 3a bursts).
- **Volume: default 15 %, capped at 40 %, both UNTUNED** (`TrackedListeningPolicy`; set before the engine starts, volume 0 before the engine stops). There is no loudness measurement and no limiter: the cap guards against a slider slip only.
- **Live readouts:** the pose actually sent (yaw / pitch / roll, after the roll is zeroed), tracking state, face yes/no, **camera fps as measured by the app (not compensated)**, time since the last frame when frames stop, pump ticks / mean / longest, poses published and refused.
- `--simulate --tracked-listening-selfcheck`: a silent (volume 0) run of the card's real code path with the SIMULATED user; it refuses to run without `--simulate` (never the real camera).

**Face loss: how it is tested.** `TrackedListeningFaceLossTests` drives the REAL chain on virtual time (no sleeps): `HeadPosePipeline` (quality gate, One-Euro filter, calibration, state machine) fed with a made-up head (yaw 0 for 1 s, a 1 s smoothstep to 30° LEFT, hold), frames stop at t = 4 s (the 50 ms watchdog replayed as `processFramesStopped` after 250 ms without a frame), frames return at t = 7 s; every pose goes through `publish` → mailbox → the real `ListenerPosePump` at the 10.67 ms block rate → a recording fake output. It asserts the largest change of the listener's forward vector per pump block in each phase against bounds **derived from the design values written in the test, not from the code's configuration** (a smoothstep over D s covering A° peaks at 1.5 A / D °/s; the camera updates at 30 Hz and the watchdog at 20 Hz, so it arrives in steps; the interpolator passes at most 1 − exp(−block/20 ms) of a step in the first block; the bound is 1.5 × that, an UNTUNED margin). Measured against the bounds [measured, virtual time]: ramp of the head's own turn 0.73° (bound 0.93°); **hold 8.5e-7° (frozen)**; decay 0.69° (bound 0.93°); re-acquire 2.97° (bound 3.72°). It also asserts the documented state order (static, locked, holding, decaying, static, locked), neutral within 0.1° while static, and the 30° turn followed again within 0.5° afterwards. The margins are tight (about 25 %) because the bounds are derived, not padded; a jump would land an order of magnitude above them.
- **It does not test a real camera, a real face loss (a hand over the lens), the real timers, or how it sounds.** The app-level self-check (below) did not reach the loss phase.

**Run, and passed (this Mac, 2026-11-15)**
- `swift build` (debug and release) clean, no warnings; `swift test`: **719 tests** = 557 Core + 44 Tracking + 118 Engine (was 692 = 544 + 40 + 108; 13 + 4 + 10 new). `scripts/scan-render-api-coverage.sh`: 134 files, 0 violations.
- **Mutation check: `scripts/mutate_trackedlistening.py`, 21 mutants, 21 killed** (final run on the final code), by named assertion failures; files restored and byte-compared. R (roll removal) 3, X (exclusion) 2, V (volume) 2, T (tracker switch) 2, N (noise) 2, S (session) 5, Z (output mapping) 2, and C01–C03 which mutate EXISTING code (interpolator time constant 0, decay 0 s, re-acquire blend 0 s) and are killed by the face-loss chain test alone, i.e. the chain test can fail. The list was written to a timestamped scratch file **before any of the new logic existed** (01:34:06); Z01 and Z02 were appended (01:40:26) after the first self-check, before any mutant ran. **One mutant survived the first full run: N01 (the loop seam as a hard cut).** My seam test measured the step across the seam, which this noise does not reveal (neighbouring samples already differ by about 0.76 σ); two other statistics I tried also did not separate the variants (a measurement, not a guess). The test now uses what a real continuation has and a cut does not, the correlation between the last and the first sample (about 0.72 averaged over 200 seeds with the crossfade, about 0 for a hard cut), and N01 is killed. **Honest caveat:** this says the seam is a continuation of the noise; whether a hard cut would even be audible with this noise was not tested by ear.
- **Not mutation-checked:** the lock in `publish` that serializes the two tracker queues (a race there needs two threads; ThreadSanitizer was not run since the mailbox was not touched, and the Engine tests never run two producers).
- **Silent wiring check in the real app bundle** (`--simulate --tracked-listening-selfcheck --mute`, volume 0, SIMULATED user, no real camera, no sound), run twice (before and after the output-mapping change), exit 0, empty stderr: the card's real Start → camera provider → tracker → feed → session → pump → engine started silently on the real output → Stop path ran; second run: 2403 pump steps, 769 poses published, 0 refused, mean pump period 10.67 ms, longest 11.7 ms, 30.0 fps, state STATIC then LOCKED, peak sent yaw 32.1°, peak sent pitch 32.2°, **peak sent roll 0.000°** while the tracker's own mapped roll reached 13.9° (the simulated user tilts), camera status idle after stop. **These numbers come from a made-up person and say nothing about any head or ear.**

**Not run, not known (UNVERIFIED)**
- Everything by ear, and whether it follows a real head: the direction (turn left → the noise moves toward the right ear), the lag, any jitter or click, whether the steady noise is a good choice (hiss, comfort), the 15 % default and 40 % cap (no loudness measurement).
- The real camera through this card: permission behaviour of the rebuilt ad-hoc bundle, the Start sequence with a real prompt, the camera light, the release check after Stop and after Cmd+Q (the release code is the existing one, the sequence around it is new), a real face loss and re-acquire, glasses / hair / lighting.
- Latency camera-to-ear: not measured; no figure is claimed.
- The two pending left/right checks: if the headphones or the route are swapped, step 3 of the manual would mislead.
- **Known risks I could see (not tested):** (a) in three of the four real 2026-11-10 self-tests the camera read a right-ear tilt mainly as a **yaw** change (raw yaw −41° to −55°), so a tilt can still move the sound even though roll is 0; (b) a one-frame spike in the raw yaw or pitch while LOCKED (like the 16.2° step of run 153425) is not filtered specially and can move the sound; (c) at 16.6 fps (seen once) the camera updates every 60 ms, and the 20 ms interpolation leaves visible steps; (d) the stop is an abrupt cut (volume 0, then the engine stops), not a fade; (e) with the card running, the app holds an App Nap activity assertion (the existing tracking one); (f) Euler conversion near pitch ±90° (gimbal lock) was not exercised.
- The CLAUDE.md gate wording: still the old text (see the report); Sanjai decides.

The tags of the card and its logic are **not raised** by it (still IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED; see the end).

- **Lag: "a little"** (not measured; no number was given).
- **No jumps and no jitter. No clicks or crackling.**

- **Tilting the head: no change.**

**From his screenshot at 22:53 [as given to me]**

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Tracking | LOCKED |
| Camera frame rate | **16.6 fps** |
| Quality (our gate) | 0.72, **limited by frame rate** |
