**Derived, not reported.** A pump mean of 11.61 ms is 512 frames at **44.1 kHz** (512 / 44100 = 11.61 ms; at 48 kHz it would be 10.67 ms), so the output ran at 44.1 kHz, as in the earlier Bluetooth runs; **whether the output was Bluetooth is not recorded for this session.** The 76 ms is the HUD's capture-to-pose figure: it starts at the camera's sample timestamp and ends when the pose is ready, so it **excludes** everything before the timestamp and everything after the pose (the mailbox, the pump, the engine, the headphone link); **it is not camera-to-ear latency.** The sent roll of −0.0 with a raw roll of +1.8° is what the design says (roll removed before the renderer). The 16.6 fps is the same rate as in the real self-test run 215400, and "frame rate" limited the quality there too; the **cause of the camera's frame-rate differences stays UNKNOWN** (see "Frame-rate finding").

**What this says.** In one session, one person, with the expected direction known, the **yaw direction was reported correct by ear** (turn left → the sound toward the right ear, as a fixed speaker would; turn right → toward the left ear); no jumps, jitter or clicks were reported; the 5 s cover was reported smooth; and a tilt was reported to change nothing (the earlier risk that the camera reads a tilt as a yaw change did not show up in his report of this one session, and is not excluded).

## Step 6b-3 plan (2026-11-16): ring recovery, the 44.1 kHz rate mismatch, the road to a first audible build — PLAN ONLY, docs-only
**What this is.** A plan for Sanjai's decision, written to docs/ARCHITECTURE.md ("Step 6b-3 plan") in this commit. **No code, script, allow-list or C file changed.** The Step 6b-1 gate stays in force (no output gain above 0, no audible ring use until the underrun recovery is implemented and mutation-checked; count-only gain-0 runs allowed). Every fact in the plan was re-read from the committed records and the SDK headers, with its source tagged there; the headline facts: speakers 48→48 kHz clean for 780 s [measured]; Bluetooth asks a constant 558 frames per call (+1294 ppm by ceil-rounding) and the count-only ring drains ~62 frames/s, starving at or before 59.6–118.3 s (the reports' derived bounds; measured at 1 Hz: 56.0–115.0 s) from first-pull fills of 4,050–7,634 frames [measured]; the 6b-2b offline experiment could NOT settle whether the device-path mixer consumes all 558 (H1) or keeps the surplus (H2) [measured offline]; recovery is NOT implemented (Step 6b-1 record); per-call frame counts were deferred to this step (Sanjai, 2026-11-08).

**The proposed split (details and the options table in ARCHITECTURE):**
- **6b-3a:** implement the ALREADY-DESIGNED recovery exactly as the design's policy table and re-prime paragraph say (per-call frame counter, zero-and-advance, overrun forward jump, divergence resync, control-thread re-prime), tested as "How this will be tested" specifies (C harness, TSan incl. plain builds, UBSan, mutation list written before code including the three named broken variants), plus a gain-0 recovery mode of the ring probe so drain → re-prime → recovery is visible in counts on the real Bluetooth path. C changes to `CSystemAudioRing` need Sanjai's approval; **no scan-rule or allow-list change** (same one symbol, called once; scans re-run and re-shown to fail broken variants).
- **6b-3b:** the rate mismatch. Four options compared in ARCHITECTURE (own C resampler + servo inside the one allow-listed function; engine conversion + `AVAudioUnitVarispeed` control-thread trim; drop-or-insert; re-prime only). Header facts quoted, not guessed: Varispeed rate 0.25–4.0, resampling changes pitch [H: AVAudioUnitVarispeed.h]; its base class "does not process audio in real-time" [H: AVAudioUnitTimeEffect.h], meaning for a live engine not stated — to be spiked. Recommendation: try (2) first (zero C-side risk; its servo path is validatable at gain 0 by watching the fill slope respond to a ±500 ppm trim, while recording per-block frame counts, valid-timestamp share, phase mismatches and L/R lockstep against the measured baseline of the three real Bluetooth reports — 100 % valid sample times, 0 phase mismatches — noting that the AVAudioSourceNode.h sample-time warning applies to Varispeed as downstream time compression, so the share may fall), (1) as fallback CANDIDATE and likely Step-8 direction — with an OPEN DESIGN REQUIREMENT recorded in ARCHITECTURE and no solution chosen: resampling in the block consumes ~557.28 source frames per 512-frame call, which breaks the advance-by-frames-asked invariant and the phase rule (the same ground on which (3) is rejected), and how both blocks keep identical source positions under a trim is unsolved, so (1) is not ready to implement; (4) as the universal safety net (honest range: the current default prime, 2,048 frames, covers only ~24 s of this drain [derived]; the measured starvation bounds came from larger first-pull fills; the ~263 s figure of the first draft was an upper bound at a prime depth the repo has not shown usable and is corrected in ARCHITECTURE), (3) rejected (breaks the same invariant).
- **6b-3c:** first audible stereo (two mono source nodes as two virtual speakers), LATER, after 6b-3a and Sanjai's go.

**H1 vs H2:** need NOT be settled to choose the 6b-3b architecture (a fill-slope servo nulls the measured net rate under either hypothesis); it SHOULD be settled before tuning and before any Bluetooth timeline claim. The cheap, gate-respecting measurement: 6b-3a's per-call counter plus the long-planned 600 s Bluetooth gain-0 run (manual Run 2, never done). Counts can refute the internal-surplus variant (≈0.78 s of audio would have to accumulate inside the converter over 600 s) but can never separate "consumes all" from "discards the surplus" — that residue matters only for audible Bluetooth quality, settled by ear later.

**Wired 3.5 mm jack (Sanjai's question):** a good first audible candidate with one measurement first: after 6b-3a, one 60–120 s count-only gain-0 probe run with the wired headphones to read the jack's transport and rate (never measured; not assumed). 48 kHz → the measured-clean path, lowest-risk first listen; 44.1 kHz → a 6b-3b case like Bluetooth.

**Decisions Sanjai owes before any code:** (1) approve the split and 6b-3a's C-file scope; (2) approve the 600 s Bluetooth gain-0 run in 6b-3a's exit; (3) pick the 6b-3b order (may wait for the counter data); (4) the wired-jack probe run. **Nothing is implemented until then.**

## Step 6b-3a-1 findings and verification record (2026-11-18): render-side recovery in `CSystemAudioRing` — IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED
**Scope.** The render-side half of the designed recovery ("Reading the ring from two blocks", policy table; "Step 6b-3 plan", 6b-3a items 1 to 4). C code in `Sources/CSystemAudioRing` (`.c` and `.h`), its Swift tests, the C harnesses and the mutation script; **no scan script, no `scripts/rt-block-allowlist.txt`, no `SASourceNode.m` changed** (hashes below). **Output gain stayed 0; nothing was played; no real tap, no real-time engine and no device was used; no sound, latency or CPU claim.** **NOT built (Step 6b-3a-2): the control-thread re-prime and the ring probe's recovery mode.** An underrun is still zeros-and-advance, and a reader that fell behind the producer still stays starved at equal rates until something re-primes it.

**What the code now does (per `SARingRender` call, in this order)**
1. **Frame-count record:** a table of at most 8 distinct `frameCount` values with their call counts (in order of first appearance), an overflow counter, min and max (every call counts, bad-buffer and oversize ones included); single writer, relaxed stores, no allocation, no call.
2. **Divergence jump (valid timestamp):** the block whose phase (cursor − render time) is SMALLER than the other block's last published phase is behind and jumps its OWN cursor FORWARD by the difference, before the availability decision (so it is served at the new position); the block that is ahead does nothing. A difference that is not a whole number of frames (fractional, or at least 9e15) is not jumped: it sets `diverged` and `needsReprime`. A jump larger than N − T is refused (counted in `refusedJumps`) and sets `needsReprime`. **No usable timestamp: the weaker rule only counts, sets the sticky `diverged` flag and `needsReprime`, and never jumps.**
3. **Overrun jump:** a lapped block (w − r > window) outputs zeros and moves its OWN cursor FORWARD to w − T, FORWARD ONLY (a target at or behind the normal advance r + F means no jump), refused above N − T with `needsReprime`. It publishes the phase its next call should compute, so the jump does not read as a phase change.
4. **Cursor:** advances by exactly `frameCount` on every call plus at most the counted jump. The underrun path is unchanged.
5. **Snapshot (control thread):** the table, min/max, `overrunJumps/Frames`, `divergenceJumps/Frames`, `refusedJumps`, `largestJumpFrames`, `diverged`, `needsReprime` per reader; `standingFill` (T) and a ring-level `needsReprime`. `SARingPrime` clears all of them and sets T to the depth it primed; before any prime T = capacity / 16 (a GUESS, 2,048 for the default ring). Flags are sticky until the next prime.

**Where I read the design (for review; it is silent or ambiguous here)**
- The size of a jump is measured **beyond the normal advance** (target − (r + F)); the bound N − T applies to that.
- A refused jump means "the block keeps playing zeros-and-advance", the table's words: the cursor advances by F only. With a stalled producer the next call's distance is F smaller, so an overrun jump refused once can be made on the next call; the request for a re-prime stays (sticky).
- **Same-cycle consequence found by the harness, not by the design:** with valid timestamps the SECOND block of a cycle never overruns on its own after the first one jumped: it sees the first block's published phase, is behind, divergence-jumps to the first block's position and is served. "Each block picks its own w − T" only happens when the first block's call had no usable timestamp (nothing published), and then the next cycle's divergence jump re-aligns them.
- **A divergence jump refused (lag > N − T) is also lapped by definition** (w − r ≥ N when the other block holds a standing fill of T), and the overrun jump in the same call equals the lag, so it is refused too (`refusedJumps` 2 in that call). The case where only the divergence rule refuses is the one where the two blocks' RENDER TIMES differ by more than N − T while nobody is lapped (harness scenario O4).
- For an invalid timestamp the design says "count and set a sticky diverged flag" (row 4) and "any divergence the render thread cannot resolve sets needsReprime" (row 5); both are set.

**Verification run (this Mac, 2026-11-17 / 18)**
- `swift build` (debug; release for the scans) clean; `swift test`: **721 tests** (557 Core + 44 Tracking + 120 Engine; was 719; 2 new Engine ring tests); **the same under `swift test --sanitize=thread`: 721 passed, no ThreadSanitizer report.** That Swift TSan run has no two-threaded ring test, so on its own it proves little about the ring; the C TSan runs below are the ones that were shown to fail on broken variants.
- C harness (`scripts/check-systemaudioring.sh`): **8,949 deterministic checks, 0 failures**; every render call in every scenario also asserts the invariant "the cursor moved forward by exactly frameCount plus the frames counted as jumps". New scenarios: the frame-count table (M), overrun jump with the exact bound (C1 to C4), two lapped readers (N), skipped block and different frame counts in both block orders (O), a refused divergence jump on its own (O4), flags and prime (P, P2, Q). Threaded stress, all clean: `paced`, `lapping` (readers jump; invariant checked from the library's own snapshot) and the new `cycles` mode (two readers concurrently with a spin barrier, a right block skipped every 11th cycle and a left block asked for half a frameCount every 13th, 20,000 cycles: every block served from the same frame index, the counters equal the injected faults).
- `scripts/tsan-systemaudioring.sh all` (plain-sample and atomic builds, paced, lapping and cycles) and `scripts/ubsan-systemaudioring.sh all` (recovery off, float-cast-overflow on; the hostile harness gained absurd render-time pairs for the jump arithmetic): no report.
- **Scans, on the final tree:** `scripts/scan-rt-block.sh`: **`_SARingRender` 268 instructions, 0 calls**; the block (`___SACreateSourceNode_block_invoke`, 15 instructions) makes 1 call, to the allow-listed `_SARingRender`; `_SARingIOProc` 88 instructions and `_SARingWrite` 41 instructions, 0 calls; the CTapProbe zero-call scan passes; `scripts/scan-render-api-coverage.sh`: 134 files, 0 violations. **All 13 broken-variant cases of `scan-rt-block-cases.sh` still fail for the named cause** (cases 1 and 1v pass as controls; 2 to 7, z, p1 to p4 fail as required) and `scan-render-api-coverage-cases.sh`: all cases behaved as expected. No compiler-inserted call appeared in the bigger function (the `no_builtin` attribute that stops a `bzero` stays).

**Mutation record (`scripts/mutate_systemaudioring.py --swift`, detectors D1 check, D2 paced / lapping / cycles, D3 TSan plain-sample, D4 UBSan, D5 swift, D6 scan).** 97 mutants: the 47 of Step 6b-1 (M01 to M41, M43 to M47 and M11b; there is no M42) and 50 new (T01 to T11 frame-count table, O01 to O12 plus O06b and O10x overrun jump, L01, D01 to D14 plus D12b divergence jump, P01 to P09 flags and standing fill). **94 killed (at least one detector failed cleanly), 0 killed only by a hang, 1 equivalent, 2 survived.**
- **M01 to M47: NOT all killed. 45 of 47 killed; M21 and M22 survive, as in Step 6b-1 (the two fences stay UNTESTED; the sentence in "Step 6b-1 findings" stands unchanged).** All 50 new mutants are killed except **O10x, which is equivalent by argument** (in the underrun branch r + F > w1, so next > w1 ≥ w1 − T and the guard `w1 − T > next` can never be true). The first form of O10 in the list ("an underrun also moves the cursor to w − T") was that guarded form; it survived the first run for that reason and was **replaced by the unguarded form** (the cursor moves backward), which is killed by D1, D2 lapping and D4; the guarded form is kept as O10x.
- **The three variants the design names:** V1 (one shared cursor): M11 and M11b — clean FAIL by D1, D2 cycles, D4, D5; the threaded D2 paced, D2 lapping and D3 TSan HANG (the harness never finishes), so they are not counted as clean kills; V2 (advance by the frames read, not asked): M12 — clean FAIL by D1, D2 lapping, D4, D5 (D2 paced, D2 cycles and D3 PASS); V3 (a behind block jumps backward): D03 — clean FAIL by D1, D2 lapping, D2 cycles, D3, D4, D5 (D2 paced PASS).
- Clean FAILs per detector over the 97 (HANGs in brackets): D1 89, D2 paced 19 (2), D2 lapping 32 (2), D2 cycles 30, D3 TSan 35 (2), D4 UBSan 14, D5 swift 72, D6 scan 0 (D6 is a guard: no mutant made a call). Killed by exactly one detector (17): M02 and M03 (D3 only, as in Step 6b-1), M07 and M15 (D1 only), M31 (D4 only), and 12 new ones (T01, T08, O03, D05, D08, D10, D11, P01 to P04 and P08; all D1 only).
- **`addedAfterList` (as run):** M11b, M38 to M41, M43 to M47 (Step 6b-1 additions), and in this step **O10x** (added when the first form of O10 turned out equivalent), **L01** (the largest-jump record of the overrun jump had no mutant: a hole in my list found while writing the patches) and **D14** (the size guard before the float-to-integer cast is only visible to UBSan: no mutant targeted it). Not flagged in the run but also changed after the list: **O06b** and **D12b** (each is the second half of a list item that named two bugs; flagged `True` in the script afterwards), **T07** (the list said "min/max only on served blocks"; built as "min/max ignore a value the first time it appears") and **D13** (built as "the block reads from its old position and only the cursor jumps").
- **O04, O05, O07 (the N − T bound):** the C harness block C3 (`systemaudioring_check.c` lines 247 to 261) makes an overrun jump of exactly 768 = N − T (T = 256, N = 1024) and one of 769: O04 (a jump of exactly N − T refused) fails line 254, O05 (N − T + 1 allowed) and O07 (bound removed) fail lines 256 and 257. In Swift the single test `aJumpOfMoreThanTheRingMinusTheStandingFillIsRefusedAndAsksForAReprimeAndExactlyThatMuchIsMade` (both variants) is the one that fails for each of the three (names read from a real `swift test` run of each mutant). The O3 block of the harness tests the DIVERGENCE bound (lags 768, 896, 1024), not the overrun bound; the refused divergence jump on its own is O4.

**Harness expectations I changed because the count-only behaviour was replaced**
- C harness: scenario C (lapped reader: was 4 overruns with the cursor at 512 and a served block at w − r = 512; now zeros, a jump to w − T, then served at a fill of T; the 513 case also asserts the jump); scenario G (the unequal call counts used a frozen render time, which now reads as divergence: now advancing render times); scenario H2 (skipped block: mismatches L 2 and R 3 with no repair; now L 0, R 1 and R jumps 128, cursors equal); scenario H3 (a violation of the weaker rule was only counted; now it also sets `diverged` and `needsReprime`, and lockstep leaves both clear); **O3 at lag 896** (below); the stress harness (the reader tracked its cursor as the sum of the frame counts and expected zero phase self-changes and mismatches in every mode; now it reads the real cursor, checks the invariant per call, and expects zero self-changes only in the paced mode).
- Swift tests: the overrun test (four overruns at cursor 512 → one jump to 960), the snapshot test (frozen render time → advancing), the phase test (H2's numbers, and its weaker-rule loop was not lockstep: now real cycles).
- **O3 at lag 896.** I first wrote: "the divergence jump is refused, but the overrun rule then moves R (896 − 128 = 768 ≤ the bound): refusedJumps 1, overrunJumps 1." The harness failed it (refused 2, no overrun jump). **The code was right and my arithmetic wrong:** in a cycle the producer commits before the blocks run, so w1 − r_R = T + lag + F and the overrun jump (w1 − T) − (r + F) equals the lag itself, not lag − F; with lag 896 > N − T = 768 both rules refuse. It now says: `divergenceJumps 0, overrunJumps 0, refusedJumps 2, needsReprime 1, largestJumpFrames 896, overrunBlocks 1, R's cursor differs from L's`.

- The first run (22:41 on 2026-11-17) was killed by a lid sleep at 23:18:40 after 57 mutants; no completed mutant ran across a sleep. Its results were not reused for the final numbers: after a harness fix the full list was run again (10:25 to about 11:15 on 2026-11-18) with the final harness, 97 of 97, results in `results.jsonl`. - **A detection power loss, found and fixed:** in the first run M03 (relaxed instead of acquire on the render's load of `w`) SURVIVED although Step 6b-1 recorded it as killed by D3, the TSan plain-sample run. Cause (reproduced, then confirmed by the fix): my 3a-1 stress readers called `SARingTakeSnapshot` before every render, and its acquire-load of `w` gave ThreadSanitizer the producer-to-reader edge that M03 removes. The harness now takes one snapshot per call AFTER the render; M03 is killed again (TSan reports a data race). M21 and M22 still survive on the quiet re-run.
- **Orphaned processes:** the hang limit killed only the shell script a detector ran, not the harness binary: M11 and M11b left six stress processes (PIDs 79160, 82541, 85973, 89630, 93049, 96531; PPID 1; started inside the M11 window (3) and the M11b window (3), 10:26:56 to 10:47:15), each using about 125 to 160 % CPU, for 26 to 46 minutes before they were stopped. They were sent SIGTERM (by PID, never by name) between 11:13:12 and 11:14:13; all six exited, no SIGKILL. Load average before 32.56 / 28.54 / 25.18 with 0.0 % CPU idle; after 20.69 / 26.32 / 24.61 with 81.75 % idle (2.58 on the quiet re-run). The exact second of the successful kill call was not recorded; a first attempt at about 11:13:3x signalled nothing (a shell quoting error).
- **Re-run on the quiet machine:** the only mutant killed solely by threaded detectors that ran while the orphans were alive is **M27** (D2 paced, D2 lapping, D3 TSan): killed again by the same three, `stress.c:296 … fill N != w - cursor (fill must come from the w loaded in the snapshot)`. M01 to M03 are also threaded-only but ran at 10:25, before the orphans existed. M21 and M22 re-run with it: still survive.
- **The runner was fixed after the run:** a detector now runs in its own session and a timeout sends SIGTERM, then SIGKILL, to the whole process group; the demo on a hung dummy child left 1 orphan with the old runner and 0 with the fixed one (and 0 with the real fixed `run()`). The script version that ran the 97 mutants has sha256 `9588e999e02d210f20a567bcbca2d87f9b2738235a64c32acbe4b1599ba0e638`. The script also now logs each mutant to `results.jsonl` as it finishes, supports `--resume`, labels HANG apart from FAIL, and restores the source from a saved copy if a `--swift` run is killed while it is patched.

**Not run, not known (UNVERIFIED).** Everything on the real path: a real tap, a real-time engine, Bluetooth, a device; the recovery's thresholds (T, the bound N − T) are not tuned and T's default is a guess; whether a real engine ever produces the divergence these jumps repair (the real Bluetooth runs showed 100 % valid sample times and 0 phase mismatches); the two memory fences (M21, M22); sleep / wake and device change during a run; sound, latency, CPU. The re-prime (control thread), the thresholds that trigger it and the ring probe's recovery mode are Step 6b-3a-2, not started.

## Step 6b-3a-2 findings and verification record (2026-11-18): the control-thread re-prime and the ring probe's recovery mode — IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED
**Scope.** The control-thread half of the designed recovery ("Reading the ring from two blocks", the Re-prime paragraph; "Step 6b-3 plan", 6b-3a items 5 and 6): the decision logic, the orchestration, and a gain-0 `--ring-probe-recovery <seconds>` mode of the ring probe. Swift only. **No C file, no scan script, no `scripts/rt-block-allowlist.txt`, no `SASourceNode.m`, no `Package.swift`, no `CLAUDE.md` changed** (`git diff HEAD` over all of them was empty before the commit). **Output gain stayed 0; nothing was played; no real tap, no real-time engine, no Bluetooth or speaker output and no device was used by me; no sound, latency or CPU claim.** Everything below that touches a real engine is **IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED**; the real runs (Bluetooth 600 s, built-in speakers 120 s) are Sanjai's manual steps (docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP6B3A.md) and have **NOT** been done.

**Does the existing `SARingPrime` already do the design's re-prime? Yes, in effect; no C change was needed.** It re-bases both cursors to `w - depth` from one `w` load (equal cursors, fill `depth`), clears the counters, the frame-count table, the jump counters and the flags, sets T to the primed depth, refuses (changes nothing, never waits) with too few commits / a zero or oversized depth, and is specified for a running producer with the readers not running. So the design's "pause the producer's writes" step is **not needed and not done** (departure (a) in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md).

**What was built**
- `RePrimeController` (Core, pure): states idle, stopping, priming, waiting for T, restarting; no clock, engine or ring in it. Triggers (all PARAMETERS, UNTUNED): the ring's `needsReprime` flag, a `diverged` flag, 4 underrun blocks, 8 divergence events (divergence jumps + refused jumps) of the larger of the two readers, since the last prime. Limits: 5 s after the last restart (the start counts as the first restart), at most 6 re-primes per 60 s (suppressions are counted). Stop: the engine must report not running, then the readers' call counts must stay unchanged for 3 consecutive observations (the headers do not say `AVAudioEngine.stop` waits for a render block that is already running, so "not running" is not taken as proof; this heuristic is UNVERIFIED on a real engine). Restart only if both cursors are equal and both fills are at least T and T is non-zero. Timeouts end the run with a reason (5 s stop, 10 s waiting for T). The first prime and engine start of a run use the same states and are not counted as a re-prime.
- `RingRecoveryOrchestrator` (Core, behind the existing `RingProbeBackend` protocol and an injectable clock, so it is testable with a model; **placed in Core rather than SpatialAudioEngine on purpose**: it holds no engine or hardware code, the same seam as `RingProbeRunner`). It drives the controller, absorbs each epoch into cumulative counters before a prime zeroes them (counters, tables and flags are summed across epochs; cursor and fill are gauges), refuses a restart unless the output gain reads back as exactly 0, ignores a tick that arrives while it is driving (reentrancy guard), and has a step cap (a count, not a time) against a controller that cannot finish.
- Engine side: `RingProbeEngine.pause()` (stops the engine; nodes stay attached and the configuration-change observer stays), `RingSnapshotMapper` maps the 6b-3a-1 snapshot fields (frame-count table, jump counters, flags, T), and both backends gain `isEngineRunning`, `pauseEngine()` and `prime(callbacks:)`. `SystemRingBackend`'s three new members are compiled but cannot be exercised without a device.
- Probe: `--ring-probe-recovery <s>` / `--ring-probe-recovery-simulate <s>` (still refuse any gain or volume option; seconds 1 to 600), the report's section 8 (re-primes performed, per re-prime: reason, time triggered, time stopped, fill at restart, underruns before and after, prime attempts; per reader the frame-count table, jumps, flags; T), report file stem `ringrecovery-...`, and the plain mode's assumption text corrected (it said "no re-prime and no cursor jump exist yet").

**Where I made a choice (for review)**
- Triggers are seen at the probe's 1 Hz sampling, so up to about a second late.
- The start counts as the first restart for the 5 s minimum interval (a trigger within 5 s of the start is suppressed and counted).
- In the recovery mode the FILL series jumps at every re-prime by design, so its slope over the run is not a drift measurement (the report says so); the counters in sections 2 to 4 are made cumulative.
- With the guard's `return` removed the state machine is re-entrant-safe anyway (mutant E06 below): the reentrancy guard is defence in depth.
- Thresholds, T, the limits and the quiet-poll count are untuned first guesses.

**Verification (this Mac, 2026-11-18)**
- `swift build` clean. `swift test`: **776 tests passed (Core 606, Tracking 44, Engine 126)**; was 721 at 3a-1 (Core 557, Engine 120): +49 Core, +6 Engine. **The same under `swift test --sanitize=thread`: 776 passed, no ThreadSanitizer report.** As at 3a-1 this proves little about the ring itself (there is no threaded ring test in the Swift suite; the Engine suite has one threaded SIMULATED recovery run whose verdict is structural, never a time).
- New tests: controller (22 tests: thresholds exact at the boundary for each reader, the sequence with every record field, quiet polls, timeouts at the exact boundary, fill / cursor conditions, the start path, minimum interval and storm limit with exactly representable times, config validation), orchestrator against a model of the ring on a virtual clock (drain, stop, prime, restart, serving resumed; a render block still in flight; refused primes; a stuck engine; a frozen clock ended by the step cap; reentrancy; the gain guard incl. nil and NaN; failed restart; a producer that never delivers cannot cause a storm; cumulative counters), runner integration, JSON round trip (no names or paths; an old report without the new key still decodes), arguments, and Engine tests on the REAL C ring behind an offline engine (SARingPrime leaves equal cursors and fill >= T and sets T; the mapper reads the right reader's table and flags; pause and restart; a SIMULATED drain on a virtual clock repaired by the re-prime with serving resumed and no new underrun, T = 6 callbacks so a re-prime that ignores the configured depth is visible; a threaded SIMULATED run).
- C harness (`scripts/check-systemaudioring.sh`): **8,949 checks, 0 failures**; stress paced, lapping and cycles clean (as at 3a-1; no C changed).

**Survivors, hangs and the equivalent mutant, explained**
- **E06 (equivalent, survived).** Removing only the `return` of the reentrancy guard lets the nested tick run. I instrumented the mutant by hand: the nested ticks do run (outer poll 1, then T1, poll 2, T2, poll 3, T3, poll 4, T4 sees the third unchanged observation, primes and restarts), but the chain consumes exactly the same four polls (0.2 s on the virtual clock), the same seven ring snapshots and ends in the same state as the un-nested sequence: the state machine is re-entrant-safe by construction, so no observable result differs. The test asserts the exact stopped time, the exact snapshot count and that the refusal counter counts; **E06b** (guard and counter removed) is killed by the counter assertion. What would kill E06: a change that makes nesting harmful (for example a second source of ticks on another thread) plus a test for it; there is none today.
- **C08b (HANG in the full filter, clean FAIL on the controller suites).** A trigger accepted while the controller is stopping makes the controller re-trigger on every observation, so the orchestrator's drive loop (which only a timeout or the 100,000-step cap ends) does not end within 150 s. A hang is not a kill. Run against the controller suites only (`RePrimeTriggerTests|RePrimeSequenceTests|RePrimeStartPathTests|RePrimeLimitTests|RePrimeConfigTests`, which step the controller directly): **KILLED, exit 1, 3 s, at least 10 tests fail** (the script keeps the first 10 names) (e.g. `aTriggerWhileASequenceIsRunningCreatesNoSecondRePrime`, `theEngineIsNeverPrimedWhileItStillReportsRunning`). What would also kill it cleanly in the full filter: a fake backend that returns no snapshot after N calls, so the orchestrator gives up instead of looping.
- **Q08 (HANG, by design).** With the step cap removed, the frozen-clock test (time never advances, so no timeout of the controller can fire) loops for ever: the cap is exactly what that test exists for, and a hang is the only way it shows. Same remedy as C08b (a bounded fake) would make it a clean fail.
- **R01b (killed by a crash, not an assertion).** Reading the frame-count calls with the wrong stride makes the Engine test process die (`Fatal error: load from misaligned raw pointer`). The detection is real but it is a crash; no assertion names the cause.
- **Before and after (the four the follow-up asked for).** C11b: survived (the boundary test used 0.05 s steps, so `(r1 + 60) - r1` is not exactly 60.0) then killed after the test used exactly representable times (2 tests). E06: survived, then proven equivalent as above (test tightened; E06b killed). E10a: did not compile (the mutated line used `stateEnteredAt` inside the `setCurrent` closure), then rewritten (the wrong instant is read into a local first) and killed (5 tests). C08b: HANG in the full filter, then a clean FAIL on the controller suites only.
- **Orphan processes (stated, not hidden).** The first full run left one orphan `swiftpm-testing-helper` (PID 98602, 100 % CPU, PPID 1, started 12:17:38 in the Q08 hang window); the second left one (PID 34587, started 12:44:58, again in the Q08 window), **even though the runner then used a process group**: the helper puts itself in its own group. Both were stopped by explicit PID with SIGTERM (98602 at 12:31:39, 34587 at 12:51:06; both exited, no SIGKILL; load average 3.85 before and after the first, 4.60 to 4.39 around the second). The runner now collects the whole descendant tree before killing it; the two HANG mutants were re-run with it: no process left (list empty after both). The unrelated `SpatialAudio.app` process seen at 12:31 (PID 11592, started 12:29:28) is not mine and was not touched.

**What ran, what did not.** Ran: build, the full test suite (plain and TSan), the C harness, all scans and their broken-variant cases, 94 mutants. Did NOT run: anything on a real tap, a real-time engine, Bluetooth, the built-in speakers or any device; a real `AVAudioEngine` stop and start on a real output (how it behaves is **UNKNOWN**: whether it posts a configuration change, how long it is stopped, whether the original audio glitches); the quiet-poll heuristic and every threshold on real data; the permission prompt of a rebuilt bundle; sleep and wake; any listening; any latency or CPU measurement. The one threaded run is SIMULATED (our own threads, an offline engine). **The 600 s Bluetooth run at gain 0 under `caffeinate` and the 120 s built-in-speakers run are Sanjai's manual steps and are not done.**

**Postponed.** Sanjai has no wired headphones (2026-11-18); the wired-jack rate check (60 s count-only probe at gain 0, wired headphones plugged in and selected) is postponed until he has some.

## Step 6b-3a-2 real runs (2026-11-18)

Real hardware, count-only, output gain 0 (read back 0.000 before start, after start and at the end in all three reports). Numbers below are copied from the reports in `docs/test-results/` (committed as e592be2). The reports carry no commit hash. **Which build ran is a file-timestamp inference made in the chat on 2026-11-19, NOT Sanjai's statement** (an earlier version of this line said it was): the bundle binary `build/SpatialAudio.app/Contents/MacOS/SpatialAudio` has mtime 2026-11-18 18:31:50 +0530, after bc32821 (13:01:39 +0530) and before the first run (23:11:49), and `git diff bc32821 8293d79 -- Sources Package.swift scripts` is empty. That shows the bundle is newer than bc32821 and that no code changed in the commits since; it does not prove which source tree the binary was built from. The pushed message of e592be2 has the same "built after bc32821 with no code change since" wording; it cannot be amended, so this line is the correction. The three runs are one Mac (`Mac16,12`, macOS 27.2 build 26B123); Created times in the reports are the run START times (IST).

| report | output | run | outcome | re-primes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ringrecovery-20261118-231617` | built-in speakers, 48 kHz | 262.7 s, stopped by hand (signal) | ok | 0 |
| `ringrecovery-20261118-232834` | Bluetooth, 44.1 kHz | 492.4 s of a requested 600 s | **interrupted**: output rate changed to 48 kHz, engine posted a configuration change | 7 |
| `ringrecovery-20261118-234455` | Bluetooth, 44.1 kHz | 600.0 s (timeout) | ok | 6 |

**The 600 s run (`-234455`).**
- 6 re-primes, all `ok`, all triggered by underruns, 1 prime attempt each; none suppressed by the minimum interval or the storm limit; no GAVE UP; 0 engine configuration changes; teardown clean.
- Engine stopped 0.067 to 0.109 s per re-prime (0.081, 0.067, 0.078, 0.109, 0.098, 0.100). Fill at restart 2,560 / 2,560 / 2,048 / 2,048 / 2,048 / 2,048 for both readers, i.e. at least T = 2,048 every time.
- Gaps since the previous restart: 208.0 (the first epoch), 71.9, 68.9, 73.9, 92.9, 69.9 s. Underrun blocks at each trigger: 11, 14, 16, 9, 5, 7 (62 in total, left; 61 right).
- Both readers: 51,622 calls, all asking for exactly 558 frames, 0 overflow calls (min = max = 558); valid timestamps 1.0000; phase mismatches 0, self-changes 0, weak-rule violations 0; lockstep max |calls L - R| = 0; overrun, torn, oversize and bad-buffer blocks 0; no overrun or divergence jump, none refused; output rate 44,100 Hz first and last with 0 changes and 0 default-device changes.
- The first epoch was long (208.0 s) and began with 13,266 frames in the ring at the first pull (not 2,048). The start sequence's stop-to-restart took 0.264 s here (0.029 s on the speakers). The engine start **seems** to have taken longer this time; the cause is not known and not claimed.

**The interrupted run (`-232834`).**
- 7 re-primes, all `ok`, before the output change; gaps 32.0 (first epoch), 59.9, 95.9, 60.9, 75.9, 80.9, 44.9 s; engine stopped 0.072 to 0.112 s; fill at restart 2,048 / 2,048 every time; underrun blocks at each trigger 17, 8, 6, 12, 13, 8, 6. No GAVE UP.
- The run ended at 492.4 s: the sample at 492.0 s shows the output nominal rate 48,000 Hz (it was 44,100 Hz at 491.0 s) and the default-output-device flag set; the engine posted one configuration-change notification; the probe stopped and did not restart (by design). **The cause of the output change at about 23:28:33 IST was NOT recorded** (the report records only that the rate and the default-output flag changed). It is unknown; no guess is made here. The fill at the end (25,748 for both readers, equal to the maximum) is the engine having stopped pulling while the producer kept writing (6,804 at 492.0 s, 25,748 at 492.4 s: about 18,900 frames in 0.39 s, about the producer's rate).
- **First real-device appearance of two of the 3a-1 render-side rules, one event each:** left reader, 1 overrun jump of 24,530 frames; right reader, 1 divergence jump of 24,530 frames (0 refused). The right reader also counts 1 phase mismatch (the left 0) and the lockstep columns show max |calls L - R| 1 and max |frames requested L - R| 558 (a snapshot is not an atomic cut).
- **When did the jumps happen? Sanjai's message put them at the end, at the output change. The report does not support that.** The report does not timestamp the jumps themselves, but its per-sample counters already read left `overrunBlocks` 1 and right `phaseMismatches` 1 at sample 0 (calls = 1, elapsed 0 s) and never change in the 493 samples after it; the fill at sample 0 is 2,048 (the post-jump value). So by the report the two events were at the first calls of the run, **not** at the output change. My arithmetic, not a verified cause: 24,530 + 2,048 = 26,578 frames of fill before the jump is about 0.55 s of producer output at 48 kHz, and the start sequence's stop-to-restart took 0.549 s here (0.264 s with 13,266 frames in the 600 s run, 0.029 s with 3,584 in the speaker run): the ring fills while the engine takes its time to start, and a long enough start overruns the serve window. Whether that is what happened is NOT established. The first epoch of this run then lasted 32.0 s with 17 underrun blocks.
- Both readers: 42,305 calls at exactly 558 frames, 0 overflow calls; 70 / 69 underrun blocks (left / right), valid timestamps 1.0000. One event only, so no rate or statistics are claimed for the two rules.

**The speaker run (`-231617`).** 0 underrun blocks and 0 re-primes over 262.7 s; both readers 24,626 calls, all exactly 512 frames, 0 overflow calls; output 48,000 Hz, 0 rate changes (actual 48,000.000 to 48,000.526 Hz); phase mismatches 0, lockstep 0; fill 3,584 at the first pull, minimum 3,584, maximum 4,096. The start sequence: 1 prime attempt, stop-to-restart 0.029 s. Stopped by hand, so the 600 s was not reached.

**What Sanjai reported (his own report, not measured).** No audible glitch during the 600 s run. At gain 0 our engine's output cannot be heard, and the tap is unmuted, so what he listened to was the original audio playing as usual: this says the stop and restart of our engine did not audibly disturb that audio. It says nothing about how our renderer sounds. Nothing about sound is verified beyond that sentence.

**The manual's derived expectation was WRONG.** `docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP6B3A.md` said about 33 s between re-primes and 15 or more in 600 s. Observed: about 45 to 96 s between re-primes after the first epoch of each Bluetooth run, 6 re-primes in 600 s and 7 in 492 s. The manual's sentence is corrected there, with the original kept and marked.

**Per-epoch reading of the gaps (added 2026-11-19; replaces "the cause is not known"; ARITHMETIC and GUESS, from the two Bluetooth reports' JSON samples).**
- **The drain did not change [MEASURED, arithmetic on the samples].** Inside every full epoch the fill slope is -54.7 to -64.1 frames/s: the engine's requests exceed the producer's by about +1,040 to +1,425 ppm (558 asked per call at 86.1 calls/s, producer about 48,000 frames/s). The 62 frames/s of Step 6b-2 holds. (An earlier line in the 3a-2 manual said the 62 figure was "not what these runs show"; that was **wrong** and is corrected there.)
- **What the 33 s expectation got wrong [ARITHMETIC].** It assumed the ring restarts at T = 2,048 frames. The first sample taken after each restart shows **4,000 to 6,232 frames** (600 s run, epochs 1 to 5: 4,512, 4,512, 5,070, 6,232, 4,558; interrupted run, epochs 1 to 7: 4,000, 6,232, 4,000, 5,116, 5,116, 2,884, 5,070). With (fill - 558) / 62: 4,512 gives about 64 s, 5,070 about 73 s, 6,232 about 91 s, against observed gaps of 71.9, 73.9 and 92.9 s; 4,000 gives about 55 s against 59.9 and 60.9 s; 2,884 about 38 s against 44.9 s. The fit is within about 8 s (the gaps also include up to a second for the trigger and four underrun blocks). The first epochs fit too (208.0 s from 13,314 frames; 32.0 s from 2,096).
- **The cause of the excess over 2,048 [GUESS].** The producer keeps running while the engine is stopped and restarted: the measured stop of 0.067 to 0.109 s is about 3,200 to 5,200 frames of producer output at 48 kHz, the same order as the excess. The reports do not record the fill at the instant of the first pull after a restart, so this link is not measured.
- **How the numbers were made.** An inline script, not committed: per epoch, the samples from 3 s after the restart to 1 s before the next stop, the slope over the part before the first underrun, the run clock and the sample time taken as equal (these reports do not record the offset between them; `firstSampleRunClockSeconds`, added in Step 6b-3b, does), so the boundaries are good to about a second. `RingEpochSlopes` (6b-3b) computes the same kind of per-epoch slope for new reports; it has not been run on these two.

**The repair does not last.** Every epoch ended with 5 to 17 underrun blocks (600 s run: 11, 14, 16, 9, 5, 7; interrupted: 17, 8, 6, 12, 13, 8, 6); after each re-prime the readers starve again. The re-prime is a safety net; the 44.1 kHz rate mismatch is still there. Step 3b (fixing the rate mismatch) is the fix and is not started. The last epoch of each Bluetooth run (about 14 s and about 41 s) ended without an underrun, which says nothing about whether the next would have.

**Device check after the runs (Sanjai, `--list-audio-devices`; not re-run by me):** 0 aggregate devices, 0 probe devices, 0 taps, 6 devices. The reports' own after-teardown lists are read straight after the teardown (aggregate destruction is asynchronous per AudioHardware.h) and read 6 / 8 / 7 devices and 0 / 1 / 0 taps (600 s run, before / during / after), 6 / 6 / 5 (interrupted) and 4 / 6 / 5 (speakers): the device count before the run differs between runs (the speaker run had 4, the Bluetooth runs 6).

**Tags.** The control-thread re-prime (`RePrimeController`, `RingRecoveryOrchestrator`, the probe's recovery mode) stays IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED as a feature; the mechanism was **observed working on the real Bluetooth path in one 600 s run at gain 0, count-only** (6 of 6 re-primes ok, no configuration change, no give-up). Not verified, still UNKNOWN: sound (anything our engine would make audible, since the gain was 0), latency, CPU load, behaviour with the output rate changing mid-run (the interrupted run stops by design, it does not re-prime), the thresholds (untuned guesses), a second 600 s run, other Bluetooth devices, sleep and wake, and the render-side jump rules beyond the single event above. The earlier line in the 3a-2 record that the two runs "are not done" is superseded by this section.

**Noticed outside this step (reported, not fixed).** The probe's report has no field for why the default output changed, which is why the cause is unknown; it records only that it did. I did not check whether the system log has the cause.

## Step 6b-3b (a2) findings and verification record (2026-11-19): the tap bound to the output device's stream — IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED

**Scope, as approved by Sanjai (2026-11-19).** (a2) only: a device-bound tap mode behind `--ring-probe-device-tap`, the rate-equality guard, the pure verdict function with the criteria G0 to G3 "pre-registered GUESS, fixed before the run" (version `6b-3b-prereg-1`), and the per-epoch slope function. Swift and Core only: **`git diff` is empty for every C file, the scan scripts, `rt-block-allowlist.txt`, `SASourceNode.m` and `Package.swift`.** Gain stays 0 (read back, refused otherwise), the tap is unmuted, there is no sound option. Arm B ((e)) is built only if S0 shows that the tap does not arrive at the output rate. **Nothing here claims that the device-bound tap arrives at 44.1 kHz, or at any rate: S0 to S3 (docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP6B3B.md) measure it, and they are Sanjai's.**

**What was built** (commit `2d05119`): `SystemRingBackend(deviceTap:)` creates the tap with `CATapDescription initExcludingProcesses:andDeviceUID:withStream:` for the default output device's stream 0 (stream 0 is a GUESS), our own process excluded, unmuted; the device's UID is read for that one call and never stored or reported; the Swift spelling `CATapDescription(__excludingProcesses:andDeviceUID:withStream:)` was typechecked in a scratch file and compiles (never run against a device). `RateEqualityGuard` (device-bound mode only: the run stops cleanly before the ring exists, with the tap's format recorded, if the tap is not Float32 interleaved stereo at the output's nominal rate within 0.5 Hz; the global mode never had it). `RateFixEvaluator` / `RateFixCriteria` / `RateFixStage` (the stage is recognised from the mode and the requested seconds: S0 plain 5 s, S1 plain 180 s, S2 recovery 600 s, S3 plain 120 s; anything else is `unclassified` and gets no verdict; a SIMULATED report's verdict says so in its first words). `RingEpochSlopes` and `RingTimings.firstSampleRunClockSeconds`. Report section 9, `tapMode` and `rateFix` fields (older reports still decode), file stem `ringdevicetap-`.

**Verification, final tree.**
- `swift build` (debug and release) clean. `swift test`: **864 tests** = 690 Core + 44 Tracking + 130 Engine (was 776 = 606 + 44 + 126; 84 Core and 4 Engine tests are new), all pass; the same 864 pass under `swift test --sanitize=thread` with no report. **TSan is a regression run here, not evidence about the new code**: the new logic is pure and single-threaded and adds no shared state.
- Scans on the final tree (real exit codes): `scripts/scan-rt-block.sh` PASS (exit 0), `scripts/scan-rt-callback.sh` PASS, `scripts/scan-render-api-coverage.sh` 146 files, 0 violations, `scripts/scan-render-api-coverage-cases.sh` all cases as expected; `scripts/scan-rt-block-cases.sh`: cases 1 and 1v pass, cases 2 to 7, z and p1 to p4 fail as required (all 13 run once earlier in this step; 2, 5, 7, z and p4 re-run on the final tree). C harness (`scripts/check-systemaudioring.sh`): 8,949 checks, 0 failures (no C changed). (My first scan pass in this step used a wrong exit-code capture and ran `scan-rt-block-cases.sh` without a case argument: exit 2 = usage; both were redone.)
- Not run: anything on a real tap, a device-bound tap, a real engine, Bluetooth or speakers; the permission prompt of a rebuilt bundle; any listening, latency or CPU measurement. The simulated runs (virtual-time and one real-time 1 s run on our own threads) rehearse the flow and decide nothing.

**Mutation record (test-the-test).** `scripts/mutate_ratefix.py`, filter `RateEquality|RateFix|RingEpoch|RingProbe|SimulatedRingProbe|RingRecovery|RePrime`.
- **Provenance.** The original list (95 ids, A to H, R, P, Q, M) was written to a timestamped scratch file at **2026-11-19 00:35:24 +0530, before any 6b-3b code existed** (HEAD 8293d79, nothing changed under Sources, Tests, scripts, docs/*.md, Package.swift), sha256 `e9cc88966cc6230afd1ce2f006a83bb208ac9b4322d22b1c8aad4a1594365c4a`; that file was **not edited afterwards** and still has that hash. Every id in the table is **original**. One addition, in a separate file (`ring-ratefix-mutations-appended.md`, sha256 `43e3963cec793b617ec2f2eb4158b09cd01b873dc9df4acff8bd7ae74acb6379`): a direct test of `judgeSlope`'s S3 contract, **appended-after-results** (after run 1, below). Not run as their own mutants: **B05** (the same patch as B02) and **M03** (the report's Codable conformance is synthesized: no code to mutate). Some patches are narrower than their list text (H05 is "the minimum is 2, not 3"; H08 bounds the first epoch below by the first restart).
- **Run 1 (partial, 69 of 93; kept for the record).** 68 killed, **E08 survived**; then my copy of the runner's plumbing crashed on a `UnicodeDecodeError` reading test output (the sources were restored, hashes equal to the pre-run ones). E08 (the S3 line of `judgeSlope` removed) is **redundant at the report level**: `evaluate` replaces the S3 G2 row with "not part of S3" and uses the slope only in the S2 branch, so the line changes nothing a report shows. The function's own contract was untested: test `theSlopeJudgementItselfIsNotEvaluatedInS3EvenForAHugeSlopeButIsJudgedInS2` was added (appended-after-results) and the runner now reads output with `errors="replace"`.
- **Run 2, in two parts, one script (sha256 `26789aaae75526c3a18b44f65ad9b8206ae649a79859b4e39e25bbb485c31f56`).** Part a: 55 mutants (A01 to G08), 2026-11-19 01:00:32 to 01:07:18, all killed, no gap inside it (the per-mutant times sum to 406 s, the wall time was 406 s). **The run then died during G09:** the power log shows `Entering Sleep state due to 'Clamshell Sleep'` at 01:07:29 (lid closed, on battery); the process was gone afterwards, for a reason I did not determine (the session that started it ended too). **Its restore step never ran, so the tree held the G09 mutant in `RateFixVerdict.swift` for about six hours**; nothing was built or run in that window. Found by the read-only status check, restored by undoing exactly that one line and verified against the pre-run hash (all eight files equal), then the tests were re-run green. Part b: the remaining 38 (G09 to M02), 07:05:09, same script, no sleep. **Combined: 93 run, 92 killed, 1 survived (Q05), 0 HANG, 0 DID-NOT-COMPILE, 0 PATCH-ERROR; the eight files are unchanged by the run (hashes before and after equal); no stray process (checked by PID listing).**
- **Q05 (survived, explained).** The device-bound tap created without excluding our own process (`__excludingProcesses: []`). The line is in `SystemRingBackend.createTap`'s device-bound branch, which needs a real default output device and a real Core Audio call: **no test can reach it**, so the survival is a hole by construction, not an equivalent mutant. **Correction (2026-11-19, to my own sentence here, which said S0 could kill it): it cannot.** The reports show only the process object our code passed to the tap description; the probe never reads the exclusion back from Core Audio and never looks at the tapped samples, so nothing in S0 to S3 can observe whether the exclusion was honoured (our process is silent at gain 0, so a feedback loop could not appear either way). The test that could kill it is the one ARCHITECTURE "4. Feedback" already plans: play a tone through our OWN engine while the tap runs and read the tap's level, which needs a level reading on the tap side and an engine gain above 0, so it cannot happen before an audible step. Q05 stays **not verified**.
- Lesson (not fixed here): the runner is not sleep-proof and a killed run leaves a mutated tree. A "restore from a backup at start" step in the runner would have prevented the six hours; outside this step's scope, reported.

**What ran, what did not.** Ran: build, the full suite (plain and TSan), the scans and the C harness, 93 mutants, simulated flows. Did **not** run: anything on hardware; a real device-bound tap (its format, rate, cadence, whether creation needs a sub-device or asks for a permission); stream index 0 against a real device; S0 to S3 (docs/MANUAL_TEST_STEP6B3B.md; they are Sanjai's, and the criteria are pre-registered there and in code before any of them).

**Open requirements recorded** (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, "6b-3b (a2) ..."): a device-bound tap must be recreated when the output device changes; the start-up fill becomes standing latency once the drain is gone; the aggregate's cadence for a device-bound tap is not stated; the product graph's environment node at 44.1 kHz is not stated.

**Noticed outside this step (reported, not fixed).** (1) The mutation runner's lack of a restore-from-backup step (above). (2) `docs/STATUS.md` line for the 3a-2 verification still says the two real runs "are not done"; the 2026-11-18 real-runs section supersedes it, as that section says. (3) The 3a-2 manual's first correction contained a wrong sentence (62 f/s "not what these runs show"); corrected in f55df39.

## Step 6b-3b (a2) real runs (2026-11-19)

Real hardware, count-only, output gain 0 (read back 0.000 before the start, after the start and at the end in all five reports). The reports are in `docs/test-results/` (committed as 21968fd); numbers below were read from the files on disk. **Build:** the reports carry no commit hash. The instruction for this record named 154662c; the file timestamps agree (the bundle binary `build/SpatialAudio.app/Contents/MacOS/SpatialAudio` has mtime 2026-11-19 07:17:57 +0530, after 154662c at 07:14:41 and before the first run at 07:18:24, and `git diff 154662c HEAD -- Sources Package.swift scripts` is empty); that is an inference, not proof of which source tree the binary was built from. One Mac (`Mac16,12`, macOS 27.2 build 26B123). The file stem is the run's END time; "start" below is the report's Created time (IST). The pre-registered criteria (version `6b-3b-prereg-1`) were **not changed** after the runs.

| file (`ringdevicetap-20261119-...`) | start | run | output | tap format and rate | stage, verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `071831` | 07:18:24 | 5 s | **built-in speakers** (transport `bltn`), 48,000 Hz | Float32 interleaved stereo, 48,000 Hz | S0, `proceedToS1` |
| `071940` | 07:19:34 | 5 s | Bluetooth (`blue`), 44,100 Hz | Float32 interleaved stereo, **44,100 Hz** | S0, `proceedToS1` |
| `072259` | 07:19:58 | 180 s | Bluetooth, 44,100 Hz | 44,100 Hz | S1, `continueToS2` |
| `073339` | 07:23:39 | 600 s (recovery mode) | Bluetooth, 44,100 Hz | 44,100 Hz | S2, **`go`** |
| `075158` | 07:49:58 | 120 s | built-in speakers, 48,000 Hz | 48,000 Hz | S3, `controlOK` |

**The first S0 report (`071831`) is the BUILT-IN SPEAKERS** (transport `bltn`, 48,000 Hz), a control, although its `/tmp` file name said `bt` (the report itself carries no such name; the transport type is in it). At a 48 kHz output the report says it "cannot tell the device-bound tap from the global one"; it is not a test of the hypothesis. All five: outcome `ok`, stopped by timeout, no flags, 0 configuration changes, teardown clean; tap lists 0 / 1 / 0 (before / during / after); device lists 6 / 8 / 7 in the Bluetooth runs and 4 / 6 / 5 in the speaker runs, i.e. one device more after the teardown than before, read right after it (the aggregate's destruction is asynchronous per AudioHardware.h; the same pattern as the 2026-11-18 runs), not examined further; no separate post-run `--list-audio-devices` was reported for these runs.

**S0 on Bluetooth (`071940`): the measurement the spike was for.** The device-bound tap reported Float32 interleaved stereo at **44,100 Hz**, equal to the output device's nominal 44,100 Hz (G0 pass, the rate-equality guard passed). Every earlier GLOBAL tap, 27 reports on both outputs, was 48,000 Hz [MEASURED, 2026-11-07 to 2026-11-18]. In this setup the device-bound tap arrives at the output device's rate. The 5 s run's own fill slope (+1,658 ppm: one 512-frame step, 3,072 to 3,584) is not resolvable (the report prints the endpoint resolution as about +/- 4,644 ppm) and S0 judges only G0.

**The 600 s Bluetooth run (`073339`, S2, recovery mode) in numbers:**
- Tap Float32 interleaved stereo **44,100 Hz**; output Bluetooth 44,100 Hz nominal, 44,100.000 Hz actual (44,100.021 Hz at every sample), buffer 512 frames; source nodes mono Float32 at 44,100 Hz, main mixer and output node at 44,100 Hz.
- **Every call asked for exactly 512 frames**: both readers 51,681 calls, frame-count table `512 x 51681`, 0 overflow calls, min = max = 512, per-interval means 512.00 / 512.00 / 512.00 (min / median / max) in every one of the 600 intervals.
- **Fill slope -0.004 frames/s = -0.1 ppm of the tap rate** (both readers); per-minute slopes -15.5, -13.5, -7.7, -7.7, -0.0, 0.0, 3.9, 7.7, 9.7, 15.5 ppm (spread 31.0 ppm). The fill took only two values, 3,584 (361 of the 601 samples) and 4,096 (240), a difference of one 512-frame step, which is the snapshot resolution the report states (about one call).
- **Underruns 0 / 0, overrun blocks 0, jumps 0 (overrun, divergence, refused), re-primes 0** (suppressed 0 / 0, no GAVE UP), torn, oversize and bad-buffer blocks 0, silent frames 0; no `diverged` or `needsReprime` flag.
- **Valid timestamps 1.0000** for both readers (51,681 of 51,681 calls), phase mismatches 0 / 0, phase self-changes 0 / 0, weak-rule violations 0 / 0, **lockstep 0** (max |calls L - R| 0, max |frames requested L - R| 0).
- Producer 44,100.23 frames per host second (+5.2 ppm against the declared rate; endpoint resolution about +/- 38.7 ppm), 86.13 commits per second of 512 frames, 0 rejected commits. Output rate: 0 changes, 0 default-device changes.
- **Per-epoch row:** one epoch (no re-prime), 601 samples, 0.0 to 600.0 s, slope -0.00 frames/s = -0.1 ppm for both readers, fill 3,584 first and last, 0 underruns.
- **Standing fill: 3,584 to 4,096 frames. ARITHMETIC: about 81 to 93 ms at 44.1 kHz (3,584 / 44,100 = 81.3 ms; 4,096 / 44,100 = 92.9 ms). This is a ring fill, NOT an end-to-end latency** (it leaves out the engine's and the Bluetooth device's own delays and the tap's, none of which is measured). The fill at the first pull was 3,584 (engine start to first pull 0.041 s); the start sequence's engine start completed at 0.150 s on the run clock after 7 prime attempts.
- Criteria G0 to G3e all `pass`; verdict `go` under the pre-registered criteria. `S1` (`072259`, 180 s): G0, G1, G2 (-5.94 ppm) and G3c pass, 15,505 calls per reader, all 512 frames, fill 3,584 to 3,072, 0 underruns; `S3` (`075158`, 120 s, speakers): G0, G1, G3b to G3e pass, 11,251 calls per reader, all 512, 0 underruns, valid 1.0000, lockstep 0; the slope (+27.7 ppm over 120 s) is not judged in S3 (one 512-frame step is about +/- 89 ppm over 120 s at 48 kHz [ARITHMETIC]).

**Before and after, Bluetooth output at 44,100 Hz, 600 s, recovery mode, the same probe** (global tap: `ringrecovery-20261118-234455`, 2026-11-18; device-bound tap: `ringdevicetap-20261119-073339`). The reports do not record the device, so whether it is the same Bluetooth device is not shown.

| | global tap (2026-11-18) | device-bound tap (2026-11-19) |
|---|---|---|
| tap rate | 48,000 Hz | **44,100 Hz** |
| frames per call, both readers | **558** every call (51,622 calls) | **512** every call (51,681 calls) |
| fill slope | -54.7 to -64.1 frames/s inside the full epochs (about +1,040 to +1,430 ppm of requests against the producer; per-epoch reading above) | **-0.004 frames/s = -0.1 ppm** over the whole run |
| underrun blocks | 62 left / 61 right | **0 / 0** |
| re-primes | 6 (gaps 68.9 to 208.0 s) | **0** |
| jumps (overrun, divergence, refused) | 0 | 0 |
| valid timestamps | 1.0000 | 1.0000 |
| phase mismatches / lockstep | 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 |
| fill at the first pull | 13,266 frames | 3,584 frames |
| engine start completed (start sequence, run clock) | 0.337 s | 0.150 s |
| producer against the host clock | -0.4 ppm | +5.2 ppm |

The start-up numbers are lower in all five new runs (first-pull fills of 3,072 to 3,584 frames; the 600 s Bluetooth global-tap run of 2026-11-18 had 13,266, and the interrupted one a 24,530-frame start-up jump): one engine start per run, so this says nothing about why.

**Tags.** The rate fix is **observed working in one 600 s run on one Bluetooth device, count-only, gain 0** (S1 agrees for 180 s on the same output; S0 and S3 are the controls). **UNVERIFIED, not measured by these runs:** the tap's CONTENT (the probe counts frames; it does not compare samples with the original audio), sound, end-to-end latency, CPU, sleep and wake, other Bluetooth devices, a device or rate change while running (the device-bound tap must be recreated then: docs/ARCHITECTURE.md), the HRTF / environment-node graph, and whether a device-bound tap changes what a listener hears. **Own-process exclusion (Q05) stays "not verified":** every report's line "Own audio process object before the tap / after the engine started: N / N; excluded: N" shows the object that the code passed to the tap description; whether Core Audio honoured the exclusion is not shown (our process is silent at gain 0, so a feedback loop could not appear either way).

**What Sanjai reported for these runs: nothing.** No notes were provided: **no audible observation, no permission-prompt text, no list of settings touched was reported.** None is guessed here; no claim is made about whether the original audio was disturbed, or whether a prompt appeared. A later docs commit will add notes if he sends them.

## Step 6b-3c plan (approved by Sanjai 2026-11-19) — PLANNED; nothing in this section is implemented or has been run

**Goal.** The first audible stereo path from system audio: ring -> two mono source nodes (left and right virtual speakers) -> HRTF environment node -> headphones; then head tracking joined through the existing tracked-listening session (yaw and pitch, roll 0). Stages: **3c-0** (nothing audible from our engine: offline calibration, a process-specific tap mode, a synthetic test file, mute-failure experiments at output gain 0), **3c-1a** (audible, static listener), **3c-1b** (the same with the original muted), **3c-2** (head-tracked). **Every audible stage needs Sanjai's separate explicit go.** Stereo as received: no Dolby Atmos or DTS:X decoding is claimed, no upmix happens in 3c (a later bed would be labelled upmix), no DRM workaround (protected audio may be silent; test with non-protected material). Nothing audible from our engine is built or run in 3c-0.

**The one change to the plan as first presented: the tap is PROCESS-SPECIFIC.** The first audible run and the mute experiments use a tap on the **test player only** (a tap bound to the output device's stream that includes only the chosen process; muted, if at all, for that process only), not a global or device-wide mute. Why: (1) an unmuted tap cannot separate our render from the original, so E2 (channel correctness) and E3 (no artifacts) cannot be judged while the original also plays: the original is in the headphones at the same time, about 100 ms earlier [GUESS, lower bound from the standing fill of 81 to 93 ms, ARITHMETIC]; (2) a global mute (the device-bound tap that excludes only our process) would silence EVERY other app's audio on that stream, system sounds and calls included [SDK-READ wording of `CATapMuteBehavior`; consequences GUESS], and the headers say nothing about what happens to muted audio if our process dies. **The unmuted global (device-bound, all-but-us) tap stays only as a fallback smoke test:** comfort, the stop paths and the machine criteria; **E2 and E3 would not count from such a run.**

**The 11 decisions, approved as recommended (with the change above):**
1. Staging 3c-0 -> 3c-1a -> 3c-1b -> 3c-2, a separate go for every audible stage.
2. Muting: the mute-failure experiments (at gain 0, a process-specific tap, the test player only) come BEFORE any muted listening; a muted run happens only if they pass the pre-registered criteria; if the audio does not come back in any scenario, muting is not shipped.
3. Gain (all GUESS until the offline calibration is read): output gain 0 until the engine runs, the ring is primed and the producer is committing, plus 1 s; a control-thread ramp (`GainRamp`) over 5 s at a 100 Hz step rate to a first target of 0.03 (-30.5 dB); notches 0.06, 0.10, 0.15 (-24, -20, -16 dB); a hard cap (`GainCap`, non-finite becomes 0) proposed at 0.15 for 3c-1, against the existing 0.40 of `TrackedListeningPolicy` (untuned, no limiter). **The 3c-0 calibration PROPOSES a cap that keeps the rendered peak at or below -16 dB relative to a full-scale input; the proposal is not applied without Sanjai.**
4. The interface is a HUD card (window) with Stop, Escape and Cmd+Q (`applicationWillTerminate`), plus SIGINT/SIGTERM from a terminal; not a headless CLI. Each stop path: gain 0, pump stopped, engine stopped, aggregate and tap torn down (which restores a muted original).
5. No sample-level protection in 3c-1a: no tap-block monitor (100 to 400 ms buffers, thread unstated [SDK-READ]) and no limiter claim. If an `AUPeakLimiter` node is ever added (parameters attack 1 to 30 ms, decay 1 to 60 ms, pre-gain +/-40 dB, no ceiling parameter [SDK-READ]) it needs an offline transparency check first. Protection is the small gain, the cap and the system volume; control-thread conditions (engine configuration change, default-output change, sleep, underruns / overruns / divergence, a stalled producer) fade out and stop, with no auto-restart.
6. Test material: a **generated synthetic channel-check file** (left-only bursts, right-only, both; a known low level; no copyrighted and no captured audio), long enough (15 minutes or more) for ONE `afplay` process so its PID does not change; later, non-protected music.
7. Music only for 3c; video lip-sync is a later question for a loopback measurement. ITU-R BT.1359 detectability is about +45 ms (sound early) to -125 ms (sound late), acceptability about +90 to -185 ms [WEB: search-result excerpts of 2026-11-19; the Recommendation's PDF was not opened; sign conventions differ between sources]. The measured standing fill is 3,584 to 4,096 frames = 81 to 93 ms at 44.1 kHz [MEASURED, ARITHMETIC]; it is not an end-to-end latency.
8. A device change stops listening (a fast fade, the documented teardown order, a visible notice) and needs a manual Start; no auto-recreate of the device-bound tap in 3c.
9. The first code step is the offline calibration (no hardware, no sound).
10. The first-run output is the Bluetooth setup that S2 measured (no wired headphones are available).
11. Reducing the prime depth T is a separate follow-up (6b-3d): a gain-0 sweep at T = 3 and 2 callbacks for 600 s each, then under CPU load; T does not change in 3c.

**Listening criteria (GUESS, pre-registered before the first audible run; machine items go into a versioned evaluator like `RateFixCriteria`).** Machine: 0 underruns, 0 overrun blocks or jumps, 0 re-primes, 0 configuration changes; the gain trace never above the cap; the time from a stop request to a gain read-back of 0 at most 100 ms. Ears (Sanjai, pass / fail, any FAIL is a no-go for the stage): E1 comfort at every step with no startle; E2 left-only bursts from the left, right-only from the right, both near the centre; E3 no click, pop, dropout or zipper in 10 minutes including the ramp and the stop; E4 Stop, Escape and Cmd+Q each silence the render within about a second; E5 (3c-1b) the original is not heard; E6 informational (externalization, tonal balance, echo). 3c-2 adds yaw direction with music, a 5 s camera-cover test, pitch (a weak cue with a non-individual HRTF [GUESS]; the card's pose readout is the objective side), Recenter, lag as a rating only. **Known unknowns:** pitch by ear, Recenter, Cmd+Q while running, a comfortable level for music, other headphones, the camera's 16.6 fps interpolation steps, tilt, glasses and lighting, what a muted process-specific tap does if our app dies, the HRTF level and the offline -3 dB at 44.1 kHz on a device, zipper noise on volume steps, a Bluetooth start-up pop, sleep and wake.

**Hardware-only (cannot be unit-tested):** the ears; what the system does with a muted tap and how it recovers; device-change timing; Cmd+Q and signals in a real process; the tap-block thread; any limiter's effect; a Bluetooth pop; sleep and wake; lip-sync; actual sound pressure. **Mutation targets** (to be written before any code, per step): ramp direction and step, a ramp that never reaches its target, cap off-by-one, non-finite passing the cap, a fade-out that does not reach 0, monitor thresholds and missing triggers, an auto-restart that must be absent, gain above 0 before the engine runs, start and teardown order, a tap not destroyed on an error path, muted as the default, `MutedWhenTapped` mapped to `Muted`, azimuth sign swapped, reader 0 and 1 swapped, distance units, the evaluator's thresholds. **No new render-thread code and no new block function:** the existing source-node block (one allow-listed call) is the only render code; the scans re-run and `git diff` of the C files, the scans and the allow-list stays empty.

**3c-0 scope as approved (this step):** (A) this record; (B) a header check for a process-specific device-bound tap initializer before any code; (C) the offline calibration; (D) a process-specific tap mode in the probe (a flag naming the target PID, an explicit mute option whose default is unmuted, our process absent from the tap, gain still forced to 0, the 6b-3b guard and verdict apply); (E) the synthetic test file; (F) the mute-experiment criteria, pre-registered before the code (scenarios: normal exit, SIGINT, SIGKILL, the tap destroyed while IO runs, IO stopped while the tap lives; the test player's audio returns within 5 s, GUESS). Sleep and wake are not in this step.

## 7.1.4 angle provenance (checked 2026-11-04)
Source: Dolby Atmos Home Theater Installation Guidelines (PDF text extracted locally; figures not readable).
- Verified in text: L/R ±30°; rear surround ≈135°; overhead elevation 45° reference (30–55° allowed); front heights ≈±30° azimuth; ceiling-mounted rear heights ≈135° azimuth.
- NOT verified: side surround ±90°. The text does not state it (figure only). 90° is an assumption; common practice is 90–110°.
- Not checked: ITU-R BS.2051 nominal positions for this layout (the search did not surface the table). Wall-mounted height variants differ from the ceiling case used here.
